At that moment, he wasn't that far from the gray fog which was hardened in midair. He seemed to just need one more step to reach that height.

Klein subconsciously looked up and seemed to see something.

Chapter 666: Afternoon Town

That's... Klein's eyes reflected a nearly transparent object.

It resembled some creature's carapace, occasionally contracting and vanishing from Klein's sight, then peeking out under the influence of an invisible wind and revealing part of its outline.

Using it as an anchor and looking farther upward, he saw patches of green so dark they were nearly black, standing there in silence.

They look like the colors of the trees of a dark forest... Klein mumbled without imagining what the object of those colors signified. All he could do was boldly guess that it had something to do with gaining a deeper control of the mysterious space above the gray fog.

Without attempting anything destined to be futile, he jumped down from the staircase that seemed to lead to heaven and passed back into the palace.

With the existence of Queen Mystic in mind, Klein did some simple tidying up before leaving the gray fog and returning to his bathroom.

After tying up the loose ends, he walked to his suitcase, took out the Sun Brooch, and wore it on his double-breasted frock coat.

After all the turmoil he had endured, the mystical items available to him had returned to the same state as when he was in Backlund. However, he was now a Sequence 5 and possessed one of the most troublesome powers below the demigod level. In the truest sense of the word, he was now a powerhouse in the Beyonder world.

I should be thrilled and elated, but I'm not. I'm not even as excited as I was when I found the mermaids... That's because I've merely taken another step along the road to revenge. The goal I truly wish to achieve remains far away...

Next, I should summarize the acting principles, digest the Marionettist potion, and seek the corresponding Sequence 4 formula and ingredients. Yes, I can only do all this after leaving these waters. I'll ask Mr. Azik, Will Auceptin, and Arrodes for advice...

Heh heh, I should relax for the next few days. Remaining this tense might cause me to break down and exhibit signs of losing control... Klein turned toward the full-length mirror in his room. The man reflected there stood 1.8 meters tall, with black hair, brown eyes, a lean face, and sharply defined features. He wore a white shirt, a suit and bow tie, a top hat, and a pale gold Sunbird brooch. His expression was calm, his gaze deep and dark.

After looking at this in silence, he raised his hands and adjusted the buttons at his cuffs and patted his black suit.

Lightning streaked across the sky, illuminating the stacked, dark buildings ahead.

Demon Hunter Colin Iliad, with two swords on his back, pointed ahead and said, "That's Afternoon Town."

He combed his grizzled hair that was flying about in the wind that swept through the wilderness.

That's fast... With his Axe of Hurricane in hand, Derrick sighed wistfully in surprise.

He soon realized that this was reasonable. After all, the Giant King's Court was located somewhere near the City of Silver, and Afternoon Town was the junction connecting the two.

In the lightning that illuminated the night sky, he clearly saw Afternoon Town in its entirety. Built at the foot of a mountain, it was naturally divided into upper and lower levels. Despite being called a town, it wasn't much smaller than most of the ruined cities discovered by the City of Silver.

Here, gray stone had been stacked into buildings of varying shapes. Some were hollow structures nearly ten meters tall. Others resembled Derrick's current residence and were so low that an ordinary person might strike their head on the ceiling.

The buildings were densely packed near the center and spread out toward the edges. Some had collapsed, while others remained standing despite showing signs of age and wear.

It's completely different from what's described in the textbooks... Derrick suddenly recalled the knowledge he had learned from his history lessons.

According to the City of Silver's records, Afternoon Town was the gateway separating reality from myth, a place where humans and giants had lived together. Day and night had existed here, but most of the daytime remained fixed at "noon." Neither fog, storms, nor snow could blot out the intense sunlight. Yet now, the town was dark and oppressive. Even when illuminated, it held no sense of brightness and showed no sign of life.

Clenching his axe tightly, Derrick, whose eyes had two miniature suns, was on the flanks of the exploration team. He followed Chief Colin into Afternoon Town.

This area had been cleared once on the first exploration. The streets were covered with signs of rotting flesh and dry pus. It was completely silent.

"Be careful. Many strange monsters lurk in the darkness." The scar-faced Colin Iliad remained on guard. He drew one of his two swords as its silver light contracted.

This is the gateway to myth? When the Creator abandoned this land, were even the myths abandoned? Derrick couldn't help imagining what Afternoon Town had experienced during the Cataclysm. He instinctively felt that it had probably differed from the Kingdom of Silver.

Before he could carefully observe his surroundings in search of possible clues, he suddenly heard a teammate on the other flank anxiously cry out, "There's something!"

Derrick turned his head and saw a transparent face growing out of the wall of a ten-meter-tall stone building.

The face was filled with countless cracks. With great regularity, they spiraled around the center, forming a single eye or mouth that resembled a vortex.

The hurricane within the vortex took on physical form and spewed out with a howl. Dense, dawn-like radiance shot from within like arrows of light.

Bam! Bam! Bam!

They struck several members of the exploration team, but it was as though they had hit thick city walls. They produced only a rapid series of dull thuds before vanishing into the darkness.

At some point, the City of Silver's Chief, Colin Iliad, had dropped to one knee and thrust his silver sword into the rotting gray ground.

He had provided the front row with the strongest protection!

At the same time, the other members of the exploration team methodically unleashed their attacks. Hurricanes of light and scarlet fireballs struck the monster.

Immediately afterward, the holy light Derrick summoned struck the vortex resembling a single eye.

Amidst the explosive boom, the boulder, which was originally covered in cracks, collapsed. A transparent face screamed as it vaporized.

Although the battle had been simple, Derrick felt no joy. He had heard Mr. Hanged Man and Miss Justice discuss Beyonder battles. Comparing their accounts with the City of Silver's accumulated experience fighting all kinds of monsters from the darkness, he had reached a troubling realization: the City of Silver had access to only a limited number of pathways, and those below the demigod level lacked effective control abilities. The recent battle proved it.

Thankfully, the Sealed Artifacts formed when monsters die can compensate for this... Derrick thought as he heard the Chief order, "Proceed according to plan. Form groups of three or four. Search and clear the different areas."

"Yes, Chief!" Most members of the exploration team were highly experienced, and they quickly formed their groups.

Derrick's group consisted of three people. Besides him, there were Joshua and Haim, who had accompanied him to the abandoned temple. The former was a Sequence 7 Weapon Master with a mystical glove capable of controlling fire. The latter was a newly advanced Sequence 6 Dawn Paladin who stood 2.3 meters tall.

The City of Silver, which had Warriors--also known as the Giant pathway--as the main force, had an average height of 1.8 meters (including children above the age of 6). Even if the Beyonder characteristics were inherited, the normal genes that were modified had accumulated with each generation. Although Derrick wasn't old, he was nearly 1.8 meters tall and still had room to grow.

Following their assigned route, Derrick, Joshua, and Haim entered an alley on the left in a triangular battle formation. They inspected every building that could still be entered.

Perhaps as a result of the former sweep, they didn't encounter any monsters. They couldn't help but feel a little relieved.

"Rumor has it that the six-member council plans to set up camp here and make Afternoon Town a stronghold," Joshua, who was wearing a scarlet glove on his left palm, said after observing the buildings.

Haim nodded and looked down at his two partners.

"The true target seems to be..."

He pointed diagonally upward.

"The Giant King's Court?" Derrick asked in surprise.

Isn't it a search for the sea where Jack and company came from? Don't we need to circle the Giant King's Court? Derrick was filled with puzzlement.

Haim shook his head.

"I've only heard rumors."

He swept his gaze and pointed at the entrance to the underground chamber.

"Let's finish our search here."

Derrick answered tersely. With his night vision and ability to emit light, he wasn't afraid of the darkness as he stepped into the underground chamber first. Haim lifted an animal hide lantern while Joshua followed closely in tow.

The building's underground chamber was rather spacious, with traces of dried, blackened pools. Despite the many years that had passed, the smell of blood lingered.

Derrick surveyed the area and suspected that a sacrificial ritual had once been held here.

A very, very long time ago... he silently added and discovered a candle left on the stone table which resembled an altar. He felt more certain about his guesses.

Why would Afternoon Town's residents secretly hold sacrificial rituals in an underground chamber? When the Giant King's Court ruled, they believed in the Giant King. Later, they came under the Lord who created everything... Did the owner of this building secretly worship other gods? Questions arose in Derrick's mind as he approached the altar. He saw that words had once been carved into the stone table, but natural or unnatural forces had damaged them.

After some careful identification, Derrick found three names:

"Ouroboros;

"Medici;

"Sasrir."

Ouroboros? Isn't that the name of the Angel of Fate? Mr. Fool mentioned before that Medici is also a King of Angels. Sasrir is another one? Derrick suddenly felt excited and horrified as he hurriedly turned his head to shout out for his companions to take a look.

But in his vision, the underground chamber was empty. There was only darkness. Haim and Joshua who were supposed to be behind him were gone!

Chapter 667: Prayer

They're gone?

There's only darkness?

Faced with this sudden change, Derrick's first reaction wasn't horror. Instead, he clasped his hands and pressed them to his mouth.

Pure light radiated from his body, dispersing the surrounding darkness and illuminating every corner of the underground chamber.

In Derrick's living environment, darkness was the most terrifying existence. Once they left the City of Silver, they had to constantly maintain the existence of light. Even a brief loss of light couldn't last for more than five seconds.

When Derrick first joined the exploration teams and lacked experience, he had nearly killed himself because of such a mistake. Thankfully, the Chief was standing nearby.

As the light slowly and continuously radiated from him, Derrick raised his tightly-gripped Axe of Hurricane and carefully observed his surroundings.

He discovered that not only had Haim and Joshua, the teammates who had entered the underground chamber with him, vanished, but the stone slabs and tufts of black hair on the walls had somehow turned blood-red. They were wet, as though freshly sprayed with blood.

Well versed in exploration records, Derrick calmed down and realized something. The problem didn't lie with Haim or Joshua, but with him!

All I did was approach the altar and silently read three names... Typically speaking, even angels will require someone to read or write down an accurate honorific name in its complete form before they can receive "prayers." And it's supposedly limited by range... I wonder if the Kings of Angels require the same conditions...

Uh, was one of those three names the key to activating the altar's hidden powers? Did I trigger it by reading in Jotun, a language capable of stirring the powers of nature? No, that isn't right. It would need to be spoken aloud. Even if these are the true names of Kings of Angels, I've never encountered such a problem before... Anxious and perplexed, Derrick turned around and returned to the stone table at the altar.

He was surprised to see that the words and symbols on the stone table were a lot more complete and clearer than before. It was as though the host of the ritual had just drawn it.

The words were written in three languages. They were in Jotun, Dragonese, and a language that Derrick couldn't recognize. However, he suspected that it was the ancient Hermes previously mentioned by Miss Justice and Mr. Hanged Man. This was because he had a basic understanding of some words from the conversations during the Tarot Gathering. They were rather similar to the words used on the stone table.

The Jotun and Dragonese conveyed identical content, repeating the three names and their corresponding titles.

Angel of Fate, Ouroboros;

Red Angel, Medici;

Dark Angel, Sasrir.

Following these names and titles was an entity Derrick was very familiar with.

Rose Redemption!

Sasrir really is a King of Angels known as Dark Angel. Are "He," the Angel of Fate, and the Red Angel the founders of Rose Redemption? I wonder whether Mr. Fool knows anything about "Him"... "He" definitely knows a great deal... The ancient Hermes likely conveys the same content... Even after Afternoon Town converted to the faith of the Lord who created everything, some residents secretly worshiped the three Kings of Angels beside the Lord... At this thought, a chill ran down Derrick's back. He felt as though he were approaching the reason the Creator had abandoned this land.

When he looked up again, he saw that the walls remained red, but Haim and Joshua were still nowhere to be seen.

Silently repeating them did nothing. Perhaps the names have no effect on their own... Derrick drew a breath and raised his Axe of Hurricane. He carefully walked toward the entrance, hoping to trace the problem to its source and determine what had caused his present situation.

One step, two steps, three steps. Like a gigantic candle, he returned to the hall upstairs.

There were rich shadows here, making it gloomy and eerily silent. The rotting chairs and the remnants of stone tables remained sitting there in silence, no different from before.

Unable to find Joshua and Haim, Derrick could only walk tensely toward the window, hoping to spot other members of the exploration team.

Thud... Thud... Amidst light footstep sounds, he approached a huge hole that should've been a window sill. He leaned forward and looked outside.

Countless dark buildings, some tall and some short, extended outward like steps.

Lightning flashed only rarely in the sky, while candlelight shone from many windows. The dim yellow flames danced without going out.

This... Derrick couldn't help but swallow a mouthful of saliva. He felt as though all the residents were still living in peace just before Afternoon Town encountered its cataclysm.

Haim, who was carrying an animal hide lantern, didn't need to bend his back to enter the underground chamber. He jokingly said to Joshua, "This house must belong to a human, but their family definitely has giant's bloodline in them. They're probably my height. Tsk, the last time we went to that ruined city, we had to bow our heads when using the main doors!"

Having a giant's bloodline didn't necessarily mean actual giant blood flowed through one's veins. Instead, it referred to physical traits inherited by descendants because their ancestors had consumed potions of that pathway. Great height was one such manifestation.

Joshua looked up at Haim and scoffed.

"That was you. I didn't need to."

"But it won't be long before you can advance. When the time comes, you won't be any shorter than me," Haim said with a smile, watching Derrick out of the corner of his eye to guard against any accidents as he approached the altar.

Joshua thought before saying, "Actually, I'm very curious. Chief is a Sequence 4 Demon Hunter. He should be like an ordinary giant with a height of three to four meters. Why does he look very ordinary, with him being only half a head taller than me?"

"A giant form? Will his clothes tear when he becomes a giant?" Joshua asked with a laugh.

"Unless his clothes and pants are mystical items." Haim and Joshua exchanged a knowing smile.

They were just about to turn their heads to share the joke with Derrick when they realized that the youth was gone!

Derrick, who should've been standing before the altar, had vanished!

Haim's and Joshua's expressions instantly turned grave. One raised his massive broadsword while the other lifted his red-gloved left hand.

They carefully approached the altar and inspected it, but found nothing suspicious.

Joshua was just about to attempt to identify the words on the stone table when Haim patted him on the shoulder.

"Don't look at it. I just recalled that Derrick was looking at those words before he vanished.

"Let's bring the Chief here."

"Okay." Joshua nodded.

They didn't leave in a panic. After surveying their surroundings, one of them rubbed his index finger against his middle finger and lit the candle remaining on the altar.

This was to protect Derrick so as to prevent him from falling into true darkness!

City of Silver exploration teams had encountered similar situations in ruined cities before. A teammate might appear to vanish suddenly when some power had actually concealed them. They remained where they were, but their companions, eager to seek help, would leave with the lanterns. The unfortunate person would then be devoured by true darkness, never to be found again. If another teammate hadn't later experienced the same situation and been rescued on the spot, no one would have learned the true cause of the earlier victim's death.

Once the candle was lit, dim yellow light spread outward. Haim and Joshua immediately left, reached the alley, and released the spiritual signal carried by every team member.

They didn't wait long before Colin Iliad leaped from the roof of another building and landed firmly before them.

"What happened?" the Demon Hunter asked in a deep voice.

The silver sword in his hand was already covered with a layer of light gray oil.

Haim immediately recounted what had happened. Finally, he said, "We couldn't determine why Derrick disappeared."

Derrick... Colin nodded in thought, walked past them, and headed straight into the corresponding building.

Despite the candlelight outside and its warm yellow glow, Derrick felt as though he had fallen into a frozen abyss. A chill spread from the depths of his heart.

He clenched the Axe of Hurricane in his right hand and withdrew his gaze from the town. Turning around, he returned to the underground chamber and once again stood before the altar.

He had already determined that he was the one with the problem!

However, he didn't have the urge to explore the strange Afternoon Town. He didn't even dare open the door.

Derrick neither panicked nor showed any obvious agitation, because he didn't believe he had encountered anything too serious.

As long as it's not an immediate threat, it's nothing severe... Derrick took a silent breath, bowed his head, and reverently whispered, "The Fool that doesn't belong to this era.

"The mysterious ruler above the gray fog.

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck..."

Klein, who had been admiring the scenery of the battlefield of the gods, had no choice but to enter the bathroom, arrange the items that would disrupt surveillance, and take four steps counterclockwise to ascend above the gray fog.

Suddenly, the prayers became exceptionally clear as the corresponding scene appeared before Klein's eyes.

He first saw the still-hazy Little Sun, before discovering that the environment around him was abnormal!

An indescribable darkness swirled around The Sun, and eyes of various shapes watched him silently from within it.

The densely packed eyes hid in the darkness like unwelcome observers, entirely unnoticed by Little Sun.

Afternoon Town is that dangerous? Klein was very aware of what dear Sun was up to lately.

He thought for a moment and instinctively believed that the darkness was extremely strange and surreal. Therefore, he abandoned the option of using the Sea God Scepter to respond to Little Sun. Instead, he switched to pulling him up above the gray fog.

Klein extended his spirituality, but he felt as though the crimson star seemed to be in a quagmire, making it rather difficult for him to pull him over.

Which King of Angels has he run afoul of this time? With a thought, Klein caused the mysterious space and the gray fog beneath him to ripple.

After advancing to Sequence 5, he could stir some of the powers above the gray fog, without the use of the Black Emperor card and the corresponding rituals!

Silently, Klein easily completed transporting Derrick as his figure appeared on the high-back chair belonging to him.

Meanwhile, Klein saw the strange darkness swirling around Little Sun's body shatter.

The Demon Hunter entered the underground chamber on high alert, followed closely by Haim and Joshua.

They saw that in front of the dim yellow candlelight, Derrick Berg's figure was quickly outlined like a picture.

Chapter 668: Confidence Might Also Be a Weakness

Above the gray fog, within the palace supported by stone columns.

Derrick quickly repeated his encounter to Mr. Fool.

Dark Angel Sasrir... The names and titles of these Kings of Angels seem to have been washed away by the river of history. Almost no one knows them. If Little Sun hadn't discovered them in the Forsaken Land of the Gods, or if I hadn't personally encountered the ancient evil spirit suspected of being the Red Angel, I might not know of a single King of Angels. At most, I would've heard of the Amon family, with no way to investigate the Blasphemer further... Where is this Dark Angel now? Is "He" still alive? Is "He" still among the upper echelons of Rose Redemption? Klein felt wistful.

As for explaining himself if others noticed anything unusual, Klein disdained reminding Little Sun to fabricate an excuse.

To mysteriously disappear and then appear again, wasn't it very normal that all sorts of strange scenarios accompanied it?

At that moment, Derrick was very grateful that Mr. Fool hadn't asked any further questions. He feared that once he escaped the alternate Afternoon Town, he might be exposed to lethal darkness or hidden monsters. How long have they been watching me like that... Although Derrick had already thought of a reason above the gray fog, he still felt guilty and nervous.

Derrick didn't answer immediately, as that would make it seem as though he had already prepared an excuse. "Later, I found myself back here."

Everything he said was true and comprehensive. He had only concealed how he returned.

Derrick had no hope of successfully deceiving the Chief. If pressed, he planned to act confused and attribute everything to an abnormality in his body that he didn't understand.

This will definitely make the Chief suspicious, but Mr. Hanged Man and Miss Justice of the Spectator pathway both said he wouldn't ask too many questions about such matters. If I appear abnormal, will he value me more highly? He'll view me as a chess piece capable of counterbalancing Elder Lovia... The outside world is so complicated. I've only recently come to understand their reasoning... Derrick couldn't help sighing inwardly.

In such a harsh environment, the loss of any measure of strength meant greater danger for the City of Silver. Internal conflict had been rare in the past, and what little there was had largely been confined to the six-member council. The first thing other Beyonders learned on patrols and expeditions was cooperation.

Almost instantly, candlelight shone from windows in different buildings. The dim yellow lights formed broken and unbroken clusters, silent and oppressive.

Klein didn't stay too long above the gray fog. He quickly returned to the bathroom and put away the corresponding items.

Let's hope there aren't any more incidents on Little Sun's side. It wouldn't look good if I kept entering the bathroom. Those in the know will realize I'm hiding a secret, while everyone else will think Gehrman Sparrow has a bladder problem. That would tarnish my persona!

Although I've already digested the Faceless potion, Marionettist Rosago advanced through each Sequence in turn. The Beyonder characteristic he left behind obviously contains a complete Faceless potion... Using it as a main ingredient is equivalent to drinking another Faceless, Magician, Clown, and Seer potion, perhaps even more...

Just as he was wondering if it was almost time for dinner and was planning to take out his golden pocket watch to take a look, the scene before his eyes suddenly turned black. He could hardly see his fingers.

It's night again... The intervals don't follow a pattern... If we encounter a monster and both sides are in an intense battle, what happens when the sky suddenly turns dark? Monsters are also creatures, so they need to sleep as well; otherwise, there's a high chance that they will disappear into the night... Heh heh, both parties in combat have to lie down and sleep, only to continue when they wake up... Is this really a story that can pass the censors? Klein, who had relaxed after successfully advancing, lampooned as he quickly walked to his bed.

Just as he got in bed, he suddenly thought of a problem.

The night here is very dangerous. If living creatures do not sleep, they will vanish completely.

The darkness of the Forsaken Land of the Gods, including that around the City of Silver, is similarly dangerous. Without light to disperse it, humans will vanish completely after more than five seconds in the darkness.

It's really similar... Could there really be some connection here?

Klein shook his head and, with Cogitation, entered a dream.

Amidst the dream, he realized that he had switched locations again once he became lucid!

The last time he left the dream, he was on the boulder where Admiral of Stars Cattleya was sitting as she hugged her knees. This time, he was facing a staircase.

Sunset streamed through the stained-glass panes high above, making the sculpture-adorned black spiral staircase look exceedingly beautiful.

Klein instinctively looked to the side and found Queen Mystic standing high up the staircase.

The long, chestnut-haired lady wasn't wearing her slit dress. Instead, she wore a white shirt with lace and floral ribbons beneath a simple dark blue coat. Her lower half remained clad in beige trousers and black leather boots. However, Klein suspected that Queen Mystic probably had an entire wardrobe, perhaps even a room, full of trousers and leather boots in the same style.

"What's the matter?" Klein took the initiative to ask.

Queen Mystic caressed the handrail with her right hand as she slowly walked down.

"Confidence might be a weakness at times.

"You trust that copper whistle and paper crane of yours too much. Perhaps one day, they will become a source of danger."

Klein felt a little uneasy at that, but he didn't show it.

"I don't understand what you mean."

"That Sealed Artifact can't actually block the ravings filling these waters? Is that why Heath Doyle mutated?" Klein keenly read between the lines.

Queen Mystic nodded.

"Under ordinary circumstances, it can. But do you know who the ravings that fill these waters come from?"

Without waiting for Klein's reply, she gave the answer.

"The True Creator."

Chapter 669: Exchange of Information

The True Creator? The ravings that fill these waters come from the True Creator? Upon hearing Queen Mystic's answer, Klein was surprised yet somewhat relieved.

He was relieved because, just as Queen Mystic had said, if she hadn't boarded the ship, Heath Doyle might have mutated despite possessing a Sealed Artifact that could suppress his hearing to a certain extent.

Low- and Mid-Sequence Beyonders of other pathways would merely feel oppressed and irritable, or occasionally suffer nightmares, when exposed indirectly to the True Creator's ravings. But for a Rose Bishop, the Sequence 0 of his pathway was the True Creator. Immersed in this evil god's ravings, something would've happened to him sooner or later even if he were deaf.

If Heath Doyle went mad or lost control, the peculiarities of these waters would make it extremely difficult for everyone else aboard to survive. However, Klein believed that as long as he survived the initial attack, he would have a chance to pray to The Fool and respond with the Sea God Scepter from above the gray fog, resolving the problem.

He was surprised because the source of the ravings completely contradicted his theory. He had imagined these waters to be a battlefield from the Second Epoch, where the ancient gods fought the Creator worshiped by the City of Silver, with their subsidiary gods, the corresponding angels, also taking part. To his surprise, this premise appeared to have been wrong from the beginning, because the True Creator had apparently first appeared after the Cataclysm at the end of the Third Epoch!

I cannot rule out the possibility that the ravings were later left behind... Klein didn't blindly make a conclusion. He watched as Queen Mystic walked past him as she caressed the sculptures on the handrail and slowly walked down.

The lady's back was tall; neither fat nor thin. Her figure was extremely proportioned, and her long chestnut hair simply cascaded down.

This made Klein feel a sense of familiarity. After recalling the matters involving Queen Mystic, he finally recalled the source of the familiarity.

While searching for the Cards of Blasphemy at the Roselle Memorial Exhibition, he had seen a similar figure from behind. She had been dressed rather oddly: despite wearing a layered yellow dress with a youthful air, she had paired it with an unfashionable black bonnet.

She was likely Queen Mystic. She had also visited the Roselle Memorial Exhibition ahead of time... Back then, she was looking at the basic education books the emperor had improved for his children and the Chinese Chess and toy blocks... She was certain that I had taken the Black Emperor card...

The emperor's eldest daughter, Bernadette, founded Element Dawn to oppose the Moses Ascetic Order. And according to the diary, she admired the maxim, "Do as you wish, but do no harm." From those two points, one could infer that she was likely a demigod of the Mystery Pryer pathway...

Sequence 4 of the Mystery Pryer pathway is Mysticologist... Queen Mystic... Of course, Queen Mystic has been renowned across the seas for more than a century. She certainly isn't merely Sequence 4 now. Yes, after leaving Queen Mystic, Cattleya joined the Moses Ascetic Order...

This queen seems to take the interpretation of Roselle's diary very seriously...

All sorts of fragmented pieces of information came together as Klein suddenly had a theory.

Perhaps Queen Mystic was Emperor Roselle's eldest daughter, Bernadette Gustav!

This might explain why this queen dresses in a style resembling Earth's yet still looks rather strange. This is actually the projection of the Giant King's Court!

Afternoon Town.

Upon seeing the candlelight light up in the buildings outside, the silent town seemed to come to life. Derrick felt as though they had plummeted into a nightmare as they instantly tensed up.

"The power of the altar has leaked out."

Chapter 670: Repenter

That strange Afternoon Town has infiltrated the real one? Derrick vaguely understood what the Chief meant and had a rough idea why it had happened.

He suspected that when Mr. Fool pulled him out of the strange Afternoon Town, "He" had broken an intricate balance, causing the powers to seep out.

This also explained why Demon Hunter Colin Iliad didn't vanish immediately when he repeated the attempt.

Just as Derrick was wondering what changes this would bring, Colin, chief of the six-member council, took luminescent powder from a pouch on his belt and scattered it into the air.

The powder suddenly exploded, spewing silver light upward. It was exceptionally conspicuous in the dark surroundings.

Derrick, Haim, and Joshua knew exactly what the signal meant: don't run about recklessly; remain in your area, stay alert for approaching enemies, and await rescue!

Without a doubt, this was a signal to all the other exploration teams scattered across Afternoon Town.

Colin released the signal three times in succession during the interval between two lightning bolts. Then he turned to Derrick and the others. "We'll sweep outward and rendezvous with the rest.

"Be careful along the way."

"Yes, Chief!" Derrick had already forgotten his earlier grievance. He only wanted to help his companions as soon as possible.

Following Colin's instructions, he took the small team's left flank. On the other side stood the red-gloved Joshua, holding an iron-black sword. The relatively stronger Dawn Paladin, Haim, guarded the rear, while the Demon Hunter led from three steps ahead.

As lightning flashed at relatively short intervals, gloomy Afternoon Town alternated between light and darkness. In every window, candle flames wavered with eerie tranquility.

Derrick was no longer the fledgling he once was. Although he was nervous, his palm didn't sweat while holding the Axe of Hurricane. He skillfully moved his gaze around, wary against any monster that might leap out from the buildings on both sides.

After a flash of lightning, the world was thrown back into darkness. The bits of candlelight in Afternoon Town seemed to await travelers that needed lodging.

The light from the animal-hide lantern in Haim's hand spread outward but barely illuminated the area around him. It was less effective than Derrick's night vision.

Its only apparent use was to dispel the dense darkness surrounding them.

At that moment, Derrick suddenly felt a chill on the back of his neck, despite the absence of any cold wind!

He didn't turn his head instinctively. Instead, he stepped diagonally, half-turned, and glanced out of the corner of his eye.

He saw the nearly 2.3-meter-tall Haim looking at him with a gloomy expression. He cleaved down with the broadsword in his hand!

Bang!

Derrick somersaulted to dodge the strike as he seemed to still hear the echoing sound of the intense wind.

Following that, he heard the Chief's voice.

"What happened?"

"Haim attacked me!" Derrick rolled toward Colin and sprang to his feet.

"Me?" Holding the animal-hide lantern in one hand and his broadsword in the other, Haim looked baffled.

Colin glanced at Derrick.

"I didn't notice anything abnormal about him."

As he spoke, the Demon Hunter's eyes produced two dark green symbols.

He surveyed the area and said, "Did the attacker take on Haim's appearance?"

Before he finished his sentence, the silver sword that was clenched tightly in his right hand stabbed backward!

Oof!

Amid the dull clash, a figure appeared in the darkness. He had disheveled, grizzled hair, deep wrinkles, weathered yet profound blue eyes, and a silver sword coated in light gray oil. He looked identical to Demon Hunter Colin, except for his gloomy expression and dark skin.

Bam! Bam! Bam!

Two silver swords clashed in midair, sending sparks flying.

Meanwhile, Colin Iliad bellowed, "Light!"

Light? Derrick instinctively raised his hands and pressed them to his mouth and nose.

At that instant, he saw three figures dash from the surrounding darkness. One was the tall and muscular Haim, another was the red-gloved Joshua, and the last was a tall youth with a childlike appearance: Derrick himself!

Derrick wasn't thrown into a fluster. He acted in accordance with the Chief's instructions and released lustrous brilliance from his body.

The three figures seemed horrified of this as they raised their palms to cover their faces while attempting to escape to the sides.

However, their speeds were in no way comparable to the speed of light.

The lustrous radiance illuminated the surroundings and enveloped the three figures.

They opened their mouths and let out silent screams, but they soon dimmed and vanished.

As the light surged outward, it engulfed the two Demon Hunters. One immediately stiffened, its movements slowing before all color drained from it and it turned completely black.

Oof!

The silver sword coated in light gray oil pierced the monster, but it was like stabbing empty air and caused no actual damage.

At that moment, the black monster suddenly set itself ablaze and fractured into distorted shadows that corroded the light and flames inch by inch.

Colin retracted his silver sword and turned his head to Derrick and company.

"The monsters this time are our shadows.

"Their weakness is the brightness of light!"

As he spoke, the Chief's body produced a bright and holy light of dawn, illuminating the entire street as though it was day.

This was a Beyonder power granted to Dawn Paladins of the Warrior pathway. He hadn't used it from the beginning because its effects were conspicuous, and he had no idea what unforeseen consequences it might cause. Now, however, he knew the weakness of Afternoon Town's monsters!

The Chief's light of dawn spread like a domain. Haim abandoned his plan to use a similar Beyonder power and continued holding up his animal-hide lantern. He, Derrick, and Joshua followed Colin Iliad onto another street.

Before long, the quartet arrived at a half-collapsed cathedral.

The cathedral had once possessed a tower, and its classical stone columns and masonry looked heavy and dark.

Passing through a doorway wide even for a giant, Derrick followed the Chief into the prayer hall. The deity's statue had been destroyed, but some unknown entity had lit the candle on the altar.

Before the altar, a figure in a plain white robe lay prostrate, praying too softly for anyone to hear.

"He's not one of us." Haim, who also had night vision, was the first to discover the abnormality thanks to his height.

That means it isn't a monster formed from one of our teammates' shadows... Derrick inwardly completed Haim's unspoken thought.

This meant an unknown, and an unknown often represented extreme danger!

"There should've been an exploration team here." Colin contracted the range of his light of dawn to avoid provoking the white-robed figure.

Haim, Joshua, and Derrick suddenly fell silent. Under such a situation, the absence of the exploration team's immediate appearance basically meant an undesirable outcome.

Their minds raced as two men dressed in tight black clothes walked out from the right side of the hall. They were the two members of the exploration team at the cathedral.

"Chief, those shadows... Those shadows are problematic! Laroya was swallowed by his own shadow!" One of the teammates swiftly made his way to Colin and immediately said in agitation and fear.

Has someone already been sacrificed... While Derrick's heart sank, he saw the light of dawn expand, enveloping the two teammates within.

Ferocious expressions suddenly appeared on their faces as their bodies rapidly darkened. Just two seconds later, they vanished completely, like shadows exposed to light.

Smack! Smack! Smack!

White bones and globs of bloody flesh fell from their bodies, smashing to the ground.

Light slowly effused out of these mangled carcasses.

Colin retracted his gaze and said expressionlessly, "Go closer to the ecclesiastic and listen to what he's reciting."

Derrick and the others nodded in silence and set off toward the collapsed statue.

After about ten steps, they saw the white-robed figure's face from an angle. He was a middle-aged man with deep-set features, and he was weeping.

This ecclesiastic's face was nearly plastered to the ground as he muttered to himself, "Omnipotent Lord, I repent. ... tempted Sasrir. The Kings often came to the palace belonging to the dusk to conspire.

"It was unknown when the people in this town changed. They set up secret altars and held strange rituals, doing things you forbade.

"It was already too late by the time I discovered all of this. Degeneration, bloodshed, darkness, rot, murders, corruption, and shadows had already drowned this piece of land.

"A huge calamity will begin here!"

These words kept repeating like a prophet describing the inevitable future with a heavy voice.

A great calamity would begin here? Is this where the Lord who created everything began abandoning this land? And who tempted Dark Angel Sasrir? The ecclesiastic should've named someone after saying "I repent," but there was only a blank... Had he originally spoken the name, only for it to vanish on its own? Who erased it? This ecclesiastic must have come from that strange Afternoon Town and appeared here after the balance was broken. Otherwise, the previous expedition should've discovered him... Countless thoughts flashed through Derrick's mind in an instant.

At that moment, he saw the Chief take a stride, walking to the side of the ecclesiastic in white.

Klein awoke from his dream as midday sunlight streamed through the window into his eyes.

He rolled out of bed and unhurriedly made his way to the pirates' dining hall.

When Frank Lee saw his arrival, he immediately waved at him.

"Gehrman, I discovered something new!"

Fuck, what now... Klein's heart immediately leaped into his throat.

"A new invention of yours?"

"No, it isn't." Frank shook his head excitedly. "I planned to study the fish in these waters. They can probably dream! But when I tried fishing, I caught this strange object instead."

Chapter 671: The Fourth Name

Strange item? Klein immediately felt a headache coming on, but his expression remained unperturbed as he asked, "What is it?"

"A fish with human fingers growing inside it!" Without waiting for Gehrman Sparrow's reply, Frank ran into the dining hall. Before long, he returned holding a strange bluish-black fish.

The fish was of an ordinary size. Where its eyes should have been, it had a face resembling a human's. Its belly had been cut open, revealing three bloody fingers stuffed inside.

"I didn't put them there. It was already like that! Look at its teeth. It couldn't possibly eat something like that, so the fingers could only have grown by themselves! Of course, I still can't determine what effect they'll have on its body," Frank hurriedly explained.

Klein glanced at the fish and said after some deliberation, "Someone might also have stuffed them inside."

"...That makes sense. Then it isn't such a remarkable fish after all." Frank froze for a second, looking somewhat disappointed. "Fingers count as flesh and blood. I'll ask Heath. He's an expert in such matters."

As he spoke, he surveyed the area and found Heath Doyle, who was huddled in a corner eating.

Frank quickly went over and placed the bluish-black fish before Bloodless.

Heath Doyle extended his hands and leaned his face toward the fish.

At the sight, Frank had the inexplicable feeling that something was amiss.

He soon reacted and laughed.

"No, this isn't food for you. You've been eating fish all this time to the point that even your body smells of fish.

"I meant to ask whether you recognize the fingers inside the fish's stomach. Can you find their original owner?"

Heath Doyle stopped leaning forward and carefully studied them for a few seconds.

"They belonged to a Rose Bishop--at least a Rose Bishop."

He took out the three bloody fingers and stacked them together.

After a brief moment, the fingers melted like wax, turning into a puddle of sticky flesh and blood.

The flesh and blood squirmed as they drew a word in blood-red: "Help!"

Fingers from a Rose Bishop... "Help"... At the sight, Klein immediately made a connection.

He recalled the Saint of Darkness, Leomaster, from the dream world!

This Aurora Order saint was in a particular ruin, where the remnant powers of an angel or deity of the Spectator pathway had caused his personality to split and produce a benevolent persona. This had left him trapped there.

His good and evil sides fought constantly, often clashing on the level of the psyche. The main persona, which inclined more toward darkness, gradually gained the upper hand, while the good persona hid within the world of his mind, seeking help.

Therefore, this is an attempt by Leomaster's good side to call for help? As a saint of the Aurora Order, he might very well have advanced from a Shepherd, so it's nothing strange to have the Beyonder powers of a Rose Bishop... Klein nodded in thought, believing that his judgment was likely close to the truth.

"Help? How?" Frank Lee turned to Gehrman Sparrow with a blank look.

You should ask your captain, not me... Klein shook his head.

"You don't have to bother.

"There are too many oddities with these waters."

He said this because, judging from the dream, Leomaster's main persona held an overwhelming advantage. To truly rescue him, they would need to prepare to face a demigod. Although the good side would certainly interfere, all it could do was weaken the Saint of Darkness to some extent. He would still be a demigod.

Of course, with Queen Mystic aboard the Future, an attempt wasn't impossible. But if it were so easy to rescue Leomaster and turn him into a wholly benevolent Saint of Darkness, Klein believed the queen would have done so long ago. She hadn't acted because it was impractical.

For example, wherever Leomaster is causes one's spirit to dissociate, to the point that even Queen Mystic doesn't dare challenge it... Leomaster's dream was only partially restored, yet it nearly put me in a situation I couldn't handle. In the end, I resolved it quickly with the Sea God Scepter. If I met him in the real world, my personality might truly dissociate and land me in an asylum. I'd have to borrow the Mental Terror Candle from Father Utravsky to have any hope of treatment... Heh heh. I could also ask Miss Justice to treat me, but she still isn't strong enough... Klein thought, recalling the incident with wry amusement.

"Yeah." Frank Lee trusted Gehrman Sparrow a lot. "Perhaps the fellow who's asking for help is long dead..."

As he said this, his eyes suddenly lit up, and he stared at Heath Doyle.

"Can you wipe out the mental imprint left on this flesh and blood?"

"Yes," Heath Doyle replied succinctly.

The corners of Frank Lee's mouth spread bit by bit until he smiled like a two-hundred-pound child.

"I've always been very curious about the flesh-and-blood structure of a Rose Bishop.

"I've always wondered what would result from using similar flesh and blood as a medium for crossbreeding."

One day, you'll die in the middle of one of your experiments. Thankfully, I'll be leaving this ship soon... Klein had the inexplicable impression of a mischievous child entering an armory.

Heath Doyle, whose pallid face was nearly translucent, was taken aback for two seconds before sincerely saying, "Thank you."

"Why thank me?" Frank Lee scratched his head, looking completely puzzled.

He's probably thanking you for restraining your curiosity and not using his flesh and blood as an experimental subject. You're a trustworthy companion... The corners of Klein's mouth twitched as he tried to interpret the reason. He realized that the Future's first and second mates had rather unusual ways of thinking.

Afternoon Town, inside the half-collapsed cathedral.

Colin stood beside the ecclesiastic in white and softly asked, "Who are the Kings?

"What is the huge calamity?

"Who tempted Sasrir?"

The ecclesiastic didn't seem to hear him as he continued prostrating to the ground. He repeated his words of penitence as though he was an illusory recording left behind by the environment.

A wraith, specter, or an evil spirit? Derrick looked in that direction, feeling somewhat nervous.

Seeing no response from the ecclesiastic, Colin extended his right hand and slowly moved his silver sword toward the man.

However, despite the sharp blade tip reaching the back of his head, the ecclesiastic remained in penitence, as though nothing had changed.

Colin Iliad retracted his silver sword as he surveyed the area with dark green symbols in his eyes.

Then, he walked diagonally toward the altar and turned his gaze to the candle emitting a yellow light.

After a few seconds of silence, he extended his left hand and extinguished every candle.

The collapsed statue of the deity in the middle of the altar suddenly dimmed, and the prostrate man in white finally ceased his penance.

He slowly raised his head. His face was a gloomy green, and his gaze brimmed with hatred.

Before Derrick, Haim, and Joshua could react, the devout ecclesiastic lunged forward with astonishing speed, leaving an afterimage behind.

Colin was already prepared for it. He took a step diagonally forward with his right foot, turned his body halfway, and swept backward with the silver sword in his left hand.

On the sword, spots of light soared, instantly forming a gigantic storm.

The storm, formed entirely of light, swept through the surroundings. It froze the ecclesiastic in midair before devouring him completely.

The storm quickly subsided. Colin looked at the ecclesiastic, whose body had been infused with the light of dawn, then repeated his questions.

"Who are the Kings?

"What is the huge calamity?

"Who tempted Sasrir?"

The ecclesiastic, whose figure had already grown indistinct, replied dazedly, "The Kings are Sasrir, Ouroboros, Medici..."

Just as he was about to say the fourth name, a transparent flame tore through him from the inside!

The flame instantly engulfed him, burning him into a spreading black gas.

So the Kings refer to the Kings of Angels... What's the fourth name? Why did he self-destruct just as he was about to say it? Is it the one who tempted Sasrir, or someone else? Derrick was filled with questions.

As the ecclesiastic died, roars like those of wild beasts suddenly erupted from the streets outside and throughout Afternoon Town.

Derrick subconsciously looked out the window and saw a gigantic face.

The glass it had originally been plastered against had grown a single, unusual eye. Its face was covered in short, dense black hair.

Tap! Tap! Tap! Another similar monster rushed out from inside the cathedral. It had the build of an ordinary human and two eyes, but the surface of its body was similarly covered with short black hair that resembled a beast's.

"A degenerate town that has been completely corrupted..." Colin sighed as he faced one of the monsters.

Derrick, Haim, and Joshua also took up battle positions in a bid to fend off the remaining monster.

The Future continued cruising in peace before encountering a brief night once more.

After entering the dream world, Klein found himself back where he had been--at Admiral of Stars Cattleya's side.

He was about to examine the projection of the Giant King's Court on the mountain opposite them for more clues when Cattleya, hugging her knees, suddenly asked in a heavy voice,

"Have you met her?"

Klein grunted tersely in acknowledgment, making no attempt to hide the truth.

Cattleya pursed her lips and asked, "She's on the ship?"

"Yes." Klein turned his head and looked at the Admiral of Stars as he said in passing, "You have very deep feelings for her."

Cattleya's expression was no longer as lost and dazed as before. Biting her lip, she said self-deprecatingly, "That's right.

"I was by her side before I was three. Heh heh. That's what they say, but I no longer have any actual memories of that.

"She taught me, and she held me by the hand as we adventured. She watched me grow up. To me, she's my captain and my teacher, as well... as well as my mother..."

As Cattleya spoke, she suddenly fell silent.

Chapter 672: Bystanier

Glancing at the silent Cattleya, Klein suddenly felt a little awkward.

He didn't speak again as he turned and jumped off the boulder. He walked into the black cloister through the half-opened door.

In the square enclosed by a gloomy tower and other buildings, the remnants of wartime fires still burned. Gigantic arrows were embedded in the ground, their shafts swaying gently in the wind.

Frank Lee was still digging and planting something, but what lay at his feet was no longer food. It was a pool of flesh and blood.

"What experiments do you plan to perform on them?" Klein couldn't resist asking as he passed.

Frank smiled excitedly. "Plenty!

"For example, all I need is a single ox that can meet an entire ship's demand for meat. Every time a piece is sliced off, new flesh will grow back!"

...Why an ox again? For a moment, Klein was at a loss for words. All he could do was silently draw the crimson moon in his mind.

Along the way, he passed Navigator Ottolov, who sat on the ground reading; Nina, who was so drunk that she was nearly taking off her clothes; and Heath Doyle, who hid silently in the shadows of a corner. He then arrived at the hall filled with murals.

At some point in time, Anderson Hood had conjured a reclining chair and was leisurely lying on it, observing the extremely religious and holy murals on the dome.

"Sigh, we're finally about to leave these cursed waters. Just two more noons and nights!" Seeing Gehrman Sparrow enter, the Strongest Hunter sighed from the bottom of his heart. "As long as we make it out successfully, I won't have to worry about any lingering problems."

Klein originally wished to ask him to shut up, but since he was talking about himself and no one else, he couldn't be bothered. He asked in passing, "Are you from Intis?"

"Barely. My father is from Intis, and my mother is from Segar," Anderson answered, with the full intention of having a chat.

Klein continued taking a few steps forward.

"Then, do you believe in the Eternal Blazing Sun, God of Steam and Machinery, or the God of Knowledge and Wisdom?"

Anderson's expression suddenly turned a little odd.

"I originally believed in the God of Knowledge and Wisdom, but the priests are too despicable. Just because I failed my examinations, they ignored my exceptionally handsome face and treated me like a retard. Pui! I'm simply better at other subjects. There's nothing wrong with my intelligence! I've always had an excellent eye for aesthetics and a solid foundation in drawing! Heh heh, before becoming a hunter, I dreamed of being an artist.

"Of course, after coming to the sea, my faith is more or less in the Lord of Storms."

Hearing Anderson's account, Klein suddenly thought of a joke--a priest of the God of Knowledge and Wisdom might say, "He failed his examinations? This child is hopeless. Just bury him."

He was about to steer the conversation toward Anderson's life as a hunter. After all, Anderson was the only person apart from Queen Mystic with whom he could communicate normally in this dream world. At that moment, he suddenly heard a door creak open.

In the depths of the hall of murals, there was the sound of a door opening!

Anderson just mentioned successfully leaving these waters... Klein felt an inexplicable urge to cover his face as he focused on the source of the sound.

Then he saw a man in a short linen robe running toward them from the depths of the hall of murals.

The man had luxuriant raven-black hair, but he had wrinkles on his face, as though he had suffered plenty of hardship.

Saint of Darkness Leomaster! Leomaster's good side! Klein recognized the man and immediately spotted a tall figure emerging from the depths of the hall of murals.

The figure was dressed in thick and heavy black full-body armor. His eyes emitted a deep redness.

He held a huge sword as he chased after Leomaster.

Clang! Clang! Clang!

His metal boots struck the ground in a rapid series of ringing clangs.

It's the real Leomaster! The main persona of the Saint of Darkness! Klein saw the two approaching figures as he instinctively turned to the side and quickly retreated.

He immediately pressed himself against the wall of the hall, only to realize that Anderson Hood had already leaped from his reclining chair and flattened himself against the opposite wall.

Sensing Gehrman Sparrow's gaze, Anderson grinned, returning a smile that meant "so you are the same as me."

Who's the same as you? This isn't cowardice. If you weren't around, I would've taken out the Sea God Scepter and fought the Saint of Darkness! I've always been considering what would happen in the real world if I were to help the good Leomaster kill the main persona in the dream...

Yes, there are crew members from the Future outside. If Leomaster's main persona truly goes mad, Queen Mystic will definitely act...

It shouldn't be a coincidence that these two fellows left their own dreams and came here... Both parties are already very close in the real world? Perhaps someone had directed the good Leomaster over. Queen Mystic? Many thoughts flashed through Klein's mind.

Leomaster, dressed in a short linen robe, saw the two men ahead. He had wanted to cry for help, but in the blink of an eye, he discovered that they had fled to opposite sides of the hall, as though they wanted no part in any of this.

...

He ran desperately out of the hall of murals.

The redness in the armored Leomaster's eyes darkened. He continued his pursuit, completely ignoring Klein and Anderson who were clinging close to the mural walls.

By the time they left the hall, Klein, who had some theories in mind, didn't hesitate to take a stride forward and rush out like a hunting leopard.

...

Anderson raised his right hand and grabbed at thin air, failing to stop Gehrman Sparrow in time.

"This guy was still rather rational a moment ago. Why did he suddenly go mad? He discovered something? What a strange person..." Anderson looked at the square outside, hesitated a few seconds, and finally chose to follow.

They chased them out of the black cloister and into the area where the Admiral of Stars was. Klein saw the linen-robed Leomaster circling the boulder to escape his evil counterpart. Seizing the opportunity, Leomaster faced the projection of the Giant King's Court on the opposite mountain and prayed softly in ancient Hermes, "The Lord that created everything;

"You are omnipotent and omniscient..."

After completing another circuit, the good Leomaster continued chanting,

"You are the source of everything great. You are the Beginning and the End;

"You are the God of Gods. You are the ruler of the vast astral world!"

With the honorific name spoken, the sea of clouds that separated the two mountains suddenly stirred before slowly splitting apart and revealing a deep crevice at the bottom.

The projection of the Giant King's Court on the opposite side suddenly drew in the frozen dusk from the distant horizon!

However, nothing happened after that.

Klein seemed to realize something. He turned toward the buildings near the black cloister's entrance and saw the beautiful yet distant Queen Mystic Bernadette silently watching everything from behind a spotless floor-to-ceiling window.

She was indeed the one who had made the good Leomaster escape his dream and come here... Since a Listener from the Aurora Order could bring Little Jack into the Forsaken Land of God, there's no reason the Saint of Darkness couldn't do the same! With no path ahead and the projection of the Giant King's Court before him, Leomaster's split personality would naturally try to escape into it. In doing so, he demonstrated the method of entering the Forsaken Land of God to the person secretly observing him... Certain of his conclusion, Klein withdrew his gaze.

As for why Leomaster hadn't succeeded, Klein believed it was because he wasn't in the right place!

He needs to venture deep into those waters and find a particular place surrounded by danger and strangeness. Then, by chanting the honorific name of the City of Silver's Creator within the dream brought on by night, he can open the passage hidden in the projection of the Giant King's Court? After that, with the aid of the dream, a person can carry their body and ship through the fog where reality and illusion intermingle and reach the shores of the Forsaken Land of God? Various possibilities occurred to Klein.

To him, the method of entering the Forsaken Land of God wasn't something he needed to care about. If he wished to do so, once he advanced to Sequence 4 and truly obtained a certain level of godhood, he could get Little Sun to set up a descent or bestowment ritual for him to directly descend!

However, if he used this to reverse engineer the way to leave the Forsaken Land of God, it would be extremely useful. This was priceless for the City of Silver!

From the looks of it, the key to leaving the Forsaken Land of God truly lies in the Giant King's Court, but there's no way to guess what it is... Amid his thoughts, pea vines rapidly sprouted from the soil, instantly turning the area outside the black cloister into a green forest and forcibly separating the Saint of Darkness's two personas.

Then, Klein saw through the gaps of the pea vines that Cattleya had stood up. She was no longer sitting there hugging her knees.

Sizzle!

Derrick rolled, sprang up, and cleaved at the giant's leg, which was covered in short black hair. Silver lightning erupted and smote the single-eyed monster, leaving it trembling and rooted to the spot.

Derrick didn't miss this opportunity as he immediately opened up his arms.

Bright and pure holy light descended and enveloped the mutated giant.

In the brilliance, the monster collapsed with a tragic cry as its body produced black fog.

After a series of battles, Derrick discovered that the monsters produced by the strange Afternoon Town were all afraid of intense light despite their different traits.

This experience had allowed him to avoid injury and keep his teammates alive.

After a while, Colin finished off the strongest monster, and silence returned to Afternoon Town. Every candle had gone out.

The Chief of the six-member council surveyed the area before saying with a sigh, "Let's regroup before we set up camp."

By then, the assembled teams had been reduced to a third of their original number. Only six people remained!

Colin Iliad's true goal was the Giant King's Court, but their encounter in Afternoon Town had made him realize that the exploration couldn't be rushed. The Giant King's Court might contain deep secrets about the cataclysm and unimaginable dangers. Therefore, he needed at least half a year, perhaps even two years, of preparation and preliminary exploration before he could attempt to open it.

Chapter 673: Blood Text

Layers of pea vines that appeared as though they were capable of weaving into a stairway into heaven dropped and shrank back into the soil.

Both the Saint of Darkness's main persona and Leomaster's good side had vanished. Only Cattleya remained atop the boulder, surveying her surroundings with a blank look.

Did Queen Mystic throw Leomaster's main persona and good side back into his own dream? Or did she pull them elsewhere in an attempt to understand the unique traits necessary for entering the Forsaken Land of God?

It seems like it's impossible to pull these two personalities into different dreams; otherwise, Queen Mystic would've long conversed with the good Leomaster individually, as well as agree to help him defeat the devil in exchange for relevant information, without going through so much trouble...

Of course, to truly kill off the Saint of Darkness's main persona, perhaps one will have to enter the dangerous ruins in the real world. Even Queen Mystic wouldn't dare to attempt it, as it might produce an evil queen who abides by "do as you wish, and do as much harm"... Klein turned his head once again in thought, looking at the nearby buildings around the black cloister's door. He saw that the figure belonging to Bernadette had vanished behind the clear floor-to-ceiling windows.

Klein didn't seek her out to learn whether Queen Mystic had obtained more information from Leomaster's good side. He remembered the essence of Gehrman Sparrow's persona--Mr. Fool's Blessed!

And Admiral of Stars knew very well that, in Mr. Fool's Tarot Gathering, The Sun came from the City of Silver in the Forsaken Land of God. To say that Mr. Fool didn't know the method of entering the Forsaken Land of God was unbelievable.

Therefore, as a Blessed, Gehrman Sparrow definitely lacked the motivation to delve deep into the matter!

In many matters, the persona that brings you success can also make you fail. This is a flaw of being a Faceless... Klein withdrew his gaze and looked back at the projection of the Giant King's Court on the opposite mountain, only to realize that the frozen sunset was slowly returning to the horizon.

On the same mountain, the boulder remained standing as Cattleya slowly sat down again, hugging her knees.

Noon and night switched thrice, but the actual amount of time that had passed in the outside world was just the daytime of a single day.

The Future circled the dangerous ruins and avoided the hidden dangers along the safe sea routes before finally arriving near the entrance to those waters.

Klein and company once again saw the ruin from the beginning of their journey, most of it submerged in seawater. They saw the gray stones and columns, as well as the huge dome at the top.

Previously, they could hear loud and clear panting sounds.

Bloodless had even painfully pointed out that a corpse was hidden in those ruins!

And that corpse was very likely to be the source of the panting!

At that moment, the immensely dangerous ruin inspired not fear but joy in those aboard the Future. Its appearance meant they were about to leave these absurd and terrifying waters!

At some point in time, Nina had already climbed up to the tall crow's nest and said loudly as she looked at the ruin, "Eh, there's a ship!"

A ship? Klein went around Anderson Hood, who was blocking his view, and approached the gunwale for a closer look.

Indeed, to the right of the stacked stones and stone columns, an ordinary three-masted sailboat was docked beside the ruin. Because it was partially obscured, the people aboard the Future would have difficulty noticing it unless they viewed it from a high vantage point or looked carefully.

The sailboat floated there without a single sailor on board. The silence made it terrifying.

"It's as though it was all eaten by the ruin." Anderson came over and shook his head with a sigh. "In these waters, one mustn't go close unless one knows the ruins well."

Someone who dares sketch a mural of an Angel of Fate has no right to say that... Your treasure-hunting team claimed to have abundant experience, but aren't you the only one left in the end... Without turning his head, Klein scoffed inwardly.

At that moment, Cattleya also came on deck and looked at the sailboat moored beside the ruin.

During this entire process, she didn't give Gehrman Sparrow a glance; it was as though he didn't exist.

After a brief moment of silence, Cattleya raised her hand to remove the heavy glasses on her nose bridge. The deep purple hue in her eyes swirled as though she was trying to outline one complex symbol after another.

A pair of eyes suddenly appeared above the empty sailboat; they were a pair of illusory, translucent dark purple eyes!

The pair of eyes slowly moved and circled the deck once before entering the cabin.

This Beyonder power is very useful... Come to think of it, judging by the abilities Queen Mystic and Admiral of Stars have demonstrated, a Mystery Pryer's powers have something of the flavor of fairy tales! Could Queen Mystic turn people into frogs? Also, is a Mystery Pryer's ability to pry into mysteries manifested in their eyes? Admiral of Stars's eyes are rather unusual. I need to take note... Klein speculated silently as he awaited the result of Cattleya's remote exploration.

After a while, the dark purple hue in Cattleya's eyes finally dimmed.

She rubbed her brow, put her glasses back on, and said to Anderson Hood and Frank Lee, "There's a problem inside."

As she spoke, she took a handful of colorful powder from her classical warlock robe and tossed it outward.

The powder didn't scatter onto the ground. Instead, it formed a realistic image in color.

The image's background resembled a captain's cabin. There was a photograph on the desk and a portrait on the wall, both depicting the same person.

He was a man from Feysac, with broad shoulders, light blond hair, and deep blue eyes!

This... Klein first found it familiar before he recalled where he had seen the man before!

Back when he was in Nas, an adventurer had been pursued by the second mate of the King of Immortality, Kircheis, and had run into the Lerdal Bar to seek help from the members of the Adventurer Association. At that instant, among the powerhouses who stood up to provide protection was a muscular man from Feysac who was more than two meters tall. Klein had found him rather strong and placed him at Sequence 6 at the very least.

Why would his ship suddenly enter these waters, and why would he rashly explore a dangerous ruin? Puzzled, Klein carefully observed the supernatural image on the deck.

This time, he saw a pool of blood on the floor, and beside the blood were a few words in Feysac: "The Fountain of Unaging..."

The final character trailed into a long streak of blood that joined clear smears extending toward the door.

Klein's mind reconstructed the scene. The Sequence 6 Feysacian had suffered a sudden attack and fallen to the ground, gravely injured. He had tried his best to record the source of the encounter, but just as he finished the first few words, some unknown entity seized him by the legs or head and dragged him away!

Considering how the red words weren't wiped away, Klein suspected that the entity that dragged the adventurer away wasn't a living person.

It should be the corpse of that ruin... he thought, feeling a headache.

"The Fountain of Unaging? They came here in search of the Fountain of Unaging?" Anderson Hood said in excitement.

"Clearly, but they didn't find it." Frank Lee shook his head in great disappointment.

He also looked forward to the Fountain of Unaging, believing that the fountain's waters could create qualitative changes to his various experiments.

The Fountain of Unaging... Slaughterer Kircheis was the one chasing after the young adventurer back then. He's the second mate of the King of Immortality... Rumor has it that the King of Immortality once drank the waters of the Fountain of Unaging... Kircheis even warned me--Gehrman Sparrow--not to interfere in the young adventurer's affairs, claiming that it was the will of the King of Immortality... Klein pieced together a rough approximation of the truth from various tidbits of information.

The young adventurer obtained the secret of the Fountain of Unaging from one of the King of Immortality's aides and was pursued for it. Later, under the protection of several powerful members of the Adventurer Association, he narrowly escaped Slaughterer Kircheis. To hide from the King of Immortality while seeking the Fountain of Unaging, they eventually chose to venture into these waters. Who could have known they would be wiped out in that ruin...

Could it be that the Fountain of Unaging is hidden deep in that ruin? Klein looked at the stacked gray stones and stone columns as he vaguely made a prediction.

As he temporarily had no way to verify his theory, nor did he know whose corpse was buried there, he didn't have the urge to explore or take risks. He rationally retracted his gaze.

I can ask Will Auceptin or Arrodes... Heh, perhaps the Fountain of Unaging is the pus produced by that rotting corpse... Klein speculated darkly.

At that moment, Cattleya considered Anderson and Frank's conversation before saying, "If their deaths resulted from their search for the Fountain of Unaging, I don't believe the man in the portrait and photograph would have had any reason to leave accurate information before his death. After all, whoever found him here wouldn't be his family."

That makes sense... If I were in his shoes and encountered a monster while seeking treasure, I wouldn't think of leaving clues for others before my death... Klein gave an imperceptible shake of his head, unable to think of a reason for the moment.

Cattleya looked at Anderson and Frank who wore looks of anticipation.

"A successful adventure requires detailed intelligence and sufficient preparation, but we have neither."

Her voice suddenly turned loud as it resounded in every corner of the ship.

"Continue sailing.

"Leave these waters!"

"Aye aye, Captain!" Nina, who was on the crow's nest, gulped a mouthful of beer.

A few minutes later, the descent and ascent that defied common sense occurred once again, but the prepared members of the Future were no longer left in a sorry state. They easily endured the thrilling experience of leaping over the ravine and being sent flying.

Soon, the Future landed over blue seas, and a distance away was a gigantic storm blotting out the sky.

Not far away, there was another ship floating there silently. It was two hundred meters long, its front and back curved up, making it look like a crescent.

Seeing the flag depicting a black tombstone, Klein instantly recalled the corresponding name: the Death Announcer!

It was the flagship of the King of Immortality Agalito!

At that moment, Klein felt not fear or horror, but excitement and anticipation.

With Queen Mystic aboard, and with no need for me to hide this time... Together, she, Admiral of Stars, Anderson Hood, the crew of the Future, and I have a chance to wipe out the King of Immortality's crew! I've found food for Creeping Hunger! I've found a candidate for a marionette!

At that moment, the Death Announcer suddenly turned and fled at unprecedented speed.

I-it fled... Klein instantly stared blankly.

Soon, the Death Announcer had vanished from his line of sight.

Chapter 674: Leaving the Ship

That was way too fast... I just had the thought... Klein looked at the undulating surface of the sea, temporarily unable to gather his thoughts.

Amidst his thoughts, he came up with a theory.

King of Immortality Agalito's second mate, Slaughterer Kircheis, is suspected to be a Devil or even a Desire Apostle. He possesses the Beyonder powers to detect danger ahead of time and the ability to lock onto the source of the danger. Then, would the King of Immortality be a Sequence 4 demigod of the Devil pathway to begin with?

So, the moment the crew of the Future possessed both the strength and the intent to harm him, he immediately sensed the danger and discovered Queen Mystic's presence, prompting him to retreat without hesitation?

Yes, this means Queen Mystic also intended to act. Otherwise, my thoughts would only have made the King of Immortality sneer and retaliate viciously...

Sigh, this Beyonder power of the Devil pathway is far too useful. It's nearly impossible to trap the King of Immortality or his first, second, or third mate... Klein thought regretfully as he looked at Anderson Hood.

The Strongest Hunter still wore a visibly twisted expression, as though despairing over the fact that his bad luck hadn't diminished. He was also astonished that the Death Announcer had fled with its tail between its legs, as though events had departed from the script in his mind.

His eyes darted around as he speculated, seemingly reaching a conclusion.

Unfortunately, Anderson's bad luck isn't strong enough. Otherwise, he could be sacrificed to bait the King of Immortality... Heh heh, isn't that the correct usage of a Provoker? Klein turned into the corridor and returned to his room.

Just as he pushed open the wooden door, he saw a familiar back standing before his window. She had a very proportionate figure and was dressed slightly oddly. She was none other than Queen Mystic, Bernadette.

Ma'am, didn't your father teach you not to enter someone's room without permission, especially the room of a male stranger? As a lady raised in a noble family, you should have waited at the door and politely requested permission to enter... Emperor, didn't you remember the books on etiquette? Klein silently grumbled as he closed the door behind him.

Without waiting for him to speak, Bernadette said with her back to him, "What happened earlier confirmed a theory of mine."

"What theory?" Klein suppressed his curiosity and asked with a deadpan expression.

Bernadette didn't turn her head as she looked out at the calm sea surface.

"Agalito's Fountain of Unaging is a scam.

"If there really is a Fountain of Unaging, it would've been one of the main ingredients for the Demoness of Unaging potion, or something produced by their corpses. Therefore, any man who claims to have drunk from the Fountain of Unaging is lying."

She didn't explain what a Demoness of Unaging was, seemingly certain that Gehrman Sparrow knew. Even if he didn't, he would have the means to find out later.

Fountain of Unaging... Demoness of Unaging... They do match. So the bloody message on the sailboat beside the ruin was meant to tell the world that the Fountain of Unaging is a scam? Has the King of Immortality, Agalito, repeatedly spread rumors of the Fountain of Unaging to lure adventurers and pirates into dangerous waters to die, or to seize the opportunity to slaughter them? That certainly reeks of a Devil... No wonder Slaughterer Kircheis warned me not to interfere... Klein thought for a few seconds, then deliberately murmured, "A scam..."

Queen Mystic Bernadette nodded and said gently, "This might be part of the ritual Agalito needs to advance to Sequence 4. It might also be necessary for digesting the Sequence 4 potion."

She paused as though making a silent sigh. Then, she said, "That's because the Sequence 4 of his pathway is Demon."

Demon? Sounds deceitful... Creating a treasure scam to harm others, that does match the style of a demon... Klein was instantly enlightened.

At that moment, Queen Mystic turned and gazed through her checkered black veil into Gehrman Sparrow's eyes.

It's my turn to provide information? Klein deliberated for two seconds.

"According to my analysis of the limited diary entries, Emperor Roselle faced an extreme predicament in his later years. This forced him to consider attempting something crazy."

He was completely candid in this respect, because the diary entries he had received so far didn't reveal what Emperor Roselle had wished to do in his later years, what predicament he faced, or what mad act he ultimately attempted.

Therefore, by providing this piece of information, he was hinting to Bernadette that, to learn the truth, she had to hand the diary entries from Emperor Roselle's critical period over to the Admiral of Stars, Cattleya.

Bernadette remained silent for a few seconds.

As the dark clouds shifted and sunlight shone through the window, the queen who ruled the Five Seas suddenly dissolved into bubbles, which burst and vanished.

The light refracted from the bubbles had produced different colors, filling the room with a fairytale-like dreamscape.
