If not for the Hidden Sage, the Mystery Pryer pathway would be truly fascinating... Klein reflected as he tapped his left thumb twice against the first joint of his index finger.

He activated his vision of Spirit Body Threads, but he didn't discover any additional black threads.

This meant that Bernadette had already left!

Phew... Klein silently sighed and quickly deactivated the vision.

Just as he was about to lie in bed to take a rest, he heard brisk footsteps approach.

Knock. Knock. Knock. Someone knocked at his door.

"Who is it?" Klein sat up.

"It's me." Cattleya's voice sounded.

Klein went over, feeling puzzled as he opened the door.

He didn't ask what it was about as he coldly looked at her, his gaze expressing everything.

Cattleya adjusted the heavy glasses on the bridge of her nose and said, "Leaving those waters didn't return us to where we entered. We're fewer than 100 nautical miles from Toscarter Island, while Nas is about three days away. Where do you wish to go?"

The entrance and exit are different? Surprised, Klein asked for confirmation, "Then is it possible to enter those waters from here?"

"No, we will directly fall into that bottomless ravine of the ocean. According to the results of divination, the people who did so are dead in the true sense of the word," Cattleya explained simply.

Is that so... Klein thought before saying, "To Toscarter Island."

He didn't choose Nas because the next Tarot Gathering was approaching, and he didn't wish to undertake anything time-consuming aboard the Future.

Furthermore, Toscarter Island was the easternmost colony of the Loen Kingdom. The currency used was pence, soli, and gold pounds. Klein no longer needed to consider the problem of foreign exchange.

"All right." Cattleya had no objection.

Watching her turn and walk to the captain's cabin, Klein shook his head slightly and sighed inwardly.

If you'd come earlier, you would've met Queen Mystic.

In the evening, the Future arrived at the port of Toscarter Island as it forcefully docked there.

Klein dressed as a gentleman, carried his leather suitcase, and came to the deck. He then handed the final payment, which he had divided between his two pockets, to the Admiral of Stars, Cattleya.

After deducting the Pugilist Beyonder characteristic worth 700 pounds, the balance came to 1,300 pounds.

As such, Klein's wealth was reduced to 8,436 pounds and 5 gold coins.

Cattleya received it in silence and opened her mouth as though she wanted to say something, but she ultimately didn't say a word.

"Are you getting off here, or are you going somewhere else?" She turned to look at Anderson Hood.

Realizing that Gehrman Sparrow was about to leave and recalling that he had hunted several pirates while aboard a pirate ship with him, Anderson immediately smiled.

"I'll get off here."

"You can pay now." Cattleya didn't spare him merely because Anderson had borrowed clothes from the pirates.

"All right." Anderson made no attempt to hide his pained expression as he reached out and pulled an ordinary button from the middle of his shirt.

Reluctantly, he handed it over.

"This is what I acquired from those waters. It's from the corpse that originated from an explorer from the Loen military.

"I'm not sure of its original name, so I can only call it by the corresponding Sequence 6 name based on the powers it exhibits. Yes, Judge.

"Its negative effects aren't especially severe. It makes the wearer prone to offending people or monsters. Perhaps one might even end up targeted by a demigod."

You call that not especially severe? If I were Admiral of Stars, I would've chosen that sword of yours... Klein scoffed inwardly as he watched Cattleya accept Anderson's fare.

He didn't bother with their affairs as he lifted his suitcase and left the Future, arriving at the pier of Toscarter Island.

Bang!

Anderson Hood leaped off the deck and landed beside him.

"Let's get a drink to celebrate our escape from those cursed waters!" the hunter invited cheerfully.

Klein swept his gaze over him, refusing the offer with his eyes. All he wanted was to put some distance between himself and this fellow with his bad luck and provocative aura.

"All right." Anderson looked from side to side, cleared his throat, and said, "Can you lend me some money? As you know, everything I had sank in those waters."

Having said that, he laughed.

"Don't worry. I'll repay you tomorrow morning. There are plenty of pirates in Toscarter's bars and brothels. I plan to ask them for a little sponsorship."

Extort those without bounties while cashing out those with bounties? Klein tsked inwardly and handed him a five-soli note.

"That little?" Anderson said with his mouth agape.

"It's enough for drinks, meals, and a hotel," Klein replied calmly. "Besides, it's worth one pound."

"One pound?" Anderson rubbed his eyes and said with a helpless smile, "All right, it's one pound. I'll return one pound to you tomorrow morning."

Chapter 675: Building Ties

Afraid that Gehrman Sparrow would go back on his word, Anderson snatched the five-soli note as he imagined ordinary beef sizzling over a fire and alcoholic drinks free of added sedatives.

Well, well, this fellow actually accepted it. I only said it in passing to reinforce my persona and show him that borrowing money from me isn't easy, so he wouldn't take a large sum and return to the Fog Sea after hunting pirates... Klein muttered inwardly.

To him, a Sequence 5 Hunter wouldn't starve or have nowhere to stay when placed in a place with plenty of pirates, even if he didn't have a single penny.

He gave an imperceptible shake of his head and was about to leave the dock when he suddenly heard a coarse shout. "Gehrman!"

...

Hearing Frank Lee's voice, Klein shuddered and turned around, his nerves taut.

The first mate of the Future, the Poison Expert with a 7,000-pound bounty, stood by the gunwale with his hands cupped around his mouth. Projecting his voice, he asked, "Where will you spend most of your time? Where should I send letters?

"I wish to share with you the latest results of my research."

I don't want to know... This fellow probably doesn't have many friends. I daresay many of the people he considers friends don't feel the same... Yes, Admiral of Stars still leans toward Queen Mystic and lacks a sense of belonging to the Tarot Club. Openly cultivating a spy at her side--no, a source of intelligence--will help me intimidate her, and it's a form of punishment on the level of "Gehrman Sparrow"... With that foundation, it would be reasonable and natural for Mr. Fool to punish her... Klein's thoughts raced as he took a notepad and the fountain pen he used for divination from his pocket.

He scribbled down the summoning ritual needed to summon his messenger, and he didn't forget to include the additional requirement of a gold coin.

With a whoosh, Klein flicked his wrist, sending the note flying at Frank Lee like a dart. It landed squarely in his hand.

"Excellent!" Frank Lee glanced at the information on the piece of paper and waved his hand in delight.

Klein didn't delay any further as he picked up his leather suitcase, left the dock, and began searching for a hotel.

During this process, he was initially adamantly opposed to Anderson's request to stay in the same hotel, but on second thought, he agreed.

He feared that this fellow, plagued by bad luck, would stumble into trouble again and bring disaster upon innocent guests and staff. Therefore, he decided to watch him closely and deal decisively with any problems that arose.

After checking in, Anderson went into his room with his key.

Bang! He sat down on a reclining chair as though relieved of a heavy burden.

After leaving those dangerous waters, he finally felt like he was human again. He didn't need to worry about suddenly dying.

After lying there in silence for a while, Anderson Hood slowly rose. He took out an iron-clad flask, turned over a glass, and poured himself some hot water.

He believed that he should collect himself and begin visiting the bars.

After filling his stomach and having a few drinks, he could seek out some sponsors!

After the hot water cooled a little, Anderson raised his cup and gulped it down comfortably.

Suddenly, he coughed intensely as his face turned a little purple.

Cough! Cough! Cough!

Anderson reached for his throat, but he seemed incapable of drawing a single breath.

With a crashing sound, the glass cup fell to the floor from his hand, shattering across the ground.

Cough... Cough... Cough... Anderson's coughing weakened as his face turned purple.

At that moment, his eyes seemed to produce a spark as the veins on the back of his hands squirmed like they were alive.

Bang!

Anderson fell to the ground, convulsing a little before becoming motionless. His breathing even seemed to come to a halt.

Seconds later, the corpse-like Anderson suddenly got up as he rubbed his face in fear.

"Damn it, I almost choked to death from drinking water...

"If that really happened, then I might be the hunter with the most laughable cause of death!

"Thankfully, thankfully, I bought this item at great cost before entering those waters. It finally came in handy today..."

As he spoke, Anderson took a doll made of straw from a secret pocket in his vest. Two eyes, a nose, and a mouth had been crudely drawn on it in ink.

The doll's surface had already begun to corrode, dripping pitch-black liquid one drop at a time.

In about eight seconds, it was completely reduced to a liquid, turning into a stain on the floor.

"My bad luck hasn't diminished at all. It's even gotten worse... Man, Gehrman Sparrow once told me of a prophecy, saying that the deadliest dangers often lurk in everyday life." Anderson paced about, carefully avoiding the shards of glass at his feet for fear they would cause yet another brush with death.

"No, I need to save myself! I need to save myself!" Anderson pulled open the door and carefully walked out.

He came straight to Klein's room, reached out his finger, and rapped on the door.

Soon, the wooden door, which was neither particularly thick nor sturdy, opened without a sound. Gehrman Sparrow, who had only taken off his coat, appeared before Anderson.

Anderson forced a smile and said, "Surprise!"

Bang!

The door closed right in front of him.

...

He was first stunned before he muttered to himself with a stiff expression, "I should change the way I talk."

Thump! Thump! Thump!

He knocked on Klein's door again.

The door quickly opened as a revolver was pointed at him.

"Haha, I only wanted to ask whether you know any Beyonders who can improve my luck." Anderson raised his hands halfway, frantically hinting that Gehrman Sparrow should give him the contact details of the powerhouse who had told him the prophecy.

Too late. I have no idea where Queen Mystic is... Eh? She didn't leave me a way to contact her. But since Frank Lee knows the ritual for summoning my messenger, Admiral of Stars must know it too, which means Bernadette knows it. Besides, after returning to Backlund, I can ask Miss Sharron for help. Queen Mystic moves in her circles, even if she seldom appears... Klein gave Anderson Hood a pitying look.

He wasn't fond of the Strongest Hunter and often mocked him inwardly while entertaining all manner of malicious thoughts. After all, Anderson was partly responsible for the loss of his cuff link. However, those thoughts remained thoughts. Klein had no intention of acting on them, and if Anderson genuinely sought his help, he wouldn't refuse.

Klein thought for a moment before saying, "I can ask for you, and I'll give you an answer tomorrow morning.

"But I suspect you don't have the means to pay me."

"I'll make the rounds of the bars later! Besides, I have plenty of stashes in the Fog Sea," Anderson replied without hesitation.

Klein nodded. As he closed the door, he said, "See you tomorrow morning. I hope you can survive until then."

Creak!

The door was locked once again.

"Was that a curse, or his way of wishing me luck?" Anderson whispered with a wry smile. "According to my experience, there probably won't be any accidents in the next two to three days."

Inside the room, Klein returned to his desk.

On it was a letter whose introduction he had already written, as well as an unfolded paper crane.

Regarding Anderson's problem, Klein had already known whom to ask before he agreed.

There was no doubt that the best way to counter the methods of a Snake of Fate was to question another Snake of Fate!

He considered the limited space on the unfolded paper crane and the questions he wished to ask, composing them in his mind. Finally, he raised his pencil and wrote:

"How should the bad luck curse from an Angel of Fate mural be removed?

"What's the name of the Sequence 4 potion for the Seer pathway? Where can the formula and main ingredients be obtained?"

Putting down the pencil, Klein carefully scrutinized the questions before folding the paper crane along its existing creases and placing it in his wallet.

After doing all of this, he continued writing to Mr. Azik.

In the letter, Klein first mentioned that, with Admiral of Stars's help, he had entered the dangerous waters at the easternmost edge of the Sonia Sea and successfully completed the ritual. Changing subjects, he described Admiral Hell Ludwell's inexplicable attack midway through the journey and how he had nearly suffered a terrible loss.

While on this topic, he began describing the ring on Admiral Hell's hand, which was suspected to be a relic from ancient Death. He asked very subtly if Mr. Azik had any recollection of it, or if he needed to obtain it to study it, so as to invoke more memories.

Klein then casually mentioned the Numinous Episcopate's Artificial Death Project and asked this big shot whether it was feasible and whether any records documented its details.

Finally, he mentioned that he didn't know the subsequent Sequences of his pathway or how to obtain the opportunities to advance. Klein then summarized the precautions one should take when traveling through those dangerous waters.

This was to provide Mr. Azik with information and prevent him from suddenly deciding to seek out the remnant aura of ancient Death without realizing the dangers that lurked there.

"...Rumor has it that those waters are filled with the True Creator's ravings. The higher one's Sequence, the more clearly one hears them and the more easily one is influenced, leading to madness or loss of control. Sequence 4 is the dividing line... However, a small number of demigods have found ways to move freely there..." Klein wrote at the end of his letter.

After folding the paper, he picked up Azik's copper whistle and summoned the huge skeleton messenger.

The messenger burrowed out of the ground and politely looked at Klein at eye level before opening its palm.

Not bad... Klein praised silently and handed the letter over.

Then, he brushed his teeth and took a bath before comfortably getting into bed.

After an unknown period of time, he found himself awake in a dream, and he saw the desolate plains and pitch-black steeple.

Following the now-familiar route into the depths of the steeple, Klein found Will Auceptin's reply among the scattered tarot cards.

"Friendly reminder: The paper crane is about to tear!

"The bad luck curse brought about by the mural can be resolved by Ricciardo.

"High-Sequence formulas for the Seer pathway can only be obtained from the crazy Zaratul or the Hornacis mountain range. If you are the Blessed of the Evernight, treat it as though I didn't say it.

"Sequence 4 of the Seer pathway is Bizarro Sorcerer!"

Chapter 676: Tripartite Transaction

Bizarro Sorcerer... Klein suddenly woke up from his dream as he opened his eyes to see the dark night.

It isn't noon yet. It's still very dangerous... he mumbled before going back to sleep.

Only then did he remember that he had already left those dangerous waters. He wouldn't vanish or go missing if he remained awake when night ended.

Phew, a stable environment like this is the best! I have to say, mysteriously vanishing because you stayed awake after dark would make a fine story for frightening children into going to bed on time. Heh, stories like that often scared me when I was young. Klein sat up, walked to the desk, and poured himself a cup of water.

After a moment of silence, he gulped down a mouthful of water as he gradually recovered his ability to think.

Zaratul really went mad... What exactly did he encounter, or what happened to him...

Bizarro Sorcerer. Sequence 4 is called Bizarro Sorcerer. Does the Seer pathway chiefly revolve around trickery, cunning, pranks, and the paranormal? Or can it simply be summed up as bizarre?

Yes, Clown, Magician, Faceless, and Marionettist do give me that impression. Seer seems to be an exception, but in the eyes of others, a charlatan's behavior can sometimes appear rather strange and terrifying... Is that why Zaratul said fate isn't this pathway's main domain?

Also, it's clear that Beyonders of this pathway are more inclined to be spellcasters.

According to Will Auceptin, there were three ways to obtain the Bizarro Sorcerer potion formula. First, seek out the Secret Order and find the mad Zaratul. Second, travel to the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range and find the treasures left by the Antigonus family. Third, obtain it from the Church. For example, the Antigonus family's notebook might contain the corresponding formula.

But each of the three options was more dangerous than the last. According to Emperor Roselle, Zaratul had been a Sequence 2 Miracle Invoker--a true angel--long ago. Later, he had even advanced to Sequence 1, making him equal to or only slightly weaker than a King of Angels. The mad Zaratul--no, "He" might have lost "His" reason, but that would likely make "Him" even harder to deal with. At the very least, I have no chance of persuading or deceiving "Him." Even with Mr. Azik's help, I couldn't match Zaratul in a contest of pure strength.

Heh heh, unless I wait for Will Auceptin to be born, but if "He" were to involve "Himself" in this matter, there's a small chance of him incurring the attention of Angel of Fate Ouroboros.

As for the treasure on the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range, the resounding ravings, the Antigonus family's arrangements, and the rumors of the Nation of the Evernight buried in the depths of mystery all tell me this is no simple matter. I suspect it's a trap.

I don't even need to consider the Church of the Goddess. Leaving aside the angels presiding over the Holy Cathedral and its collection of Grade 0 Sealed Artifacts, the Backlund diocese alone, where the Antigonus family's notebook is kept, has a terrifying demigod...

Klein couldn't help but recall Mr. A being erased like a pencil drawing. The person responsible was likely a high-ranking member of the Church of the Evernight Goddess!

A beautiful lady whose eyes lacked spirituality... She even smiled at me. I have no idea what that meant... Klein shook his head resignedly, believing that only one course of action remained open to him.

That was to find a demigod from the Secret Order who was still considered normal.

Compared with the mad Zaratul, such a person could at least be reasoned with, perhaps even bargained with. Klein couldn't manage it alone, but for a price, he could seek Mr. Azik's or Queen Mystic Bernadette's help.

I can only consider this for now... Klein rapidly turned his thoughts back on how to help Anderson Hood rid the bad luck curse.

It's been more than two months. I wonder if Fate Councilor Ricciardo has left Oravi Island. Sigh, he hasn't summoned my messenger all this time to inform me of clues to the mystical item I seek. However, it's not a big problem. Bellman Carnot definitely hasn't left his post. I can contact Councilor Ricciardo through him.

That works as well. The Life School of Thought still hasn't completed my request, meaning they haven't made their final payment. They can settle it by improving Anderson's luck, and I can collect payment from Anderson in turn.

Heh heh, when it comes to mystical items or Sealed Artifacts with immense offensive power, doesn't Anderson's sword qualify? Judging by the traits displayed by a Reaper, I can forget about the item that would form after his death... Heh, I'm no greedy demon. I'll definitely pay extra to tide him over.

Klein wiped away his mischievous thoughts and took out the paper crane from his wallet. After unfolding it, he carefully erased the pencil marks on it.

"It's really about to tear. I can use it another two times at best..." he muttered sadly. Then, he folded the paper crane and returned to his bed to continue sleeping. As for using the radio transceiver to contact Arrodes, he planned on doing so after leaving this pirates' playground.

After daybreak, Klein languidly got up and slowly washed up, believing that this was how life should be.

Thump! Thump! Thump!

The knocking on the door interrupted his state of wistfulness.

Without his acumen for danger, his spiritual intuition told him that it was Anderson Hood.

As expected of the Strongest Hunter. He actually survived this long... Klein clicked his tongue inwardly and composed his expression before opening the door.

Anderson was wearing a deer-hunting cap of unknown origin. He grinned as he handed him a Loen gold coin.

"What I owe you from yesterday."

Klein received the gold coin and measured its weight in his hand.

"There's an answer to your problem."

Anderson's eyes lit up.

"What's the solution?

"Don't tell me that the answer is that there are no solutions..."

Am I that kind of person? I'd simply say it's hopeless, so wait for death. Goodbye! Klein retorted inwardly as he swept Anderson with an indifferent gaze.

"A demigod skilled at altering luck is staying on Oravi Island. He owes me a favor."

"Excellent!" Anderson didn't hide his joy. "So, what sort of payment must I make?"

Very sensible of you... Klein deliberately fell silent for two seconds before saying, "I need a mystical item with powerful offensive abilities. Do you have any clues?

"If the value exceeds that of the luck enhancement ritual, I will pay the difference."

Anderson's brow furrowed little by little before smoothing out. He smiled. "I have a mystical item that meets your requirements. It possesses a Beyonder power capable of inflicting lethal damage. Its negative effects aren't bad either: it eats, sleeps, and suffers from bad luck, easily attracting monsters and enemies. Occasionally, it talks too much and can be rather annoying. Haha, it's a joke.

"To be frank, my Death Knell is the mystical item you need, but it's the only weapon I have left. Eh... I do have a relevant lead. It's a rather special revolver whose shots have the effects of 'Weakness Attack,' 'Lethal Attack,' and 'Slaughter.' Furthermore, it can use bullets with different properties. Its negative effect is that after every use, the wielder develops a weakness they didn't previously have, such as fear of light, ships, or dogs. The weakness lasts for six hours.

"Simply carrying it has almost no negative effects. It only makes you prone to thirst, which is entirely tolerable. If the revolver's abilities didn't overlap with my own and those of my mystical items, I would've bought it back then. The seller is asking 9,000 pounds!

"So I'll pay 1,500 pounds and give you the lead on the revolver. How about it?"

Sounds very suitable. Besides, it fits my combat habits... Klein didn't directly agree as he asked in return, "1,500 pounds?"

"Haha, I found more than ten pirates yesterday. They were all very kind, contributing all their wallets to me. Otherwise, they would lend me their characteristics and heads. In just one night, I received 1,600 pounds. Seriously, I love this pirates' paradise!" Anderson said with a beaming smile. "I have to keep 100 pounds for myself for the ship tickets to return to the Fog Sea. So all I can do is pay you 1,500 pounds."

Earning 1,600 pounds a night? Not only are there many pirates in Toscarter, but they are either worth a lot or have plenty of money? Klein suddenly had thoughts about staying a few more nights in the port city.

But considering that Anderson had already cleaned out most of the easy-to-find targets whose rewards could be readily collected, those remaining would certainly be troublesome. His spirits sank again as he asked coldly, "Aren't you afraid of retaliation after doing something like that in a pirates' playground?"

"What's there to worry about? I'm not afraid, even if they're subordinates of a pirate admiral. Heh heh, I believe you're the same. If they're men of the Four Kings, that's not a big problem either. We're about to leave, and the propagation of information takes time. By the time they come, I'd have changed ships and identities several times!" Anderson said without much concern.

Why are you cursing yourself again... Klein silently swept him a look of pity.

"Deal."

"Haha, here's 300 pounds. You'll have to wait for the remaining 1,200 until the bounty rewards and payment for the characteristics arrive. Don't worry, they'll definitely come today. It isn't much." Anderson took out a thick wallet stuffed with soli notes and handed them to Klein.

In consideration of his persona, Klein only did a simple count before stuffing the cash into his wallet and pockets. He said without emotion, "Buy two tickets to Oravi for tomorrow."

He didn't specifically remind Anderson to buy them under a different identity, as he believed the hunter before him was mature and experienced.

If he lacked experience or strength, the way he did things would've gotten him buried at sea long ago... Klein couldn't help but scoff inwardly.

"All right." Anderson pointed downstairs. "Breakfast together? My treat."

Klein nodded without refusing him.

Once they arrived downstairs, the two sat at a table near the window. Midway, a waiter brought white porcelain cups and teaspoons over.

Just as they began talking, the waiter's gaze suddenly went unfocused. Without warning, he picked up a teaspoon and stabbed it at Anderson's throat.

Although Anderson was surprised, his reaction was anything but slow. He immediately leaned backward and dodged the sudden strike.

Bang!

Not far away, the hotel's owner suddenly fired at Anderson as he dodged.

"W-what am I doing..." After the gunshot, the owner muttered, looking both alarmed and bewildered.

Chapter 677: Falling to the Ground

At the sound of the gunshot, Anderson abruptly collapsed to the floor, dodging the bullet in a comical fashion.

As for Klein, who didn't fully understand the situation, he jumped to the side and activated his Spirit Vision while pulling out his revolver.

At that instant, his first reaction was that Anderson had gone overboard in his hunting last night and was now the target of revenge. He only wished to shout, "I don't know him! It has nothing to do with me!"

At the table beside Anderson Hood, a stout man in a shirt with rolled-up sleeves threw aside his fork and knife. He suddenly drew an already-loaded double-barreled hunting rifle, aimed down from above, and pulled the trigger.

This series of actions began almost simultaneously with the hotel owner's, but the additional steps left the man a moment slower.

Bang!

The scattershot sprayed countless tiny pellets, riddling the floor with holes. Although Anderson dodged in time and avoided most of the blast, some pellets still struck him, immediately mangling his side.

Just as Klein was about to kill the stout man with the double-barreled hunting rifle and free Anderson Hood from danger, he realized that the man's expression had suddenly gone blank, just like the hotel owner's. Alarm and horror then filled his face, as though he had jolted awake from a stupor.

That's not right. They're not the true assailants... Klein rationally stopped attempting to pull the trigger. He quickly swept his gaze across the restaurant.

Discovering nothing with his Spirit Vision, he tapped his left thumb on the first segment of his index finger twice, activating his vision of Spirit Body Threads.

By then, all the ladies and gentlemen in the restaurant had risen in panic at the sudden gunfire and were rushing toward the exit.

As she passed the tumbling Anderson, a pretty, elegantly dressed lady suddenly stopped. She uncorked the dark glass bottle in her hand and poured its contents over the Strongest Hunter.

Sizzle!

Anything the liquid splashed rapidly blackened under its intense corrosion. Anderson covered his face and leaped up, dodging yet another attack.

Immediately following that, a gentle and cute lady, a gentleman with newspapers, a red-vested waiter, and a five-year-old child whose hands were stained with sweets attacked Anderson Hood in all kinds of manners.

Flour, lit matches, fruit knives, boiling coffee, and high-proof liquor inundated him. Almost everyone in the hotel restaurant seemed to have one goal--to kill Anderson Hood!

In this extraordinarily dangerous situation, despite no Beyonder powers being involved and no clear route of escape, Anderson employed a flurry of maneuvers--leaping, sending tables flying, and igniting objects ahead of time. He narrowly protected his vital points and avoided serious injury.

Meanwhile, Klein also noticed something abnormal.

Thin, illusory black Spirit Body Threads clearly extended from a corner of the restaurant concealed by a decorative cabinet, yet that area remained silent.

In this chaotic and panic-stricken restaurant, it appeared especially abnormal!

The true assailant who orchestrated this "murder by passersby" is sitting there? Judging by the confusion, bewilderment, horror, and panic the hotel owner, waiters, and guests showed after attacking Anderson, they haven't become marionettes. This is another form of control... An illusion, an emotional seed planted by a Desire Apostle, or some kind of psychological influence? An idea flashed through Klein's mind. He immediately took two steps forward and sent a customer with butter still clinging to the corner of his mouth flying, opening a path for Anderson Hood.

The Strongest Hunter immediately somersaulted out of the encirclement via the path that was opened up, and he ran up to the hotel's second floor. Then, with his back to the wall on the corner of the staircase, he gasped for air.

"Have my powers of provocation reached this level? Even ordinary residents I've never met want to kill me and have actually taken up arms? Man..." As he spoke, Anderson tugged at his right side and nearly cried out in pain.

No, no, no. The truth is that someone plagued by bad luck shouldn't do things like hunt pirates... Klein had abandoned his attempt to approach and control the target through their Spirit Body Threads because a possibility had occurred to him.

The passersby had been implanted with psychological cues or manipulated on the level of the psyche. Only that would make them attack Anderson in such an orderly fashion. This didn't match a Desire Apostle's Beyonder powers, as the manipulated passersby attacked with purpose and precision without displaying any observable signs of preparation. And Klein had previously heard the name of a Sequence 4 potion--Manipulator!

In addition, according to the Psychiatrist released from Creeping Hunger, Klein always suspected that Toscarter Island had a mission or figure related to the Psychology Alchemists.

In addition to the psychological cue and the control on the left side of the mind, it was rather similar to that of the Spectator pathway. Klein had long believed that a Manipulator very likely belonged to the Spectator or dragon pathway; thus, making the outline of the matter relatively clear.

The Psychology Alchemists truly do have an important base on Toscarter Island. They've even sent a Sequence 4 demigod to guard it. This demigod influences certain pirates, making them unknowingly do his bidding, but unfortunately for him, those pirates became Anderson Hood's sponsors last night. Hence, their true backer came knocking!

Keeping his expression in check, Klein looked at Anderson and said indifferently, "It's likely that one or more of the pirates from last night is involved with a hidden demigod on this island.

"Do you think something like this could be done by a Mid-Sequence Beyonder?"

"I can't be that unlucky, can I..." Anderson's voice dwindled to a mutter. "Indeed, those people were being controlled and are innocent. Thankfully, I didn't fight back, or I'd become the prime suspect in a shocking murder case and have a bounty placed on my head! Then I'd be in trouble and have no choice but to become a pirate."

The corners of Klein's mouth twitched slightly.

"If the ones being controlled had been pirates with Beyonder powers, Mandated Punishers, or priests of the Church of Storms, what would have happened?"

"I would already be dead." Anderson threw up his hands as he said in realization, "You mean he doesn't want me dead and only wants to give me a warning?"

Klein nodded gravely and said, "So you still have a chance.

"Yes, to apologize."

Go see what that demigod is up to.

"Apologize?" Anderson's face immediately scrunched up. Deeply conflicted, he forced out, "I have quite a reputation in the Fog Sea."

Klein didn't say a thing. He stood up, patted his coat, and prepared to walk off.

At that moment, Anderson lunged forward and rushed to the top of the stairs ahead of Klein. He yelled, "I'm sorry! It was my fault!

"We can talk about anything!"

He paused for a second and repeated, "I'm sorry! It was my fault!

"We can talk about anything!"

Clap! Clap! Clap! A series of slow claps sounded on the first floor as a figure appeared at the staircase.

Amid the light footsteps, the figure slowly walked to the bend, but Klein instinctively moved his gaze away as though he didn't wish to know what that person looked like.

In addition, he realized that he had no intention of raising his arms or aiming his gun. It was as though he had been cued and had lost the will to resist.

This is terrifying... Yes, it's not a face-to-face hypnosis, which is why I could detect it. But if I was directly targeted, the repercussions would be unimaginable... I can be influenced now. In the way Miss Justice would explain it, the other party will use the sea of collective subconscious to silently arrive beside my island of consciousness, and then they would do something to a certain extent? Klein came to a realization as he had the sudden urge to leave and return to his room.

This is the silent "instruction" given to me by the demigod? Klein roughly understood that the other party wished to communicate with Anderson in private; hence, he didn't resist and walked to the stairs before returning to his room.

In less than five minutes, Anderson knocked on his door with a pained expression.

"Done talking?" Klein asked without much surprise.

Anderson gave a heavy nod.

"Phew... Yes, he asked me to do something for him. As for what it is, I can't tell anyone."

"Do you still remember what he looks like?" Klein deliberately asked.

Anderson thought carefully as he suddenly frowned.

"I don't remember..."

As expected... Look at you. You offended a demigod for 1,600 pounds. It wasn't worth it at all... Klein sighed inwardly, then said, "Can we leave tomorrow?"

"Yes. That matter isn't urgent. Let's resolve the problem with my bad luck first," Anderson answered without hesitation.

Klein didn't speak further as he pointed at the floor.

"Still having breakfast?"

Anderson was taken aback at first, but then smiled.

"Of course!

"No type of frustration can influence eating and sleeping!"

The two went down to the first floor and discovered that the waiter was silently clearing up the shattered items. The boss and the customers had all forgotten what had happened.

After breakfast, Anderson went out again, busy collecting bounty rewards and payment for Beyonder characteristics. He also made certain preparations, while Klein remained in his hotel room, reining in the spirituality overflowing after his advancement and using worms to test his Marionettist powers.

At half past two in the afternoon, he went above the gray fog ahead of time and began rehearsing for the upcoming Tarot Gathering. After all, Mr. Fool had to punish The Hermit Cattleya with casual ease.

Seated in The Fool's chair, Klein rehearsed the three punishment plans he had devised over the past few days. Two required him to borrow some of this space's power, and two required props. Therefore, he needed to settle the entire process in advance and familiarize himself with it so that he wouldn't appear unpracticed at the critical moment.

After an unknown period of time, Klein exhaled and confirmed his plan.

Then, he conjured The World. Without any items, he saw the countless dense black threads that emanated from his body.

Items conjured with the power of the mysterious space above the gray fog possess a measure of spirituality... So they naturally come with Spirit Body Threads. In the real world, lifeless objects don't have any. With practiced ease, Klein manipulated the black threads and quickly took complete control of The World.

Now, not only could he give The World more intricate expressions and reactions that better resembled a real person's, but he could also make the marionette's spiritual fluctuations more natural. It would no longer appear lifeless!

Apart from that, he was like a player with two accounts, gaining access to The World's vision, hearing, and other senses.

After completing all of this, Klein glanced at his golden pocket watch, sent Little Sun a message, and began to silently count his heartbeats.

Chapter 678: Punishment

On both sides of the ancient, mottled bronze table, blurry figures emerged from the embrace of deep red beams and gradually turned corporeal. The surroundings remained as silent and empty as ever, as though no living creature had set foot there for millions of years.

"Good afternoon, Mr. Fool~" Audrey's brisk and cheerful voice soon resounded through the resplendent palace held up by stone columns.

Klein nodded with a smile, watching as the other members offered their greetings under Miss Justice's lead.

The Hermit Cattleya was noticeably reticent. Audrey could tell that much weighed on her mind.

After the greetings ended and the members took their seats, The Fool Klein glanced at Miss Justice. The Psychiatrist immediately understood his intentions and refrained from raising her hand to speak. He then looked at Cattleya and chuckled indifferently.

"Tell Bernadette that she can use certain items in exchange for some answers."

Bernadette... After hearing this familiar name which was neither Queen Mystic, the owner of Dawn, nor the leader of the Element Dawn, Cattleya realized that Mr. Fool already knew everything. Her cheap tricks were laid bare in front of "Him!"

Her heart sank, and intense fear gripped her. For a moment, she had no idea what attitude to adopt or how to respond.

To her, Mr. Fool's words were clear. Pass on the message, but only that one message; nothing else. She wasn't allowed to provide any other hints!

Cattleya believed she knew what "He" meant between the lines.

Bernadette? That's a common Intisian woman's name. Who could she be? What answers does she wish to obtain in exchange? What relationship does she have with Ma'am Hermit? Did Ma'am Hermit make a private request, and is Mr. Fool's answer "yes"? No, that isn't it. Definitely not. If it were a private and reasonable request, Ma'am Hermit wouldn't have deliberately mentioned it before us. "He" would've answered her prayers directly... Is "He" issuing a warning? A torrent of questions made Audrey forget to observe the other members. Then, with the acuity granted by the Spectator pathway, she grasped Mr. Fool's true intention.

Then, a particular theory occurred to her.

Ma'am Hermit privately hinted at the existence of our Tarot Club to this Ma'am Bernadette in hopes of obtaining certain answers... Mr. Fool is very displeased, so "He" exposed the matter directly and warned this first-time offender?

Seriously, why would you divulge matters of the Gathering to others? I haven't even told Susie! This will bring danger to everyone! Thankfully, Mr. Fool is around!

Audrey nearly puffed out her cheeks, forgetting her decorum and etiquette. For the first time, she realized that not every Tarot Club member shared her sense of belonging or showed the same reverence and faith in Mr. Fool.

The Hanged Man Alger, The Magician Fors, and The Moon Emlyn also had similar questions and theories, but their focus wasn't the same.

As Alger anticipated what Mr. Fool would do, he wondered whom Bernadette--a common Intisian woman's name--represented. He was curious why the reserved Hermit would risk divulging information about the Tarot Club. Fors worried that the Tarot Club's existence might be exposed, while instantly imagining a tale of spies and double agents. Emlyn watched with schadenfreude, believing The Hermit truly foolish.

Heh, even our Sanguine Ancestor treats Mr. Fool as an equal and sent me as an envoy to be nurtured. You aren't even a demigod, yet you tried to play petty tricks under Mr. Fool's gaze? Do you have a death wish? Indeed, I can't understand how short-lived creatures think. Emperor Roselle once said that insects born in summer can never truly know snow... Emlyn leaned back, relaxed, and made no attempt to hide his derisive shake of the head.

The Sun Derrick didn't have many thoughts on the matter; all he felt was that the mood wasn't right. Out of curiosity and puzzlement, he asked, "Mr. Fool, who's Bernadette?"

Good question! I thought Miss Justice would be the one asking. Yes, she seems a little angry, which is why she doesn't wish to speak... Klein silently commended him as he casually answered, "Roselle's eldest daughter;

"Owner of the Dawn;

"The leader of the Element Dawn."

He mentioned all of Bernadette's identities, making her appear without any secrets before all the Tarot Club members.

And the reason why he had used "Owner of the Dawn" in place of Queen Mystic was that Klein didn't believe that The Fool should address Bernadette as a queen.

The owner of the Dawn... Queen Mystic! She's actually Emperor Roselle's eldest daughter! Ha, The Hermit, I can now confirm that you're Admiral of Stars Cattleya. So the rumors that you fell out with Queen Mystic are false... Alger felt a surge of excitement. The restraint and pressure he had endured for the past three months vanished at once.

This made his staid self mock The Hermit inwardly.

Emperor Roselle once said that those who play with fire will ultimately burn themselves. And you, Admiral of Stars, dared to challenge a god's clairvoyance!

At that moment, Alger was rather thankful that, although he had attempted to pull off some cheap tricks and figure out Mr. Fool's identity, goals, and present state, he hadn't involved any outsiders. He hadn't divulged any information, so he hadn't suffered any punishment.

Since he had introduced the Four Kings and the Seven Pirate Admirals, Audrey and company only needed to recall that information to confirm that Bernadette was Queen Mystic, a demigod who reigned supreme over the Five Seas. They were also surprised that Emperor Roselle's eldest daughter was still alive and had become an important figure known throughout the world.

Are the answers Queen Mystic seeks hidden in Roselle's diary? Combining everything that had happened with what had been said, Audrey vaguely guessed Bernadette's goal. She believed the emperor's daughter wished to uncover the truth behind her father's assassination.

By then, Cattleya had regained the ability to think. She turned to face the head of the long bronze table and said without any hope of being excused, "Yes, I made a mistake. I won't defend myself. I was indeed wrong.

"Mr. Fool, no matter how much you punish me or even kill me, I'll accept it."

Hypocrite... If Mr. Fool really wishes to punish you, do you think you have any means of resistance? Mr. Hanged Man scoffed on the opposite side of the table. He could identify the problem with such a simple sentence.

Ma'am Hermit is still a little afraid... Audrey could notice the hidden fear from the minute actions and words Cattleya had used.

To her, people who deliberately emphasized their willingness to be executed were often afraid of dying right there and then.

Fors found her peace from The Fool's calm attitude, believing that there weren't any leaks about the Tarot Club; or that the leaks weren't anything important. Hence, like Emlyn, she waited curiously and expectantly for the punishment Mr. Fool would mete out.

Derrick remained clueless about what had happened, uncertain why Ma'am Hermit would suddenly request to be punished.

At that moment, seeing Mr. Fool's brief silence, Cattleya resisted her unease and lifted her head slightly, attempting to observe the existence behind the thick gray fog without being noticed. She hoped to discern "His" true intentions and give a better response. She didn't wish to make another mistake and provoke "Him," making the situation irredeemable.

A mysterious dark purple gleam suffused her black eyes as her gaze pierced the gray fog and reached The Fool.

Suddenly, Cattleya's eyes heated up as illusory blood bled from them.

A sinister, terrifying, and depraved language, accompanied by an indescribable roar, sounded in her ears. Excruciating pain instantly overwhelmed her senses, and her body began to convulse and tremble uncontrollably.

Her face, hands, and every area not concealed by clothing rapidly split open, revealing flesh and blood. Within them, black worms and white moths writhed, forming all manner of indescribable eyes.

Cattleya's cries and painful grunts resounded above the gray fog, causing Alger, Emlyn, Fors, and company to exchange looks as though they could sense the pain she was suffering.

Meanwhile, the blurry figure turned rather clear, allowing them to see The Hermit's mutation.

The disgusting and nasty scene frightened Audrey enough for her to look away. She straightened her back and looked straight ahead without daring to move.

Although the others didn't have such an exaggerated response, they also shared the same behavior.

The True Creator's ravings are indeed useful... Klein, who was shrouded in the thick gray fog, reflected from the bottom of his heart.

He hadn't immediately responded to Cattleya's request for punishment because he wanted to confirm whether there was anything special about her eyes and whether they could see beyond what he allowed!

For this, he had stirred some of the mysterious space's powers and hidden them ahead of time within the gray fog that shrouded him. This would connect anyone who used a Beyonder power to see through the obstacle to Tinder!

This was equivalent to the owner of the Beyonder power directly observing, on a psychic level, an item corrupted by the True Creator. Hence, because Klein wasn't using the gray fog to deliberately suppress the influence, Cattleya naturally heard the True Creator's ravings. The "organ" she used to activate the Beyonder power was severely damaged, and she was then subjected to extreme pain that caused her mutation!

If Admiral of Stars hadn't tried to observe him, Klein's plan had been to make her apologize to every Tarot Club member and let them discuss her punishment democratically.

And regardless of how democratic it was, the major punishment would ultimately be to stir some of the mysterious space's powers to connect Cattleya to Tinder!

After two seconds, Klein knew it was enough. He gently lowered his hand, allowing the gray fog to silently suppress the True Creator's ravings and reverse Cattleya's mutation.

The Admiral of Stars trembled as she visibly calmed down. The cracks in her skin gradually closed as her thoughts returned, allowing her to take in her surroundings once again.

At that moment, Alger said in a deep voice, as though warning himself, "Do not pry into the mysteries of God..."

Chapter 679: Murder Request

"Do not pry into the mysteries of God..."

Alger's whisper soon faded, but it continued to echo in everyone's ears, forcing them to recognize a reality.

Although Mr. Fool seldom put on airs or spoke, and answered nearly every request they made, making "Him" seem warm and approachable, "He" was ultimately a god whose mysteries must not be pried into. "He" was a god who transcended reality!

Audrey, Emlyn, and the other Tarot Club members instinctively accepted Mr. Hanged Man's altered saying, pretending that they had forgotten the original wording, "do not look directly at God." After all, they looked at Mr. Fool from time to time while asking him questions or seeking his advice, and Mr. Fool didn't seem to mind.

Of course, we weren't looking at him directly due to the thick gray fog's obstruction... From Ma'am Hermit's outcome, Mr. Fool had done so for our own good... Audrey slowly exhaled.

At that moment, The Fool Klein thought, Mr. Hanged Man is cooperating wonderfully. I was prepared to control The World and have him say something similar to complete the final step of the punishment...

He had originally felt that making the fake World say something like "do not lie to God" or "do not pry into the mysteries of God" would be rather embarrassing. If people ever learned that the so-called Blessed, the so-called World, was actually The Fool's alternate identity, he would be too ashamed to face anyone. But on second thought, The World had done similar things before, so one more would make no difference. Besides, he simply had to ensure no one ever found out, right?

Having overcome his mental barrier, Klein had planned on acting according to his rehearsals, but to his surprise, reality went better than he had expected. The Hanged Man seemed to be shocked to the bone as a result of The Hermit's punishment. As such, he helped him say the sentence "do not pry into the mystery of God," making the effects even more natural and perfect!

Yes... The Fool only touched briefly on Ma'am Hermit's leak of information about the Tarot Club. "He" warned her through the implications behind "His" words, while her subsequent suffering resulted from prying into the mysteries of a god. It wasn't truly The Fool's intent...

This is the outcome I wanted most. It effectively preserves The Fool's image. After all, a god wouldn't stoop to being petty with mortals; that would only tarnish "His" reputation...

However, the way Ma'am Hermit does things is way too bold. I just realized and confirmed today that she has pried into the mysteries of The Fool more than once. Although it wasn't for any vile motives, it's also something worth punishing her for. Heh, my previous reactions must've made her believe that I had "acquiesced" her "observations," so she made it a habit. In the end, she ended up falling headfirst into my expectations...

In addition, she directly hinted to outsiders without fully grasping Mr. Fool's attitude. Her audacity is truly extraordinary. Clearly, she hasn't suffered enough in the past. Today's lesson should remain firmly engraved in her memory for a very long time...

Thinking back to her state in her dream, all of this seems pretty much the expected outcome... Sparing the rod spoils the child!

Heh heh, as a result, I also managed to awe Mr. Hanged Man and the other members today. Amused, Klein muttered inwardly, surveyed the gathering, and said calmly, "That shall be all."

Hearing this, The Hermit Cattleya, whose Spirit Body had just recovered, relaxed. Intense fatigue and joy surged through her. All she wanted was a reclining chair where she could rest for a while.

The first time is a warning, but the second won't end so pleasantly... The pirate admiral sighed inwardly and warned herself not to attempt any more petty tricks or imagine that her hints could fool Mr. Fool. Nor could she ever again try to pry into "His" secrets!

The pain she received was in no way weaker than the torment of having knowledge injected into her by the Hidden Sage. Therefore, she believed without a doubt that Mr. Fool was essentially a god, a true god, an unfathomable god whose mysteries couldn't be pried into!

Thankfully, Her Majesty now knows where she can trade for the answers she has been desperately seeking all this time... I won't need to hint at anything or tip her off in the future... Cattleya turned her body to the side again, trembling as she looked at the end of the long bronze table. This time, she only dared to look at the end of the table or the armrest. The dark purple hue in her eyes had also turned faint.

Amid the silence, she said sincerely, "I'll remember your leniency in sparing me."

In the gray fog, The Fool Klein nodded gently without repeating his previous words.

After waiting a moment, The Magician Fors straightened her back, looked around, and spoke before Justice Audrey could.

"Everyone, is anyone interested in an assassination mission?

"The target is an important member of a cult."

Grateful for the favors her teacher, Dorian Gray, had given her, Fors had been recently hoping to do something for him.

After some consideration, she set her sights on the Aurora Order Oracle who had inflicted immense harm on her teacher's family--Lewis Wien, who might be a Scribe or a Traveler!

Fors hadn't let Leymano's Travels go to her head to the point that she believed she could kill an experienced Sequence 6 or Sequence 5 Beyonder adept at escaping. She entertained the idea only because she believed the secret organization backing her, the Tarot Club, could provide unimaginable support.

Ma'am Hermit and Mr. World both appear to have the means to fight Lewis Wien. With any of them taking action, and with me using Leymano's Travels to help, it's not impossible for us to succeed... Fors began formulating the most ideal situation.

Of course, she knew her current savings weren't enough to kill a powerful Beyonder like Lewis Wien. After all, her 830 pounds probably couldn't even buy one of his hands. She knew very well that Miss Audrey Hall had once spent more than 10,000 pounds to kill the Intisian ambassador, a Sequence 6 Conspirer. The cost of assassinating Lewis Wien, who was of a similar or even higher Sequence, was obvious!

Fors planned to accept a series of requests from whoever carried out the mission, helping them complete matters they found inconvenient to handle themselves in order to pay off the cost. She believed that after obtaining Leymano's Travels, she was equipped with the abilities to complete certain difficult tasks.

After hearing Miss Magician's request, The Hermit Cattleya, The Hanged Man Alger, and Justice Audrey cast their gazes onto The World. They believed that this gentleman, one who had a unique penchant for hunting Beyonders, possessed the necessary strength.

I'm not in Backlund... However, I can't respond that way. Otherwise, it would reveal to Mr. Hanged Man and Ma'am Hermit that Mr. Fool might have only two or three Blessed... Klein controlled The World, overcoming his emotions as he laughed hoarsely.

"Where? Which cult?

"What's his Sequence? What unique powers does he have?"

Eh, Mr. World is a little different from before... I can't explain it clearly, but it feels like he's suddenly in a better mood. Perhaps he recently encountered something worth celebrating... Audrey suddenly noticed the difference and excitedly imagined what might have happened to Mr. World.

Fors happily replied, "He's an Oracle of the Aurora Order in Backlund. He was formerly a Sequence 6, but he might be a Sequence 5 now. I can't be certain.

"He can record the Beyonder powers that others have used and can use them once. He's good at escaping any form of entrapment, and it's difficult to surround him. Perhaps, he might be able to travel through the spirit world..."

The target is an Oracle from the Aurora Order, a Sequence 6 or 5, with powers seemingly from the Apprentice pathway... Indeed, Miss Magician doesn't appear as simple and ordinary as she seems. My original judgment was right... Cattleya quickly returned to her usual state. An Oracle of the Aurora Order can't possibly be innocent. He's a madman who destroys lives. I won't feel guilty for killing him...

It's not like I haven't offended the Aurora Order more than once or twice...

Sequence 6 or 5; that's something I can handle... I've seen something that matches the trait of recording and releasing Beyonder powers as described by Miss Magician. Mr. A had used it before, but it might not really be it...

It doesn't matter to me whether he's skilled at escaping or traveling through the spirit world. As long as I can get close and successfully control his Spirit Body Threads, he won't be able to flee!

It's hard to tell what will happen in a direct clash. I do have quite a solid chance of success if I sneak an attack in. Of course, succeeding at a sneak attack is a whole other matter...

After some serious consideration, The World looked at The Magician Fors.

"I can consider taking on the job, but not anytime soon. It will be at least two months before I can do so."

He wasn't sure what other mishaps he would encounter at sea, so he gave himself a generous margin of time.

"Two months later..." Fors repeated, visibly torn.

It was too long; besides, she wasn't sure how long Lewis Wien would stay in Backlund.

At that moment, Alger, who had been watching from the side, considered the matter and interjected, "Miss Magician, do you need to kill that Aurora Order Oracle personally?"

"No. As you can see, I'm considering asking Mr. World for help," Fors replied with a smile.

Alger nodded as though in thought.

"No, but I'll investigate," Fors answered frankly.

"Why don't you get The World to do it after your investigations bear fruit?" The Hanged Man pressed.

"Yes, but I haven't decided." Fors was somewhat confused, unaware of why Mr. Hanged Man was asking her all these questions.

"If you can confirm the Aurora Order Oracle's location, why spend a large sum hiring someone to kill him? Wouldn't reporting him directly to the Churches be enough? After the Great Smog, none of the Churches will ignore a related lead."

He wasn't trying to make The World lose any business. Instead, he had clearly read the dilemma inside her, believing that there was a higher possibility of her not going through with the request. After all, too many incidents might happen in two months. Therefore, he gave some suggestions to ensure that this matter reached some preliminary agreement.

Report him to the Churches? This sounds familiar... Klein was surprised, never expecting Mr. Hanged Man to say something like that.

Heh heh, while everyone is being influenced by Mr. Hanged Man, he has also been influenced by us... Klein immediately felt relieved and rather pleased.

"When the time comes, I'll discuss the price with Mr. World."

Chapter 680: New Thoughts

After receiving The World's nod of assent and settling the matter regarding Lewis Wien, Fors thought for a moment and continued, "Everyone, does anyone have a Meteorite Crystal or the crystallized blood of a Lavos Squid, or any information about them?"

These were the main ingredients of the Astrologer potion formula.

Fors had originally planned to continue making requests, hoping that Mr. Moon, who was also in Backlund, could help her find Lewis Wien. After some consideration, however, she decided to try on her own first. Only after confirming that there were no solutions or clues would she request the Tarot Club's help.

Meteorite Crystal? Crystallized blood of a Lavos Squid? That sounds familiar... Is it because I know the Seer potion formula? Yes, it's familiar because the Seer potion's main ingredients are 50 grams of Star Crystal and 10 milliliters of Lavos Squid blood! The Astrologer's main ingredients are like upgraded versions of the Seer's... Indeed, the Apprentice and Seer pathways can be interchanged at the higher Sequences... 600 pounds, a very reasonable price... Pleasantly surprised, Fors replied, "60 grams."

Mr. World suddenly seems so far above me... Fors was similarly surprised.

As expected of Mr. Fool's Blessed... Alger sighed.

Chapter 681: Indirect Answer

Despite finding himself the center of attention, Derrick did not falter. He eagerly said, "I recently joined an exploration team led by the Chief to Afternoon Town.

"It's a key gateway that leads to the Giant King's Court. It's a door that separates myth from reality."

Another King of Angels! And Afternoon Town has such a dark, frightening atmosphere. The ecclesiastic's penitence has a certain, hmm--air of a prophet foretelling a calamity... Audrey listened with fascination, her curiosity about the blank line and unspeakable name piqued.

Thankfully, a Magician never performed unprepared. After that day, Klein had naturally begun to seriously consider how to answer such questions. Now, projecting great confidence, he rested his right palm on the armrest and said with a profound, meaningful glint in his eyes:

"It's because it's concealed."

He used his gaze and body language to hint to the Tarot Club members that "concealment" wasn't to be taken at face value and had a deeper, more substantial meaning. As for what it was, they had to figure it out themselves. Deities had compelling reasons to conceal certain matters.

After this performance, Klein couldn't help but feel guilty. He felt that his air of charlatanry was growing stronger. At the same time, he regretted that The Hermit's prying into his secrets had resulted from his own enticements. Mr. Fool would use "His" gaze and body language to provide additional hints; thus, all the members would subconsciously observe "His" attitude.

That's because I have no other choice. Without doing this, how am I supposed to continue the act... It's not like I'm a real evil god! Klein sighed silently.

Secret? The name itself is a secret? Is this what Mr. Fool is pointing to? Hmm, which names are concealed... True gods at Sequence 0? Alger instantly thought of many things, forming a theory by combining them with the matters Little Sun had previously mentioned.

When a name itself becomes concealed, it means that the matter involves a true god. Moreover, the Evernight Goddess is likely involved, because she's the Mother of Concealment! A King of Angels who was tempted, the residents of Afternoon Town falling from grace, a town gradually corrupted, and a black flower of calamity blooming. While the Tarot Club members contemplated, Klein also began analyzing why one name was blank and the other couldn't be spoken.

Could it be the true name of a deity? A true god had tempted Dark Angel Sasrir, and the fourth name representing the King of Angels also became a true god later?

But it's not like I haven't said the true name of a deity before. Primordial Demoness Cheek is understood by many to be on the same level as the seven deities. It's not like anything happened in the end...

Perhaps it has something to do with the language used? The languages of Loen, Intis, and Feysac, and even ancient Feysac do not possess the ability to stir the powers of nature. On the other hand, the commonly used Jotun in the City of Silver can. That ecclesiastic likely used a similar language.

I should later try to pronounce Cheek using Jotun? Then, I'll die on the spot and succeed in courting death... Forget it. Besides, having a blank name and an unspeakable name means different things... I've no idea why.

"The two names might separately represent two deities, so they cannot be said.

This is similar to my guess, but they can't be confirmed... Cattleya did not add anything, as she didn't believe that their theories were facts.

Audrey, Fors, and Emlyn listened attentively as they could not help but feel poignant that the Tarot Club often made things seem exceptionally lofty when discussing such matters. Things like Kings of Angels, evil gods and true gods, or ancient secrets all depended on a single word.

That's what I believe, but the problem is that I'm unable to confirm it... To not search for the sea and switch to exploring the Giant King's Court was likely Shepherd Lovia's suggestion. But it doesn't exist here.

Chapter 682: Seeking "Fool"

Cattleya controlled herself without looking at The World Gehrman. She said in a deep and slow voice, "Northeast from the Gargas Archipelago, there's a safe sea route that allows one to enter those dangerous waters..."

The gray, blurry sky cracked open as he saw a towering mountain tear through the clouds.

At the mountaintop stood a collapsed palace whose walls were covered in weeds and moss and riddled with conspicuous holes.

Inside the palace's hall was a huge throne carved out of stone. It was adorned with dull gemstones and gold. It was mostly mottled and damaged.

It seemed not to have been prepared for a human, for countless translucent maggots were densely clustered upon it. They squirmed slowly and continuously grew.

Around the throne echoed ravings that seemed to penetrate the long river of time and history. They were illusory, ethereal, and unceasing.

It is indeed the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range. Moreover, I can see and hear it more clearly than before...

Is that my fate? It really makes me want to be rebellious and not go... Sigh, I can't deal with absolutes. It depends... Klein sighed and conjured the five bounty notices provided by Emlyn White. Combining the information he had, he used the methods of divination to find the locations of the Primordial Moon believers.

Galis Kevin, Windsor Behring, and Argos are all in Backlund.

Dandy and Lara are at Enmat Harbor and Pritz Harbor respectively.

This is as good as nothing... Klein shook his head and returned to the real world.

Recalling how he had already agreed to provide Little Sun with the Notary potion formula within three days, he wore his coat and a top hat, and he prepared to head out to find a target.

When he opened the door and came to the stairwell, he saw Anderson Hood walking up, twirling his deer-hunting cap with his hand while humming a folk song.

This fellow is really good at recovering from setbacks... He was just taught a lesson by a demigod in the morning and was forced to apologize and agree to a request, but I don't see any sign of trauma from him anymore... It's no wonder he became a Sequence 5 powerhouse. Just this state of mind alone makes it difficult for him to lose control... Klein looked at him, nodding slightly as a greeting.

As he spoke, he took out stacks of cash of different thickness from different pockets.

Anderson immediately laughed.

"That's right. It went far more smoothly than I imagined! Those fellows who ought to have rocks for brains were surprisingly friendly, polite, and efficient! I even wonder whether I've become a Blessed of Lady Luck!"

"There's no such deity." Klein ruthlessly shattered his fantasies.

He was asking about the prey that resulted in the Manipulator's involvement.

Although I appear nonchalant in front of you, I investigate my prey's background and circumstances ahead of time to avoid provoking someone I can't afford to offend. Who could've known... Sigh, I can only blame my bad luck." This fellow is more careful than I thought... That's right, his previous Sequence was called Conspirer... Klein realized before asking indifferently, "Who deserves death the most in this area?"

Anderson was taken aback before he chuckled.

"Does our craziest adventurer plan on beginning his hunting activities?

Don't worry, we're different. I'm a Seer. I have various means to hide my tracks. I will not allow someone to come knocking at my door. Besides, that's a demigod of the Spectator pathway. He's not good at divination or prophecies... Klein maintained his cold attitude that was unique to Gehrman and said, "You don't have to bother."

Anderson immediately raised a thumb.

"Your madness is worthy of praise!"

"He has committed many crimes, killed plenty of people, and basically did it with the help of pirates. On the surface, there's no apparent problem.

"Heh heh, he isn't a Beyonder, but the difficulty in killing him is how troublesome it is, yes--troublesome!

"He has three to five Beyonders from different pirate crews providing him with protection. On the rooftops, outside the building, beneath the windows, everyone there belongs to him. To finish him off, the only way is to forcefully storm in and kill a large number of people.

"I can do it, but it's too troublesome. There's also some degree of danger. You'll become a wanted criminal after that, so I didn't deal with him and only dealt with his safe at home."

Safe... To talk about banditry in such a fresh way... Yes, I previously heard that Toscarter's main industries are its plantation economy and pirates' black market trading. It also has flourishing bar, brothel, and gambling industries. I never expected that it includes new drugs... Molsona is an ordinary person and not a Beyonder... Perfect, Creeping Hunger happens to lack food... Klein nodded and indicated for Anderson to elaborate.

That evening, at the Oaktree Bar.

A boxing match was about to begin in the boxing ring. Many drinkers surrounded it with mugs in hand.

They were like sharks who had caught the scent of blood. As they placed their bets, they loudly yelled words such as "kill him"!

This was a business of the head of the Loen's New Party, Molsona. The unique thing about the boxing matches held here was that death was permitted!

Molsona greatly enjoyed matches that resembled ancient wrestling competitions. He often came to watch a few matches. At that moment, he was seated on the second story, overlooking the ring.

Around him were several bodyguards. They were watching every direction, and among them were Beyonders sent from the pirates he worked with, as well as experienced adventurers he hired with large sums of money.

These people surrounded Molsona with their backs or sides to him, preventing anyone from approaching. Revolvers, rifles, and hunting rifles all pointed outward, intimidating the crowd.

After confirming the situation, Klein pressed down his top hat, entered the bar, and saw Molsona sucking on a cigar.

This mob boss had a highly distinctive face. His brandy nose and thin eyebrows were both unmistakable.

Klein looked away and first went to the bar to get a cup of local malt beer worth 4 pence. Then, he made his way to the railing beneath the second floor.

Although he wasn't directly under Molsona, as it was heavily guarded, he was already quite close to him.

I'm within five meters... Klein silently muttered to himself, raised his cup of beer, and looked at the boxing ring.

Chapter 683: So You Are Here as Well

Black Spirit Body Threads that came from different lifeforms appeared in Klein's vision, but he didn't immediately extend his spirituality to attempt to control them.

After distinguishing and confirming which Spirit Body Threads belonged to Molsona, he gulped a mouthful of malt beer as he began to focus on the boxing match in the ring, just like a real member of the audience.

The two boxers were bare-chested and wore no protective gear. They fought without restraint, constantly clashing as fist met flesh, and the atmosphere quickly reached a fever pitch.

Many of the alcoholic gamblers had adrenaline pumping through them as they shouted for the boxer they supported passionately, yelling, "Kill him!"

"Finish that son of a b*tch!"

On the second floor, Molsona also forgot the cigar in his hand as he was fixated on the ring below, his hands clenched tightly into fists.

Apart from those who had to have their backs facing him because they were keeping tabs on any suspicious people or watching the important regions such as the rooftop or area beneath, the people around him could not help but become absorbed in the intense boxing match.

Klein raised his hand again, gulping a mouthful of beer as if he were out of breath from the tense atmosphere.

At that moment, his spirituality silently extended and grabbed onto the illusory black threads corresponding to Molsona.

One second, two seconds, three seconds... The brandy-nosed Molsona was just about to pump his fist a little as if he were in the ring himself when he suddenly found his brain go numb.

He felt the surroundings abruptly turn odd, as if several panes of glass were in between him and them.

Molsona immediately realized that his thoughts had noticeably slowed, as if every part of his brain had suddenly rusted.

As the target was only an ordinary person whose Spirit Body was far inferior to that of a Beyonder, Klein needed less than twenty seconds to achieve initial control over him.

Seven seconds!

It took only seven seconds!

Oh no... Something wrong is happening... It's likely... a Beyonder... with relatively... special powers... Molsona, who often interacted with pirates, was no stranger to the mysterious world. That was why he spent large sums of money to hire Beyonders to protect himself. If he hadn't long destroyed his body with sex and alcohol, making his psyche relatively weak and his condition terrible, giving him a high chance of losing control if he were to consume a potion, he would've also wanted to obtain supernatural powers himself.

At that moment, as his thoughts were slowing down and due to his lack of experience, Molsona needed more than ten seconds to figure out that he was under assault. He immediately reached out his arm and was about to shout for help.

Yet his movements were agonizingly slow, and his voice was weak. A number of the bodyguards around him were engrossed in the exciting, nerve-racking boxing match; the audience's roars rose in an ever-louder crescendo; and all the guards along the perimeter had focused their attention on possible avenues of attack to protect their employer. Consequently, his obvious abnormality went unnoticed.

When the climax of the match received a temporary respite, a number of bodyguards and subordinates turned to look back at their boss, and they saw that his eyes looked somewhat dazed. His hands didn't seem to be in the right place as if he was still caught up in the match, anxiously awaiting the final results.

The corners of the mob boss's eyes had tears welling up as he tried hard to release his fingers to drop the cigar to attract the attention of his subordinates, but he discovered in despair that his line of thought was becoming highly impeded and stiff. Even a simple action needed more than a minute to complete, and his fingers were even resisting his will!

Pa!

The burning cigar finally fell to the ground as Molsona's tears flowed down to his neck across his cheeks.

A few bodyguards discovered this and were just about to ask their boss if it was because the match was too exciting when Molsona suddenly bent down. As he wiped his face, he picked up the cigar.

"That was an excellent match! Give the victor more money!" Molsona flicked the cigar and pulled up his collar as he grinned happily.

He didn't say exactly how much was to be given, as Klein didn't know the market rates. All he could do was give a vague comment.

Yes, Molsona of the Loen's New Party had already become his marionette!

As this mob boss was only an ordinary person, and his Spirit Body was even weaker than most healthy humans, it took only two minutes and fifteen seconds!

If the time needed was any longer, he would've had to divert his attention to create an illusion to create some chaos, making the bodyguards place their attention on protecting Molsona and not being able to discover his abnormality in time.

"Kill him!"

"Kill him!"

The shouts of the audience suddenly merged into a single chant as the match in the ring came to its end. Molsona also allowed his bodyguards to continue watching the match.

When a boxer collapsed to the ground unconscious, Molsona took a puff of his cigar and said, "To the lounge.

"I want to take a break."

"Yes, Boss." His bodyguards and subordinates immediately surrounded him, escorted him to the corridor on the second floor, and helped him open the door to the lounge.

After instructing his guards to guard different critical spots and to not disturb him, Molsona paced around and opened a safe. He found documents involving various new drugs and selected the most important ones.

Afterward, he placed the documents and an address he cut from the newspapers, as well as a total of 758 pounds in cash into a briefcase.

With a creak, he opened the door and called for a subordinate.

"Throw this bag under the third street lamp around the alley."

"Yes, Boss." The subordinate asked no questions.

This was a rule!

After closing the door again, Molsona found three candles and items containing spirituality, then carefully used pen and paper to draw the symbol corresponding to The Fool--a half Pupil-less Eye representing concealment and Contorted Lines representing change.

Then, this mob boss who had become a marionette lit the candles, used cologne to represent essential oil and extract, and solemnly held a bestowment ritual.

He softly chanted The Fool's honorific name, and he used ancient Hermes, which he originally didn't know, to recite the corresponding incantation. Afterward, he picked up the item with spirituality and let it fuse into the wind, constructing an illusory door with the transformed candlelight. If he couldn't find any items with spirituality, Klein had planned on using Molsona's blood. A human's blood was an item with spirituality to begin with!

In the washroom on the first floor, Klein seized the opportunity to take four steps counterclockwise and go above the gray fog.

He didn't use the Black Emperor card. Instead, he directly stirred some of the mysterious space's power, combined it with the paper figurine, and threw it through the bestowment door.

The pitch-blackness immediately transformed into an angel with twelve pairs of wings. It flew through the illusory, mysterious door, passed through the pitch-black depths of the void, and arrived beside Molsona.

This would disrupt any subsequent investigations via divination, prophecy, or other Beyonder powers!

Immediately afterward, Klein picked up Creeping Hunger and threw it into the ritual's door!

Through the bestowment ritual, Creeping Hunger entered the real world and appeared in front of Molsona. Having gone without food for a long time, it immediately became restless.

At that moment, Klein, who had returned to the washroom, controlled the frozen Molsona from a distance of dozens of meters away to immediately close his mouth and pick up the glove on the altar.

A crack opened in the middle of the glove as two rows of illusory, white, and eerie teeth were revealed!

The senses he received from the marionette rapidly weakened, and he decisively severed his control.

The slight backlash made his mind spin, but it didn't take long for him to recover.

Then, as if nothing had happened, he left the washroom, returned to the bar counter, and continued drinking the malt beer he didn't finish earlier.

At the same time, he found a rat on the second floor through the use of Spirit Body Threads, and he made it become his marionette in less than two minutes.

Moving with awkward, unfamiliar motions, the rat began searching for a tunnel or path. After quite some time, it entered Molsona's lounge through a hole hidden behind a bookshelf.

At that moment, a thin glove resembling human skin lay silently on the ground. Nothing remained of Molsona, not even his clothes.
