"Ahem..." Anderson finally recovered as he stood straight. He gave Gehrman Sparrow an astonished look. "Your messenger... Your messenger is a demigod!?"

Chapter 691: Meeting

There's no need for you to tell me. I more or less confirmed it moments ago... Upon hearing Anderson Hood's words, Klein maintained a calm expression as he muttered inwardly.

Back when he had encountered Reinette Tinekerr in the spirit world and seen her enormous true form and Gothic-style castle, Klein had already believed that she wasn't someone to be trifled with. And after Miss Messenger had nearly killed the Strongest Hunter with such ease, he had no doubt that she was a demigod--a Sequence 4 at the very least!

A demigod is willing to deliver letters for me at one gold coin per trip? Obviously, things aren't that simple. Miss Messenger must have her own motives. Of course, I can't discount the fact that I'm always encountering all sorts of strange matters. They might have piqued her interest, and since she happens to be free, she doesn't mind acting as a messenger...

Similar cases include Arrodes's fawning and the friendliness of Will Auceptin, the Snake of Mercury... Regardless, I should remain wary and not trust her completely... Until I have a chance to discuss such matters, I shouldn't consider blowing the harmonica whenever I'm in danger. Who knows whether Miss Messenger might simply tear me to shreds... Countless thoughts flashed through Klein's mind, but he continued to wear a stoic expression. Faced with the alarmed Anderson, he merely nodded slightly.

"It's none of your business."

...This fellow really is mysterious! A demigod-level spirit world creature is his messenger! On top of that, he casually knows a demigod skilled at improving a person's luck... No wonder he's so calm and composed after offending the King of Immortality... No wonder the King of Immortality didn't dare seek revenge or even show himself! Anderson suddenly came to a realization and couldn't help carefully sizing up Gehrman Sparrow.

"Oh?" Klein swept an emotionless gaze over the Strongest Hunter.

Anderson hurriedly looked away and chuckled dryly.

"I realized that you're particularly suited to being the subject of a portrait--the kind with a dark, gloomy background. It would really accentuate your bearing.

"How about it? Would you consider it? I can paint your portrait. Believe me, I'm a master at this!"

Klein couldn't be bothered to listen to his nonsense. He took out his gold pocket watch and flipped it open.

"Return to your room. I'll come for you in five minutes."

"All right," Anderson replied with a beaming smile.

After the Strongest Hunter left, Klein took out Azik's copper whistle and Will Auceptin's paper crane, then entered the bathroom and set up a ritual.

After bringing Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina's pearl earring above the gray fog, Klein sat at the head of the long bronze table. There, he conjured a pen and paper and wrote a simple divination statement: "Edwina Edwards's location."

Holding the paper and the earring, Klein leaned back in his chair and recited the divination statement as he entered a dream through Cogitation.

At first, a gray world filled his vision. Then, plains blanketed in ice and snow appeared before his eyes.

A howling blizzard enveloped everything, and the entire area was shrouded in thick fog. It didn't seem to correspond to any real place.

Klein quickly saw Edwina. Her long brown hair was tied simply behind her head, while the loose ends whipped wildly in the blizzard.

Dressed in dark trousers and a fitted white shirt with elaborate floral patterns around the collar and sleeves, she appeared frail in such an environment.

Her leather-booted feet moved constantly through the snow, leaving a trail of distinct footprints that the violent blizzard swiftly erased.

The scene shattered, and Klein opened his eyes. He realized that he couldn't determine Vice Admiral Iceberg's actual location from the revelation provided by the divination.

The polar regions? Feysac's Plains of Evernight? There's no way to tell. Apart from the blizzard, there aren't any distinguishing features... Klein sat up and put down the pearl earring and the paper bearing the divination statement.

After pondering for a few seconds, he confirmed another fact--Edwina Edwards had truly vanished. She wasn't aboard the Golden Dream. This also ruled out the possibility of a trap.

Klein cautiously performed several more divinations on the matter and received the result that there were no traps aboard the Golden Dream.

After some thought, he left the mysterious space above the gray fog and, through the usual series of procedures, brought the pearl earring back to the real world.

Recalling the map of the waters surrounding Oravi Island and the Golden Dream's present location, Klein selected an uninhabited island where fishermen sometimes took shelter from storms. In his letter, he instructed Danitz and the others to sail to a point nearby.

After folding the letter, he blew the harmonica and once again saw Miss Messenger appear, carrying her four heads.

As he handed her the reply, Klein coughed lightly and asked, "Can you confirm Danitz's location?"

One of the heads in Reinette Tinekerr's hand nodded before the others spoke in turn.

"Yes..." "As long as..." "He isn't beyond my range..."

Seeing Miss Messenger continue to float there with no sign of leaving, he averted his gaze and said, "Danitz will pay the gold coin."

"All right..." Reinette Tinekerr's figure quickly faded away.

Phew. Klein exhaled. After making meticulous preparations and cleaning up the scene, he picked up the suitcase he had finished packing and left the room. He then knocked on Anderson Hood's door.

"We'll go somewhere else before heading to Bayam," he calmly informed the Strongest Hunter. "You can either wait for me in Bayam or come with me."

Anderson gave a cheeky smile and said, "I can feel my adventurer's blood burning. I'm very curious about the matter that messenger brought.

"I thought I had no way to learn more, but to my surprise, you actually invited me!"

I didn't. I'm only giving you two choices... Klein coldly turned around and walked toward the stairs. Anderson hurriedly picked up his newly purchased suitcase and followed.

After leaving the hotel, Klein took a carriage out of the port city, then walked to the edge of an uninhabited cliff on Mount Saint Draco.

Looking at the waves crashing against the cliff below, Anderson glanced around in surprise.

"This is our destination?"

Klein ignored the question. He took out a tin charm and softly chanted the incantation, "Storm."

He infused it with spirituality and split the charm's power in two, using half on himself and the other half to augment Anderson. He then tossed the spent charm off the cliff.

"Underwater breathing, deep-sea membrane..." Anderson identified the additional supernatural effects on him in surprise.

At that moment, a loud splash came from beneath the cliff. A massive creature resembling a whale surfaced from the sea.

It was entirely dark blue. Its mouth opened to reveal white teeth and a blood-red interior.

Klein walked to the edge of the cliff and jumped straight down. With the charm's power, he landed gently inside the undersea behemoth's mouth.

Anderson stared blankly for a moment before jumping down in excitement and landing beside Klein.

The massive undersea creature closed its mouth, plunging the interior into pitch darkness.

Then, it submerged and began swimming toward its designated destination.

Inside its mouth, lightning flashed in Klein's eyes, allowing him to see through the darkness. He found a tooth to lean against and casually sat down.

Protected by the deep-sea membrane, he wasn't worried about dirtying his clothes.

"Man, this is quite fascinating..." Anderson surveyed the area and asked curiously, "How did you come up with this? How are you doing it?"

Of course, I prayed to myself, then used the Sea God Scepter to draw over a suitable undersea creature from nearby... Klein didn't answer Anderson's question. He half-closed his eyes as though resting.

"It's just a little stuffy..." Anderson took a cigar case and a match from his pocket. "Can I smoke in here?"

"Ask it." Klein didn't open his eyes.

Anderson chuckled dryly before putting away the cigar and match.

"I don't think it'll like the smell of tobacco."

In the dark sea, the massive creature swam swiftly, periodically using its specialized organs to efficiently extract oxygen from the water.

After an unknown period, it surfaced in front of an uninhabited island.

After using the charm's power to make his way ashore, Klein removed his hat and bowed to the undersea creature.

"You're very polite..." Anderson remarked in amusement upon seeing this.

"I've always been very polite, even when facing prey." Klein shot him a glance as though including him in that category.

Anderson chuckled dryly and pointed toward the other side of the island.

"There's a ship over there.

"Oh, it's the Golden Dream!"

Klein had also spotted the sailboat, scrubbed spotlessly clean. On the vessel, which stretched dozens of meters, a gleaming main cannon that looked entirely out of place swirled with overlapping symbols.

Carrying his suitcase, he immediately headed over and soon reached the place where the Golden Dream lay at anchor.

Then, he saw Danitz leap from the deck and run across the water.

Just as Danitz arrived before Gehrman Sparrow and began deliberating over what to say, he suddenly noticed a familiar figure.

"Anderson Hood!" He pointed at the Strongest Hunter and shouted.

Anderson immediately burst into laughter.

"You weren't expecting me, were you?"

Although he had no idea why they had encountered the Golden Dream, that didn't stop him from entertaining the idea of seizing the ship by force.

You know each other? Klein indifferently swept his gaze toward Danitz.

Danitz instinctively shuddered and forced a smile.

"This fellow is no good. In the Fog Sea, he's often pursued by groups of pirates, but in the end, every one of those pirates becomes bounty money.

"You might not know this, but he started out in the Church of the God of Knowledge and Wisdom. Later, he was expelled after repeatedly failing his exams. I heard all this from Captain. They were once classmates."

He gave Anderson a contemptuous look that mocked his low intelligence. Flowery Bow Tie Jodeson and the others aboard the ship wore similar expressions.

Anderson's gaze slowly swept across their faces before he clicked his tongue.

"That isn't the main point. The main point is that I once pulled your captain's hair!"

The scene instantly fell silent, and Danitz couldn't stop his expression from twisting.

Chapter 692: Suspect

Seeing everyone aboard the Golden Dream turn their attention to Anderson, Klein stepped forward and said to Danitz, his body angled sideways, "Take me to the captain's cabin."

"All right, all right." Danitz withdrew his furious glare from Anderson Hood.

Rescuing Captain comes first. Rescuing Captain comes first... he repeated inwardly.

The Golden Dream first launched a dinghy, then lowered a gangway, allowing Klein to board with ease without using a charm.

Anderson followed beside him, completely ignoring the glares from the Golden Dream's crew. Smiling, he strolled forward and looked around as though he were in his own home.

His mental fortitude truly is remarkable... Even after offending a demigod, being forced to apologize in public, and accepting a mission, he could still laugh at himself and enjoy a meal... Klein sighed inwardly as he walked toward Third Mate Jodeson and the others.

"Hello, Mr. Sparrow. I'm this ship's first mate, Bru Walls." A 1.8-meter-tall man wearing a monocle bowed politely.

The pirate Gourmet, worth 6,200 pounds... The bounties on Vice Admiral Iceberg's crew are clearly lower than those on Admiral of Stars's crew. They live up to their reputation as treasure hunters who happen to be part-time pirates... Klein greeted him with Gehrman Sparrow's characteristic politeness. "Hello. I've heard of you."

"Haha, it's my honor. I'm merely a treasure hunter who dreamed of becoming an artisan, but somehow ended up a gourmet," Bru Walls said self-deprecatingly. He pointed to the man beside him. "Our second mate, Singer Orpheus."

A bounty of 5,500 pounds... The Beyonders aboard the Golden Dream all have very unusual nicknames. If I didn't know they were the subordinates of a pirate admiral, I would've believed them to be a traveling troupe that sings, enjoys good food around bonfires, and searches for legendary treasures. What a beautiful life... Klein looked at Orpheus and nodded.

The Singer had sharply chiseled features and brilliant blond hair. With a sorrowful smile, he said, "In truth, I merely praise the sun, but now my 'sun' has disappeared."

...

Klein nearly broke out in goosebumps.

"Tsk, as expected of someone from Intis. He speaks as though he's singing. What a pity. I grew up in Segar, in Lenburg, and failed to acquire that talent," Anderson said with a chuckle. It was unclear whether he was praising or belittling Orpheus. After all, half the blood in his veins came from Intis.

Born in Segar and later studied in Lenburg. Yes, likely at a Church school. He was Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina's classmate... Mr. Orpheus is definitely from the Sun pathway, but he probably isn't from the Church of the Eternal Blazing Sun. Judging by his bounty, he's likely a Sequence 6 Notary... I nearly forgot to tell Little Sun that I have his formula. I wonder what he'll offer in exchange this time... Klein turned toward Flowery Bow Tie Jodeson and Barrel Daniels before Gourmet Bru Walls could introduce them.

"We've met.

"Let's not waste any time."

"All right." Bru Walls sighed in relief, stroked his short beard, and led the way into the cabin.

If Gehrman Sparrow hadn't been so famous aboard the ship, he wouldn't have been nearly so polite.

At that moment, Anderson deliberately fell behind to walk alongside Danitz, Orpheus, and the others.

He looked from side to side, unfazed by the surrounding pirates' desire to tear him apart, and clicked his tongue with a laugh.

"I'm not the one you should be wary of."

"Right, we aren't wary of you. We just want to stuff you in there. See that? Into that cannon!" Danitz wasn't afraid of Anderson despite his title as the Strongest Hunter. After all, they were aboard the Golden Dream. There were plenty of pirates here, many of them Sequence 6 or 7 Beyonders.

Anderson curled his lips.

"I'm actually no threat at all. Think about it. Your captain definitely hates and loathes me. She wouldn't even want to speak to me. Isn't that ideal?"

...

Danitz's mouth fell open, but he said nothing. He suddenly felt that this piece of dogshit had a point.

The gazes of Orpheus, Jodeson, and the others subconsciously softened as well.

Anderson chuckled as he looked at them. In a rather ethereal tone, he said, "The person you should be wary of is Gehrman Sparrow."

"Why?" Danitz blurted out.

Although he's a madman who warrants caution, he isn't an enemy at the moment... Danitz silently added.

Anderson laughed.

"Let's suppose--suppose Gehrman successfully finds and rescues your captain. Wouldn't your captain develop a favorable impression of him as a result? Besides, he's quite good-looking. He has that cold, aloof aesthetic, and he's powerful. His strength is on the level of a pirate admiral, and his background is exceptionally mysterious. They're a perfect match..."

How... How is that possible... Danitz wanted to retort, but for a moment, he couldn't find the words. He grew increasingly convinced that something wasn't right.

The expressions of Orpheus and the others collapsed bit by bit. They looked at Gehrman Sparrow's back with newfound wariness.

Resolved! The provocation problem from earlier has been resolved... Smiling, Anderson entered the cabin.

Upon entering the captain's cabin, Klein first circled the room, which was nearly filled with bookshelves laden with all kinds of books.

The average captain's cabin is filled with racks of liquor... he muttered inwardly before walking straight to the desk by the window.

According to Danitz's account, Edwina had vanished in the midst of her research. Therefore, Klein's goal was to find traces of that research. Once he had gathered enough information, he would go above the gray fog and divine the matter.

At that moment, the desk was in disarray, with many objects scattered across it: white paper, a fountain pen, an ink bottle, a bronze dagger, and untidily stacked books.

At the center of the desk lay a book made of goatskin. Its dark brown cover bore the words "Groselle's Travels" in ancient Feysac.

Isn't this one of Vice Admiral Iceberg's collectibles? It has a mysterious origin and is suspected of being connected to dragons and the City of Miracles, Liveseyd... Edwina was studying this before she disappeared? Klein looked at the book and instinctively formed a conjecture.

Seeing Gehrman Sparrow examining the ancient book, Danitz forced a smile and said, "There's nothing wrong with it. We've already inspected it."

Is that so? I seriously doubt your thoroughness... Since someone had already leafed through it without encountering any obvious abnormalities, and since his spiritual intuition told him that Danitz was telling the truth, Klein reached for it and asked, "Have any of you read Groselle's Travels?"

Danitz shook his head. Bru Walls, Orpheus, Jodeson, and the others did the same.

Their expressions seemed to say that their daily studies were tiring enough. They had no desire to read more books during their breaks!

Running a fingertip over the yellowish-brown goatskin, Klein carefully and earnestly read through each page.

Soon, he reached the place where the pages were stuck together. From the corner of his eye, he caught sight of the text written there.

Eh... That's not right! His gaze sharpened, and he hurriedly flipped back two pages.

He clearly remembered that his previous reading had ended with the giant Groselle and his team preparing to challenge the frost dragon, the King of the North, in a head-on battle. There had been nothing after that, but now two new pages had appeared!

This also meant that the block of stuck pages had grown thinner, while the book had gained two readable pages!

The story stopped for a thousand years before suddenly continuing? Is this the result of Vice Admiral Iceberg's research? Did it also cause her disappearance? As Klein lampooned inwardly, he frowned and read the new content.

The two pages described a lost female pirate. She encountered the King of the North in a blizzard and was nearly killed. Only by using all her strength did she manage to escape and encounter the protagonist's team, who had come to challenge the frost dragon.

A female pirate has been added... Female pirate... Klein mulled over the description as an idea suddenly occurred to him.

Could she be Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina?

Did she enter the book and become a character in the story?

With this thought in mind, Klein quickly realized several things.

Arrodes once mentioned that the previous owners of Groselle's Travels had vanished...

Groselle's Travels contains a giant from the Dark Epoch, which is also the Second Epoch; an elf; an ascetic from the Third or Fourth Epoch; a noble of the Solomon Empire; and a Loen soldier from the Fifth Epoch. Their eras are completely jumbled.

If they were all previous owners of Groselle's Travels who disappeared, that would explain everything... They didn't belong to the same era. They were swallowed by the book and became characters in its story! Klein found his own theory absurd, yet highly plausible.

In the world of mysticism, this isn't impossible!

I have to confirm it... Also, what did Edwina and the previous owners do to be "swallowed" by the book... And what should I do to free them... Klein withdrew his gaze and pondered silently.

Soon, he looked up at Danitz and the others.

"Prepare candles and the other necessary items. I will pray to a secret existence for an answer."

And that secret existence is me... Klein jokingly added inwardly.

He really is both professional and insane... Gourmet Bru Walls and the others didn't dare say anything more. They hurriedly provided the materials and left the captain's cabin.

They didn't dare observe such a dangerous ritual unless Gehrman Sparrow explicitly asked them to.

Inside the captain's cabin, Klein locked the door, shut the windows, and quickly set up the ritual. He then brought Groselle's Travels above the gray fog.

After placing the ancient book at the end of the long bronze table, he sat down and conjured a pen and paper. He then wrote the divination statement: "Edwina is in the story of this book."

Chapter 693: Attempt

After setting down the fountain pen, Klein removed the spirit pendulum from his left wrist. He held the chain in one hand, allowing the pendant to hang close above the paper.

"Edwina is in the story of this book." Klein closed his eyes and silently repeated the divination statement.

After reciting it, he opened his eyes and saw the topaz pendant rotating clockwise.

This meant that Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina was indeed inside Groselle's Travels!

It really is a world inside a book... Furthermore, the circumstances inside are very unusual. Without new characters, the story can't continue... Klein nodded almost imperceptibly. Winding the silver chain and topaz pendant around his wrist once more, he picked up a dark red fountain pen and wrote a new divination statement: "The method of entering Groselle's Travels."

This time, he used dream divination. In the gray, hazy world, he saw indistinct figures.

The figures varied in size. Some were huge, while others were slender. The one thing they had in common was that each held a book with yellowish-brown goatskin pages.

What followed branched into two different scenes. Some of them silently vanished with Groselle's Travels in their possession. Others accidentally or deliberately dripped blood onto the cover while carrying Groselle's Travels, then suddenly vanished!

The scene shattered, and Klein opened his eyes to see the mottled table before him. Frowning, he interpreted the revelation.

To enter or activate Groselle's Travels, one must either remain in prolonged contact with it until a certain condition is met, or drip one's blood onto the cover?

Isn't that too simple? Perhaps... it really is that simple. In the story, the Loen soldier first appeared as an ordinary person who knew nothing of mysticism. With his companions' help, he gradually became a Disciplinary Paladin... Therefore, the activation method can't be too complicated. An ordinary person must be capable of carrying it out.

A small number of earlier researchers, including Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina, were Beyonders with sufficient knowledge of mysticism. They knew better than to rashly drip their blood on it; otherwise, they might die without even knowing why. It's like an ordinary person using the correct form of magic mirror divination. It's very easy to provoke a powerful or secret existence. That's why nothing happened to them...

Furthermore, Edwina usually keeps Groselle's Travels in the collection room and only handles it occasionally. Was it because she recently developed a new research idea and remained in prolonged contact with it that she fulfilled the condition needed to activate the book?

Yes, even Arrodes only knew that the book was strange, that many of its former owners had vanished, and that it was suspected of being connected to dragons and the City of Miracles, Liveseyd. This means Groselle's Travels interferes with its surroundings while being activated. Therefore, most previous collectors didn't know there was a problem and had no thought of studying it.

The owners who vanished probably aren't limited to those in the story. The others might have died for various reasons and failed to leave their names in the book.

Klein focused again and performed another divination, hoping to learn how to leave Groselle's Travels.

This time, in the gray and hazy dreamscape, he saw an even more violent blizzard. Atop an icy peak stood an enormous figure.

It was a translucent dragon nearly five meters tall while standing on all fours. It resembled the monstrous cousin of a lizard and had an ugly face with eerie blue eyes. It possessed a thick, powerful tail and a pair of enormous membranous wings. Merely spreading them seemed enough to blot out the sky.

Its scales resembled ice crystals and swirled with crystalline light. They were the most beautiful, dreamlike part of its entire body.

Suddenly, the dragon, which resembled an ice sculpture, raised its neck and body and let out a terrifying roar that pierced the blizzard.

Standing on its hind legs, it reached a height of ten meters.

The King of the North... That frost dragon... Klein left the dream and tapped the armrest of the high-backed chair.

His interpretation of the divination revelation was that the key to leaving Groselle's Travels lay with the King of the North!

Klein's initial conjecture was that only by helping the protagonist, Groselle, accomplish his goal--slaying the frost dragon--could they bring the story to a complete "end" and open a passage out of the book.

However, I can also try forcibly breaking through the barrier between the book's world and the real world... Drawing on his wealth of experience, Klein quickly devised an experiment.

He first picked up the face-down Black Emperor card and fused it with his Spirit Body.

Instantly, Klein's body was covered in black full-body armor, and a heavy crown appeared on his head. His aura became majestic and dignified, radiating an awe-inspiring presence.

Next, he stirred the powers of the mysterious space above the gray fog to their limit, causing them to surge forth like a tidal wave.

Seeing this, Klein didn't hesitate to summon the Sea God Scepter from the junk pile and infuse it with spirituality.

The blue gems at the tip of the bone scepter lit up one after another, radiating a blinding brilliance.

Countless silver bolts of lightning appeared and crackled throughout the grand palace, as though forming a sea of lightning.

Finally, Klein used the suppressive power and balance provided by the Black Emperor's lofty status to infuse the stirred powers into the Lightning Storm.

Rumble!

Thunder reverberated above the gray fog and into the distance as thick bolts of lightning struck down upon Groselle's Travels.

Blinding light engulfed the entire palace for a full twenty seconds.

When it finally subsided, Klein looked at his target and discovered that the mottled table had been reduced to wreckage. The dark brown leather-bound book, however, was completely unharmed. Only its edges had curled slightly.

It's far more impressive than I imagined... That's right. How could an item capable of creating an entire world inside a book be simple... Heh, I wouldn't have suffered a loss if I'd bought it for 8,000 pounds back then. It can be used as a shield and can definitely withstand attacks from Saints. The only problem is that it's too small. It can't cover much... As Klein's thoughts raced, the bronze table swiftly restored itself.

Since he had no way to forcibly tear open the barrier between the book's world and reality, he could only consider entering it through the normal procedure.

Obtain some blood, bring it up here, smear it over the cover, then enter as a Spirit Body with the Black Emperor card and Sea God Scepter? That way, I won't have to worry about encountering the King of the Five Seas, Nast, since he can't sense it. He won't be able to enter the book's world either. But the problem is that rescuing Vice Admiral Iceberg this way will allow her to identify Gehrman Sparrow as Hero Bandit Black Emperor.

Yes, there's an even more important problem. Entering as a Spirit Body means my physical body will remain in the outside world--in the Golden Dream's captain's cabin. I have no idea how time flows inside the book. It could easily take several days. In that case, without protection, my body might meet with an accident. I'm in unfamiliar territory as well. When the time comes, I might rescue Edwina only to discover that "I" am gone. That would be fun. Klein quickly rejected the idea of entering as a Spirit Body.

He didn't trust most of the people aboard the Golden Dream, and he remained wary of the Strongest Hunter, Anderson.

His attempt to divine whether entering the book to rescue Edwina would be dangerous ended in failure. Klein pondered for a moment before returning to the real world. Then, he unhurriedly brought Groselle's Travels back and removed all traces of the ritual.

Looking out the window at the approaching dusk, he walked to the captain's cabin door, unlocked it, and opened it.

Gourmet Bru Walls, Singer Orpheus, and the others were all waiting outside. None had left. Sailors even poked their heads out from the stairwell.

"Any clues?" Bru Walls blurted out, only to find his voice drowned out because everyone at the door was asking the same question.

Klein swept his gaze over them and nodded.

At once, he heard sighs of relief and saw a variety of joyous, excited expressions.

If I vanished one day, who would react like that... Klein reined in his thoughts and said to Danitz, "I need an assistant."

With that, he turned and walked toward the desk.

"All right!" Danitz hurriedly followed and skillfully locked the door.

"What do you need me to do?" he asked hastily. He looked as though he could already see his captain being rescued through his efforts.

Standing beside the desk, Klein solemnly said, "What happens next will be extremely dangerous."

"Extremely dangerous?" Danitz instinctively gritted his teeth.

"You might vanish or even die instantly." Klein informed him of the worst possible outcome.

Seeing how serious the madman Gehrman Sparrow was, Danitz immediately understood the gravity of the situation. His heart sank, and he subconsciously grew agitated.

"W-what does this have to do with rescuing Captain?"

"It's directly related," Klein answered succinctly.

Danitz's expression twisted, and he fell silent for two seconds.

"What happens if we do nothing?"

"Your captain might remain trapped there forever, or she might die in the next second," Klein answered truthfully.

Danitz's mouth fell open, then closed again in silence.

His gaze drifted for several seconds before refocusing on Gehrman Sparrow's face. Gritting his teeth, he said, "Let's begin."

"Dogshit!" he cursed himself softly.

Klein picked up the pen and paper on the desk, wrote a note, and folded it into a square before handing it to Danitz.

"Put it in your pocket. Read it after you enter."

"Enter?" Danitz asked, puzzled and bewildered.

As he spoke, he automatically accepted the slip of paper and stuffed it into his trouser pocket.

Klein didn't answer. He pointed at Groselle's Travels.

"Smear some of your blood on the book's cover."

This... Having formed a vague conjecture, Danitz picked up the bronze dagger beside him and nodded heavily.

"All right!"

Chapter 694: Burn Upon Chanting

Danitz held the bronze dagger and drew it across the back of his left hand, but his hesitation kept him from applying much force.

He looked up and forced a smile.

"Although I've been injured many times, I'm still afraid of pain."

"Get to the point," Klein replied coldly.

Danitz gave a hollow laugh.

"Haha, I'm just a little afraid of pain."

As soon as he said that, he exerted force with his right hand and sliced open a wound on the back of his left hand with the bronze dagger. Evidently, he had spoken to distract himself and reconcile himself to the pain.

Blood quickly seeped out. Danitz immediately put down the dagger, dabbed some blood with a finger of his right hand, and smeared it across the dark brown cover of Groselle's Travels.

Once he had finished, he held his breath and waited for a change.

Suddenly, he saw snowflakes the size of goose feathers. Strong winds howled in his ears, and a biting chill immediately burrowed madly into his body.

Although Danitz was mentally prepared, he was still alarmed. He instinctively looked around to determine where he was.

He realized that, at some point, he had left the Golden Dream's captain's cabin and arrived in a frozen land ravaged by a blizzard. Because of the extreme conditions, he couldn't see into the distance at all. He couldn't even tell whether he stood on a mountain or a flat plain.

I've really entered a strange world... Is Captain here too? Danitz raised a hand to shield his face, afraid that the snow would get into his eyes.

After calming himself, he recalled Gehrman Sparrow's instructions. He hurriedly took the square-folded piece of paper from his pocket and carefully unfolded it.

While doing so, he was especially afraid that some accident would tear the paper or allow the wind to carry it away. That would leave him with no hope at all. Fortunately, nothing of the sort happened, and he saw the words Gehrman Sparrow had written.

"Recite the following honorific name in Hermes, preferably ancient Hermes:

"The Fool that doesn't belong to this era;

"The mysterious ruler above the gray fog;

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck.

"Burn this upon chanting."

This... This is a secret existence on the level of a deity? Thanks to Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina's strict instruction, Danitz wasn't illiterate when it came to mysticism. In fact, he had quite a solid foundation.

Looking at the piece of paper in his hand, he subconsciously gasped, inhaling the freezing wind and icy snowflakes. The biting cold immediately made him cough, and his expression twisted.

He could now confirm one of his previous theories.

Gehrman Sparrow really did belong to a powerful and highly secretive organization! And that organization worshiped an existence known as The Fool, a godlike being!

Indeed, someone so powerful and insane couldn't have appeared out of nowhere. People like that don't simply sprout from the ground... Danitz tugged at his collar and tightened his clothes. Looking at the paper fluttering in the wind, he wore an unmistakably hesitant expression.

He knew very well how dangerous it was to recite the name of an unknown secret existence whose intentions were equally unknown. It could lead to a fate more terrifying than death!

Captain is trapped here too. Furthermore, there's no way out... Danitz clasped his hands and held them before his mouth.

Suddenly, he flung his arms outward and recited The Fool's honorific name in ancient Hermes.

Meanwhile, in the captain's cabin aboard the Golden Dream.

With his own eyes, Klein watched Danitz inexplicably turn illusory and disappear. There was no longer any doubt as to how one entered Groselle's Travels.

After waiting patiently for a moment, he heard a series of illusory pleas, clearly from a man.

Phew, it appears Groselle's Travels can't block the gray fog. The world inside the book remains connected to that mysterious space... This way, even if I enter, I won't be completely trapped or left without any trump cards... Klein made no effort to conceal his sigh of relief.

To confirm this, he took four steps counterclockwise while chanting the incantation. Upon arriving above the gray fog, he saw the light representing Danitz rippling beside The Fool's high-backed chair.

His body and soul entered together... The book's world is extremely stable. I can't shatter it merely by pulling at his Spirit Body... Indeed, if that were possible, the earlier divination would've produced a corresponding revelation... Using the Sea God Scepter is too dangerous. Bestowing it directly on Danitz would only hasten his death... Klein half-closed his eyes and extended his spirituality. After carefully sensing everything for a moment, he reached several conclusions.

Without delay, he immediately returned to the real world. Holding the bronze dagger, he wiped Danitz's blood from it with a piece of paper.

After folding the paper and placing it in his pocket, he began considering his next move.

From the looks of it, smearing blood on the cover causes one to enter directly. That way, I won't have to worry about anything happening to my body. Furthermore, I can resolve the problem very quickly.

But I still can't afford to be careless. I have to guard against other dangers. Hmm... If someone aboard this ship is problematic, they might sneak into the captain's cabin after I enter Groselle's Travels and sacrifice the book to the True Creator, the Primordial Demoness, or the Hidden Sage. Then I'd be in serious trouble. I would definitely suffer a fate worse than death.

Most of the Golden Dream's sailors appear infatuated with Vice Admiral Iceberg. The fact that Anderson's superficial provocation worked so well proves it. Therefore, if I tell them that Vice Admiral Iceberg's life is at stake and have them watch one another to ensure that no one enters, I can be at ease.

The problem is Anderson. He's the Strongest Hunter. He's stronger than any of the Beyonders aboard this ship. Furthermore, he excels at ambushes and infiltration. There's a small chance he could evade everyone's surveillance and secretly sneak into the captain's cabin... His background remains too dubious, and I have no way to truly trust him.

I need to find a way to make him enter Groselle's Travels with me...

As these thoughts ran through his mind, Klein flipped through the ancient book with its yellowish-brown goatskin pages. He discovered that no new content had appeared. The story bore no trace of Danitz's existence.

Does that mean a person must successfully survive and join the protagonist's team before being considered a true part of the story? Will more pages appear then? Klein formed a preliminary conjecture as he walked to the door again and opened it.

"Did it work?" Flowery Bow Tie Jodeson and the others asked.

Klein shook his head and calmly said, "What follows will be a lengthy ritual.

"No one is to enter and disrupt it. Otherwise, Edwina Edwards might disappear forever or even die."

After emphasizing the main point, he looked around and said directly, "I suspect that one of you might have a problem.

"Everyone is to watch everyone else."

Out of habit, Gourmet Bru Walls instinctively wanted to object, but then he suddenly noticed that the captain's cabin was empty. Danitz, who had just entered, was gone.

Recalling the captain's disappearance and confirming that Gehrman Sparrow had found its cause and was now attempting to rescue her, he nodded and said, "I'll take charge of this matter.

"And they will monitor me as well."

Klein didn't dwell on the topic. Instead, he turned toward Anderson Hood, who was leaning against a wall.

"Come in."

Anderson curled the right corner of his mouth and clicked his tongue.

"Anyone unaware of the situation might think I'm your subordinate. Your attitude is practically a model for Provokers."

Despite his complaints, he straightened and strode into the captain's cabin.

After closing and locking the door, Klein turned to face Anderson.

"Are you interested in taking part in a rare adventure?

"You might earn the achievement of hunting a dragon."

Based on his observations over the past few days, Klein believed that Anderson Hood was a curious, adventurous hunter. He enjoyed learning about new things and experiencing novel thrills.

Anderson stared at Gehrman Sparrow's face and seriously sized him up before smiling.

"I'm not interested."

He firmly shook his head.

Then, before Klein could speak again, he chuckled.

"I smell danger. A conceited, insane, and powerful adventurer is actually inviting me on an adventure. What does that imply? It means the matter is extremely troublesome and dangerous!"

I thought you'd be interested. This is different from how you usually act. You really do know when to restrain yourself and when to let loose... Hmm, I'll try threatening him. If that doesn't work, I'll leave this fellow on a deserted island and have the Golden Dream sail away. We can pick him up later. As a Hunter, surviving on a deserted island shouldn't be difficult for him... Klein quickly made up his mind. His gaze turned cold as he looked at Anderson and said, "I will not permit latent risks to remain around me."

Anderson was stunned for a second before swiftly smiling.

"Haha, I was only joking. I'm very interested in the title of 'Dragon Hunter.'"

...You changed your mind far too quickly... If you'd held out a little longer, you could've enjoyed the feeling of being abandoned... Klein nodded, walked back to the desk, and said to Anderson Hood, who had followed him, "Smear your blood on this book's cover. You don't need much."

"Smear it?" Anderson scrutinized Groselle's Travels curiously before surveying the room. "Edwina's disappearance is connected to it? And so is that failure of a Hunter's disappearance? Ah, right. Blazing Danitz. I nearly forgot his name. Fortunately, his bounty has risen a little."

The corner of Klein's mouth twitched as he answered frankly, "That's right."

"Interesting..." Anderson glanced at the desk, picked up the bronze dagger, and casually sliced a bleeding gash in his hand.

Immediately afterward, he carefully put down the dagger and smeared his blood on the dark brown cover of Groselle's Travels.

After observing for a few seconds, Anderson was about to pick up the bronze dagger and wipe away the remaining blood when his vision was abruptly filled by a blizzard.

Seeing Anderson disappear just like Danitz, Klein picked up another piece of paper, wiped the blood from the dagger, folded the paper, and placed it in his pocket.

He wasn't certain whether all three of them would appear in the same place after entering the book's world. Therefore, he had prepared the necessary materials for Dowsing Rod Seeking!

After doing all this, Klein took the dagger and dragged it across the back of his hand.

His facial muscles twitched as he looked diagonally downward. Only after several seconds did he exert force.

His body trembled slightly, and his head turned aside. The corners of his mouth couldn't help parting.

Once blood began flowing, Klein, still holding the dagger, grabbed his cane and quickly smeared the red liquid over the ancient book's cover.

After a brief delay, Klein found himself amid a white expanse of snow.

Chapter 695: Stark Contrast

Gales laden with ice fragments and goose-feather-sized snowflakes battered Klein's face. As he surveyed his surroundings, he couldn't help tensing. He bent forward slightly and trembled.

So... cold... He nearly cursed aloud as he confirmed that he stood in a land of ice and snow with extremely poor visibility.

He had once imagined that Backlund's damp winter cold was terrifying, but now he knew that extremely low temperatures combined with winds as sharp as blades were a deadly combination. Even though he had put on an extra sweater beforehand and wore a thick, long coat, he still couldn't withstand the chill.

He wasn't wearing the Sun Brooch because the heat it generated was psychological. For a short time, it could resist the effects of the intense cold and prevent his body from going numb, but in a perpetually frozen environment, using it would be tantamount to suicide. Psychological heat would cause his pores to open as though it were summer, stripping away his last defense against the low temperature and even inviting it in.

Therefore, Klein had sent the mystical item above the gray fog. He planned to use it only on special occasions.

He couldn't afford to delay in such harsh conditions. After a preliminary survey of his surroundings, he immediately burned the blood on the bronze dagger and stuffed it into his pocket. Then, Klein took out the Adventurer's Harmonica and blew it.

Amid the howling wind, even with Spirit Vision activated, he failed to see Miss Messenger Reinette Tinekerr appear.

Indeed, this place isn't connected to the spirit world. Or rather, it has a unique spirit world of its own... Hmm, it appears praying to the Sea God would be useless. Only charms that point to the mysterious space above the gray fog can tear through the barrier...

That raises a question. As a believer in the God of Knowledge and Wisdom, Edwina knows the true god's honorific name. Why didn't she seek "His" help? Or did she try without success?

Yes... Not every deity personally responds to "Their" believers. Most of the time, "They" seem to provide feedback according to certain laws. You probably couldn't find another "secret existence" like me who personally handles jobs... Klein laughed self-deprecatingly and formed a preliminary judgment.

He then put away the harmonica, took out the slip of paper bearing Danitz's blood, and wrapped it around the tip of his cane.

"Danitz's location."

Klein spoke softly as he began Dowsing Rod Seeking.

Following the result, he trudged through the deep snow and swiftly cut through the icy winds beneath the gloomy sky. From time to time, he performed another divination and adjusted his direction. After all, Danitz wouldn't remain where he had arrived. If he did, he would end up as an ice sculpture.

About ten minutes later, Klein spotted a scarlet flame.

Phew... He exhaled and took a few steps forward to identify his target.

It was indeed Danitz. The famous pirate was rather lightly dressed. He walked forward with his arms wrapped around himself and a lost expression on his face.

However, he didn't seem particularly cold. Scarlet Fire Ravens circled him, boiling the snow and blocking the wind, bringing springlike warmth.

At times like this, Klein especially envied a Pyromaniac. Although a Magician could likewise summon flames, it was an offensive ability that couldn't be sustained. It lasted only an instant. To use it for warmth, he would have to cast it continuously and would quickly exhaust himself. As for Flame Controlling, it required existing flames or combustible material, both of which were sorely lacking in this frozen world.

Seeing the Fire Ravens soaring through the air, Klein quickened his pace and approached.

Danitz jumped in fright when he sensed someone approaching. Upon seeing the newcomer, he immediately sighed in relief. Then, wearing an odd expression, he forced a smile.

"Haha, there aren't any stars here. Getting lost is inevitable."

Klein ignored his words and asked directly, "Did you burn it?"

"I did!" Danitz hurriedly nodded, his entire bearing exuding indescribable fear.

Klein observed him for several seconds. After confirming that he wasn't lying, he displayed Gehrman Sparrow's polite smile.

"Remember.

"Chanting 'His' honorific name makes you 'His' believer."

Danitz's expression twisted, and he forced a smile uglier than tears.

I don't want to change my faith! I have no intention of believing in some unknown existence of dubious origin! he roared inwardly, but didn't utter a word.

He suspected that any retort would result in the madman burying him in the snow!

Klein wore a smile tinged with madness. In a calm tone, he added, "Remember to keep it secret.

"If it is divulged, both you and your captain will die."

"What does this have to do with Captain?" Danitz blurted out.

Maintaining the same expression, Klein smiled at Danitz.

"Guess."

Danitz's mouth fell open. Knowing the reason, all he could do was chuckle hollowly.

"Do I look like someone who can't keep a secret?"

Klein nodded. As he took out the slip of paper bearing Anderson's blood, he chuckled and said to Danitz, "Believe in 'Him.' Serve 'Him.' Perhaps one day, you will become a Blessed like me.

"When that time comes, your name will resound across the Five Seas. You will be in no way inferior to a pirate admiral."

As he spoke, he considered adding a gesture befitting a believer of The Fool, only to realize that no such gesture existed. All he could do was console himself.

A secret organization needs to remain secret. Such superficial gestures are meaningless... Mr. Hanged Man was right...

In no way inferior to a pirate admiral... Danitz's eyes suddenly lit up.

Since I've already chanted the honorific name of this secret existence, there's no way to avoid the consequences from a mystical perspective. I might as well seize this opportunity... Countless thoughts instantly filled his mind, and he had even named his future child.

Heh heh, if it weren't for Gehrman Sparrow's persona, I would've directly said that you'll one day possess a reputation and strength matching Vice Admiral Iceberg. Of course, whether she'll like you is another matter entirely. From my observations, your chances are slim. The partner Edwina desires is someone who can study and improve alongside her, someone capable of discussing all kinds of knowledge... Klein muttered inwardly as he once again used Dowsing Rod Seeking.

"Anderson Hood's location."

"He came in too?" Danitz was first taken aback, then asked in surprise.

Klein finished reciting the statement, released his cane, and determined the direction in which it fell. He then nodded and said, "I couldn't rest easy with him outside."

So you're wary of Anderson Hood as well... Danitz couldn't conceal his smile as he echoed, "That's right! He's the kind of person who smiles to your face, then stabs you in the back the instant you turn around!

"His reputation in the Fog Sea is terrible. No pirate likes him!

"He even deliberately tried to slander you, making us wary and hostile toward you!"

If pirates liked him, his title as the Strongest Hunter would be meaningless... I heard Anderson's provocations... Klein didn't respond. He picked up his cane and proceeded through the blizzard.

Danitz followed closely, using Fire Ravens to scatter the snow and block the cold so that neither of them had to freeze.

Not bad. Quite sensible... At that moment, Klein once again appreciated the benefits of having a servant--someone to hold an umbrella on a rainy day and provide warmth in a blizzard!

Against the vast whiteness, the two resembled black dots trudging through the snow. They continued for nearly twenty minutes before reaching the spot indicated by the dowsing.

"He isn't here..." Danitz surveyed the area but found no trace of Anderson Hood.

He had no doubts about Gehrman Sparrow's Dowsing Rod Seeking, because he himself had just been found with it.

Klein frowned, activated Spirit Vision, and heightened his spiritual perception.

Suddenly, he sensed something. He prodded the ground ahead with his cane, causing the snow to collapse.

The collapse revealed a cave. Inside, dark rocks reflected the glow of a fire.

Klein crouched. He saw that the cave extended into a narrow passage. At its end was a cluster of glowing red rocks. Strange subterranean plants burned slowly while Anderson Hood sat beside them, leisurely roasting an animal resembling a rabbit. The aroma of sizzling fat and the warmth of the fire wafted out and reached Klein and Danitz.

"You fellows are finally here. Want some? There are actually strange rabbits capable of living in this frozen wilderness." Anderson bent down and looked toward the entrance, greeting them as though they were companions joining him on a camping trip.

Although he didn't say anything provocative, I really want to beat him up... Wearing a stoic expression, Klein entered the cave. He approached the fire and savored the long-awaited comfort.

Danitz followed. He looked at the fire roasting the rabbit, then at the Fire Ravens beside him, and silently dispelled them.

"H-how did you find this cave?" Danitz asked resentfully, unwilling to admit his inferiority, even as his body betrayed him by leaning closer to the fire.

Anderson turned the rabbit skewered on his pitch-black sword and glanced at Danitz.

"The first lesson of being a Hunter: observe your surroundings, familiarize yourself with them, and make use of them."

Danitz's expression immediately froze.

Anderson looked at Gehrman Sparrow and chuckled.

"It's a cave I blasted open. How is it? Not bad, right? I controlled my strength perfectly."

As he spoke, he sniffed the air and said, "How fragrant. It seems to be done. Would you like some? Although I didn't bring any seasoning, there's rock salt here. It'll just be a little astringent."

"Are you sure it's edible? If it's a Beyonder creature, a single bite might make you lose control," Danitz said with a click of his tongue.

Anderson glanced at him.

"The second lesson of being a Hunter: distinguish what can and cannot be eaten in the wilderness."

He carefully reached out, tore off a leg, stuffed it into his mouth, and ate with relish.

Klein was about to speak when he suddenly sensed a wanton aura approaching from afar. The oppressive presence of a high-level creature felt extremely real and made Danitz tremble uncontrollably.

The aura swept past without detecting the unusual cave below, then swiftly departed.

The King of the North... The title flashed through Klein's mind.

Chapter 696: Giant Guardian

As the tyrannical aura flew past high above, Anderson abruptly stopped chewing. Only after the entity had moved some distance away did he swallow the remaining rabbit meat and look up at Gehrman Sparrow.

"Was that the dragon you mentioned?"

Klein nodded slightly, confirming Anderson's guess.

The corners of Anderson's mouth slowly curled up, revealing an expression caught between laughter and tears.

"I thought you meant a young or adolescent dragon. The one that just flew past...

"I probably won't become a dragon hunter. Dragon dung, perhaps."

The tyrannical aura emitted by the King of the North really is rather terrifying. Compared to the pieced-together monster aboard the Future, the one that caused hair to grow uncontrollably, it's much stronger... Perhaps it's a Sequence 4, on the level of a demigod... Klein calmly made his assessment without revealing the slightest panic or fear.

He clearly remembered that Groselle's Travels had stated that the female pirate successfully escaped the King of the North's attack before encountering the team led by the protagonist, Groselle.

And Edwina Edwards clearly wasn't a demigod. She was a Sequence 5 of the Reader pathway. Furthermore, because she had been suddenly swallowed by the book, certain mystical items and Sealed Artifacts she didn't habitually carry had been left in the captain's cabin. She probably had only one or two useful tools on her.

Even under those circumstances, she had survived the King of the North's attack. Having only recently advanced and adjusted his inventory, Klein believed it wouldn't pose an insurmountable threat to him. Furthermore, he remained connected to the gray fog and could use the Sea God Scepter to respond!

This was also why Klein had dared enter directly after confirming during Danitz's prayer that he was in an ordinary condition.

Yes, the King of the North doesn't seem like a demigod from an ordinary pathway. According to Vice Admiral Iceberg, it's a Rampager that has accumulated frost-related Beyonder characteristics. It can rival a demigod in certain domains but has flaws in others... Edwina, Anderson, and I, together with the Beyonders in the protagonist's team, won't be helpless! If all else fails, I can still use the Sea God Scepter. I don't believe this book can block items above the gray fog. If it could, it would've demonstrated that long ago... Standing beside the fire, Klein looked down at Anderson and grinned.

"Are you afraid?"

Anderson was taken aback, then immediately beamed.

"Not at all. You seem very confident."

After saying that, he looked at Danitz, who was still trembling and struggling to compose himself, and clicked his tongue.

"Do you know what's most important for a man?"

Danitz had just taken a deep breath. Stunned by the question, he held his right index and middle fingers together and uncertainly pointed toward his crotch.

Anderson blinked, then burst into laughter.

"...Bastard, you really are a vulgar pirate!

"Haha, I was about to say something, but... Haha, I can't remember it!

"Oh, right. Courage. I meant that courage is the most important thing for a man. Look at you. The dragon hasn't even attacked, yet you're nearly cowering with your head in your hands and begging for mercy!"

Danitz's face instantly flushed red as he glared at Anderson.

You didn't act like this back in Toscarter... Klein couldn't help lampooning inwardly.

Danitz was about to explain that he had merely been affected by the high-level creature when he suddenly recalled what had just been said. His expression immediately returned to normal, and he casually replied, "I can't compare to dragon dung."

Anderson's smile froze. He coughed lightly and, as though nothing had happened, tore off another rabbit leg and handed it to Gehrman Sparrow.

"Want a bite?"

Klein remained silent for several seconds before slowly shaking his head.

"This is a strange world. Until we've confirmed that there are no problems, it's best not to eat anything here.

"Perhaps a piece of rabbit meat might make you remain here forever."

...

Anderson moved the roasted rabbit leg toward his nose before slowly lowering it. Then, his face collapsed. "Why didn't you say that earlier?"

Klein calmly replied, "I only just thought of it."

Anderson's expression twisted as he lowered his head and quickly bit into the roasted rabbit leg.

"A-aren't you afraid something terrible really will happen?" Anderson's actions alarmed Danitz.

Anderson laughed helplessly.

"I already ate one earlier. I've probably digested it by now... Since there's no turning back, I might as well enjoy it."

Klein and Danitz were momentarily at a loss for words.

After Anderson finished gnawing the rabbit leg, he deliberated and asked, "Are you really not eating?

"We have no idea how long we'll be here. If we truly starve, how are we supposed to fight the dragon?"

Klein didn't answer. He took out his gold pocket watch and flipped it open.

"It's ten past six in the evening outside.

"We'll eat a little in four to six hours if nothing happens to you."

...

Anderson's mouth fell open, and he was rendered speechless.

Ignoring him, Klein turned to Danitz and said, "We'll rest for fifteen minutes, then search for your captain."

As he spoke, he had already taken out the pearl earring belonging to Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina Edwards.

"All right." Danitz suddenly felt his blood surge with excitement, completely forgetting the cold outside.

About eight seconds later, however, he huddled close to the fire again.

At around seven in the evening outside, Klein pressed down his hat and held his cane. Together with Danitz and Anderson, he followed the revelation provided by his divination and reached a mountain.

After circling boulders encased in thick ice, they saw a dark cave. Standing at the entrance was a woman holding an ancient bow.

The woman had soft, lustrous black hair tied simply in a ponytail. Her delicate features made her look unlike anyone from the Northern Continent.

She wore an ancient-style brown hunter's jacket and trousers and cast an alert gaze in their direction.

Upon seeing her slightly pointed ears, Klein immediately matched her to the contents of Groselle's Travels and guessed her identity.

She was the female elf who had met the giant Groselle at the very beginning. Her name had been unknown.

Compared to Earth, people from the Northern Continent look more Caucasian, but this elf has a more Eastern charm... Klein quickly noted this distinctive trait.

"An elf! She looks exactly like the elves depicted in ancient Church paintings!" Anderson suddenly grew excited. "I have to negotiate with her and convince her to model for me so I can paint a few portraits!"

Beside him, Danitz scoffed and succinctly mocked, "Vulgar!"

Clearly, he hadn't forgotten Anderson's earlier ridicule.

"Is that the only kind of painting you know?" Anderson glanced at him, quickened his pace, and walked toward the female elf.

The moment he approached, the female elf raised her bow without hesitation. The arrow nocked upon it shimmered with silver lightning.

"Wait!" Anderson immediately raised his hands.

It's useless. Most elves belong to the Storm pathway. They're prone to being irascible and rash... Klein secretly activated his Spirit Body Threads vision, planning to establish initial control over the elf so she would listen calmly.

At that moment, Anderson's vision blurred, and he saw two thick, enormous, muscular grayish-blue legs. Embedded in the snow beside them was a huge and terrifying sword!

...

Anderson was horrified to discover that he only reached the figure's knees. His gaze instinctively followed the sword upward, bit by bit.

Only after tilting his head almost completely back did he finally see the giant, who stood nearly four meters tall!

The giant's skin was grayish blue. His abdomen and waist were covered by thick, furry animal hide, leaving the rest of his body bare. Even his legs lacked protection.

He rested his hands on a sword wider than a human doorway and looked at Anderson, Klein, and Danitz with his trademark single vertical eye. In a booming voice, he asked, "Who are you?

"Why have you come to Groselle's camp?"

Klein was about to answer when a familiar figure suddenly emerged from the dark cave. Danitz's eyes instantly filled with delight.

Dressed in an elaborate shirt and dark trousers, Edwina swept her gaze over the trio. Her usually cold expression revealed considerable astonishment, as though she hadn't expected to see Gehrman Sparrow and Anderson Hood here.

She quickly composed herself and looked up at the giant.

"Groselle, they are my companions."

Groselle widened his enormous mouth in a grin and asked happily, "Are you also here to help us deal with Ulyssan?"

Ulyssan? Klein was momentarily at a loss for an answer.

At that moment, he saw Edwina, standing in the giant's shadow, give him a look. She wanted him to answer affirmatively.

Ulyssan is the King of the North? Klein thought, then answered, "Yes."

"Haha, then we're friends!" Groselle looked down at the trio and laughed.

As he spoke, Anderson silently retreated to Gehrman Sparrow's side and whispered, "This is the first time I've met a living giant.

"There's no way to hit his vital points. He's far too tall!"

You can hit his legs... Klein lampooned inwardly before calmly replying, "A larger target is easier to hit."

"...That's true." Anderson agreed.

At that moment, Edwina walked over and introduced the two figures.

"This is the leader of the camp, Giant Guardian Groselle.

"This is the Elven Songster, Siatas."

Elven Songster? Ocean Songster? Klein suddenly felt there was hope of obtaining Mr. Hanged Man's potion formula.

Edwina turned halfway around and said to Groselle and Siatas, "They are my companions.

"The strongest adventurer, Gehrman Sparrow; the treasure hunter, Anderson Hood; and the renowned sailor, Danitz."

...I always thought Vice Admiral Iceberg was the serious type who never lied... Renowned sailor. Heh, in a certain sense, that's true... Klein removed his hat and bowed formally. Anderson followed suit in a casual and perfunctory manner.

Delighted that his captain had introduced him as a companion rather than a subordinate, Danitz reacted more slowly than the others and bowed in a fluster.

Groselle laughed.

"Come inside. We may have to fight that evil dragon, Ulyssan, at any moment!"

How warm and amiable... Yet both the Churches' canons and the City of Silver's myths describe giants as extremely violent creatures with a powerful urge to destroy... Yes, anything is possible in a book. It all depends on whether the author can make everything flow naturally... Klein nodded slightly and followed Groselle into the spacious cave.

Seeing this, Edwina approached the trio without displaying anything unusual. As though merely guiding them, she whispered, "The history they describe is somewhat strange.

"The same is true of their languages. Regardless of what language each of us speaks, everyone can understand it."

Chapter 697: Story's Progress

Everyone can understand one another regardless of the language they use? Klein set aside Edwina's first sentence and focused on the second, which didn't appear especially problematic.

Although this was a book world created by Groselle's Travels, and anything was possible, certain details still revealed underlying issues.

Klein wasn't particularly concerned with the fact that they could understand one another, but rather with the mechanism that made that understanding possible.

Does this world incorporate something like a law of language comprehension, or is there a consciousness high above everyone, translating in real time just as I do during the Tarot Gatherings? If it's the former, those who don't understand Jotun will hear an unfamiliar language but comprehend its meaning. If it's the latter, they will hear a language familiar to them... Because Klein knew many ancient and supernatural languages, he couldn't immediately determine the answer. He slowed and walked alongside Danitz, asking in a lowered voice, "Can you tell what language Groselle was speaking?"

Danitz was taken aback. After recalling it, he said, "It sounded vaguely familiar yet unfamiliar, but I could understand everything."

Of the supernatural languages, he knew ancient Hermes and, to a lesser extent, Elvish. He was only a beginner in Jotun.

Yes, it's something like a law of language comprehension... Comprehension on the level of the mind... This means that the fundamental rules of the entire book world can differ from those of the outside world. It's an inherent setting, but the changes don't appear capable of exceeding certain limits. This remains uncertain and requires verification. After all, I can't rule out the existence of someone like The Fool who translates communication directly through the mind... Edwina truly is perceptive and observant. The issue she discovered points directly to the essence of this book world... Amid his thoughts, Klein unhurriedly entered the vast, dark cave.

As for the history described by the protagonist's team, he wasn't surprised in the slightest. In fact, he looked forward to hearing the details.

Klein had long known that the various Churches and countries of the Northern Continent had consciously attempted to destroy or conceal information, hiding the true history of the Fourth, Third, and even Second Epochs. What passed for common knowledge differed from what those who had lived in those eras had actually experienced.

This was also one of the reasons Klein had taken the risk of entering the book world!

Inside the spacious, drafty cave, a bonfire cast its light and warmth upon three humanoid figures.

One was a middle-aged man in an extremely austere white robe. He had wrinkles but didn't appear old. With his back to the fire, he faced the stone wall, eyes closed and absorbed in prayer. His hair was short and brown, and his shoulders, arms, calves, and feet were exposed, revealing all kinds of old scars.

Beside him was a young man using a stone as a pillow. He wore heavy black full-body armor. Near his hand was a black sword that emitted a cold glimmer. His rather deep-set features were distinctly Loenese.

Opposite the two sat a strangely dressed man in his thirties whose appearance evoked a sense of unease. He wore a sharp, rigid black hat, and the buttons on his coat didn't match. Everything about him was messy, chaotic, highly asymmetrical, and discordant.

Furthermore, the toes of his leather boots curled sharply upward, making him resemble a circus clown.

The man was rather handsome, with flaxen hair, dark brown eyes, a high nose, and thin lips. Even while seated, he gave off an air of arrogance.

Edwina pointed at him and said, "Viscount Mobet Zoroast of the Solomon Empire, a gentleman capable of stealing the thoughts and dreams of others."

"You needn't mince words. Hello, I'm a Sequence 5 Dream Stealer of the Marauder pathway," Mobet said with a chuckle, displaying none of the arrogance his appearance suggested.

A member of the Zoroast family... The angel parasitizing Leonard is from this family. Perhaps they know each other? Heh, I now know the names of Sequence 4 and 5 of the Marauder pathway, yet I don't even know the corresponding Sequence 6 and 7... Numerous thoughts flashed through Klein's mind while he maintained his stoic expression.

At that moment, Anderson warmly greeted Mobet with a smile and asked as though they were old friends, "To be honest, this is the first time I've heard of Dream Stealer. I only know Marauder and Swindler. I'm unaware of the two Sequences between them."

"Are Beyonders of this pathway already so rare? Doesn't Edwina know? Sequence 7 is Cryptologist and Sequence 6 is Prometheus. Haha, allow me to make the introductions." Mobet warmly pointed toward the praying man whose back faced the others. "The devout ascetic Snowman. He believes in the Lord who created everything, the omnipotent and omniscient God. Don't mind him. He has completely sealed himself within his faith. In battle, however, he's an extremely dependable companion. Eh, Snowman, you should at least say something."

Receiving no response, Mobet stroked his chin with a rueful smile.

"This is the treatment I usually receive. You might find it difficult to imagine that I was once an arrogant, reserved, and refined nobleman, but time changes everything. Heh heh, that's what happens when your companion is a giant who only knows how to chuckle foolishly and shout slogans..."

As he said this, Groselle, who was seated on a rock, smiled simply and good-naturedly. He raised a hand and scratched the back of his head, his single vertical eye showing none of the ferocity and cruelty for which giants were reputed.

Mobet shook his head and pointed at the ascetic Snowman.

"As for him, he might not utter a single word for years, even a decade. Siatas is an extremely violent woman. Whenever her emotions are stirred, she beats me. Sigh, I'm now as afraid of her as I once was infatuated with her. Therefore, I can only keep talking to them. Otherwise, I'd definitely go mad!

"Thankfully, Frunziar arrived later. He's quite the conversationalist. Hey, Frunziar, wake up! We have new companions!"

The sleeping, black-armored knight slowly awoke and looked at Klein and the others.

Metal clattered as he suddenly stood. Staring at Klein, he asked, "Loenese?"

"Yes," Klein answered frankly. He discovered that the Loen soldier who had gone missing 165 years ago didn't appear old. He had black hair and sharp blue eyes that unconsciously compelled submission.

Frunziar appeared dazed for a moment before swiftly composing himself.

"Do you know Backlund's Edward family?"

"There are many Edwards in Backlund," Klein replied simply.

"The Edward family who lived at 18 Delahire Street in Northwest Borough," Frunziar pressed anxiously.

Klein shook his head.

"Northwest Borough no longer exists."

"Northwest Borough no longer exists..." Frunziar repeated softly, his voice growing quieter.
