"I have no idea how many years have passed outside, but it must have been a long time. Edwina told me the current year, but I don't remember the year when I entered... I spent most of my time asleep, and time here seems frozen."

Mobet Zoroast chuckled upon hearing this.

"That's because you were unlucky. Back when we traveled through cities and villages, everything was wonderful."

He looked at Klein, Anderson, and Danitz.

"Back then, we lived in places inhabited by humans and other intelligent races. We married again and again, watching our wives grow old, weaken, and die. Heh, before a new member joined us, we would forget our goal and lead ordinary but happy, peaceful lives for decades or centuries. The only drawback was that we couldn't have children of our own.

"Later, Frunziar arrived, and we entered this region blanketed in ice and snow. We hunted many monsters, but gradually began falling into slumber. We seldom awoke until we met Edwina."

This means time flows normally in the book world. The story's progress is maintained by some force that influences the protagonist's team. Before new members join and the book's pages turn, they'll stop at a certain point and do other things... This is similar to the mental translation of languages...

Conversely, does this mean the towns and villages in the book world also develop normally? Yes, I have to find the King of the North, Ulyssan, quickly. Otherwise, as time passes, we might also succumb to that influence and fall asleep or forget our main objective. We'll remain here for a long time until a new member is swallowed by the book and finds us... Klein fell silent for several seconds. Just as he was about to speak, Edwina said, "There's no need to worry about that.

"We will encounter the King of the North very soon."

"Why?" Anderson and Mobet asked in unison.

Edwina surveyed the area and said, "Before I entered, only a few readable pages remained before the block of stuck pages.

"Now that you have entered and found the camp, there definitely can't be many pages left. The story is about to end."

Mobet nodded almost imperceptibly, agreeing with Edwina's judgment. Anderson softly murmured words such as "stuck pages."

Edwina then introduced Klein, Anderson, and Danitz. Setting an example herself, she sat beside the bonfire.

Klein removed his hat and held it with his cane. He slowly sat and looked at Mobet Zoroast, who seemed inclined to talk.

"Have you heard of the Tudor Empire and the Trunsoest Empire?"

He didn't beat around the bush and asked directly. That suited Gehrman Sparrow's persona.

"No." Mobet shook his head. "Edwina already asked me. Heh, in my era, the Tudors and Trunsoests were like my Zoroast family. We were aristocratic families of the Solomon Empire and loyal subjects of the Black Emperor."

So the Trunsoest and Tudor families betrayed the Solomon Empire... Klein thought for a moment and asked, "What other aristocratic families did the Solomon Empire have?"

"Many." Mobet smiled and looked at Frunziar. "Augustus, Abraham, Zaratul, and so on. In my era, the Church of the Evernight Goddess's archenemies were the Church of the God of Combat and the Eggers family of the Southern Continent. The Churches of the Lord of Storms, the Eternal Blazing Sun, and the God of Knowledge and Wisdom were all at odds, each hoping to gain the Solomon Empire's support."

He paused for two seconds as his expression gradually turned solemn.

"In those days, deities walked the land, not the astral world."

Chapter 698: Fifth King of Angels

Deities walked the land, not the astral world... In the early Fourth Epoch, during the era of the Solomon Empire, there was no distinction between myth and reality. Deities walked the land directly, with no need to descend?

This is somewhat similar to the Second Epoch described in the City of Silver's records. The Giant King's Court and other places were separated from the real world by a door. One needed only to pass through it and could return the same way. Mortals and deities mingled amid chaos and darkness... Also, the astral world truly does correspond to deities... Upon hearing Viscount Mobet Zoroast's description, Klein immediately began connecting the clues.

He subconsciously glanced at Groselle. This giant was very likely someone who had personally experienced the history of the Second Epoch!

Groselle picked up a cup larger than a wooden bucket and gulped down melted snow before laughing.

"Mobet, what is there to be surprised about? Why are you so solemn?"

"I don't know why I became so solemn either." Mobet Zoroast gradually smiled. "Haha, it might have been perfectly ordinary to us, but to them it's terrifying and unbelievable. I have to adopt an appropriate expression to produce a satisfying effect. Do you still remember Frunziar's expression when we first told him those stories? He nearly knelt and begged the Lord of Storms for forgiveness."

...

Klein, Danitz, and the others were momentarily unsure how to respond.

Anderson leaned toward Gehrman Sparrow and said in a lowered voice, "I think he has a talent for being a Provoker."

He appeared to be suppressing his voice, but everyone present could hear him.

Mobet didn't mind. He chuckled and continued, "I know you aren't convinced and find it inconceivable that deities walked the land, just as Edwina did at first. Heh heh, I can give you two examples. The Chasm of Storms on Pasu Island and the Tenebrous Heaven in the Amantha mountain range were the divine kingdoms of the Lord of Storms and Evernight. They were divine kingdoms situated on land, separated from the real world by nothing more than an illusory door!"

Pasu Island? Isn't that where the Church of Storms' holy altar is? The Amantha mountain range... Amantha means serenity in Hermes. Does this refer to the Cathedral of Serenity? After the deities ceased walking the land, did "Their" kingdoms become the headquarters of the respective Churches? Klein instinctively believed that Mobet Zoroast wasn't lying and used his words to reach several conclusions.

Danitz was bewildered and horrified by what he heard. He subconsciously wanted to leave, but when he saw his captain listening attentively, Gehrman Sparrow lost in thought, and Anderson Hood watching with interest, he could only suppress the impulse and seek a more comfortable sitting position.

At that moment, Elven Songster Siatas, who had been guarding the perimeter, walked in and said contemptuously, "Do not mention that false god. The authority of storms belongs only to the king of the elves!"

Her voice was clear and beautiful, but her tone brimmed with anger and irascibility. It seemed as though she might raise her hands and shoot an arrow at Mobet Zoroast at any moment.

"All right, I will use the words 'false god.'" Mobet raised a hand to adjust his sharp, rigid black hat.

Siatas withdrew her gaze. Then, she said to the former Loen soldier Frunziar Edward, who wasn't a particularly devout believer in the Lord of Storms, "It's your turn!"

Frunziar raised his head slightly, his expression dazed.

Apparently, he hadn't noticed the preceding conversation and argument. He drew the iron-black sword beside him and walked toward the cave entrance.

Klein observed him for a moment, then took the opportunity to address the elf Siatas.

"Do you know the Queen of Calamity, Cohinem?"

He wasn't actually certain whether Cohinem was the Elf King Soniathrym's subsidiary god, the Queen of Calamity. His question was intended to have Elven Songster Siatas provide the answer.

Siatas's delicate, exquisite face immediately acquired the same distant expression as Frunziar's.

"I haven't heard 'Her' name in a very long time. 'She' is the queen of us elves.

"Neither Mobet nor Frunziar even knew of 'Her' existence...

"Where did you encounter 'Her'--no, where did you learn about 'Her' circumstances?"

As she spoke, Siatas's tone grew urgent.

At that moment, Danitz looked at Gehrman Sparrow in surprise, marveling at the madman's extensive knowledge. He could even share a topic of common interest with an elf from ancient times.

"I never expected you to be a scholar... I really couldn't tell. Couldn't tell at all..." Anderson sighed and shook his head.

Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina likewise looked at Klein, her limpid blue eyes filled with a powerful thirst for knowledge.

Klein answered frankly, "I once entered ruins belonging to the Queen of Calamity Cohinem and obtained several items."

"Ruins?" Siatas softly repeated the word, her tone suggesting that she had lost something unimportant yet impossible to relinquish.

"Judging by the situation inside, 'She' might not truly be dead." After seeing Siatas's eyes light up, Klein went straight to the point. "Do you have the Ocean Songster potion formula? Can I offer you something in exchange for it?"

He believed honesty and directness were the best approach when dealing with a Beyonder of the Storm pathway.

Siatas considered the question and said, "Exchange one of Her Highness's items for it."

"I only obtained a gold wine cup. It has already been crushed flat. Elaborate patterns are engraved on it, along with the Elvish words 'Calamity' and 'Cohinem.'" Klein didn't conceal the truth.

"I know that cup. It was Her Highness's favorite," Siatas said, unable to conceal her excitement. "Deal!"

"The cup is outside." Klein had no intention of going above the gray fog in front of everyone.

Siatas nodded.

"I understand.

"We will complete the transaction after leaving this book."

With that, she pressed her palms together.

"The Storm will definitely belong to the elves!"

Before anyone could speak, she asked curiously, "What else did you discover there?"

"Several murals depicting the Elf King battling an ancient sun god." Klein glanced at the ascetic Snowman, who believed in the Lord who created everything, the omnipotent and omniscient God.

Still facing the stone wall with his back to the fire, the middle-aged man finally spoke.

"No, 'He' isn't the Sun God.

"'He' is our Lord, the father of all things, the great source of everything.

"'He' wasn't battling the Elf King, but reclaiming the authority that belonged to 'Him.'"

The moment Snowman said this, Siatas stood and aimed her arrow at him.

Suddenly, the Elven Songster's braided black hair flared upward in defiance of the laws of nature. Every strand was distinct and swirled with silver lightning, emitting a strange, deep blue radiance.

Just as Siatas was about to release the arrow, an enormous grayish-blue palm appeared before her and blocked its tip, unafraid of being struck.

It was the giant Groselle's palm. One of his race's defining features was its exaggerated limbs. They were so long that they appeared somewhat distorted. Therefore, despite remaining seated, he could stop Siatas merely by extending an arm.

"All right, Snowman, enough. You know Siatas is an elf who acts before she speaks," Groselle told the ascetic before turning toward the Elven Songster. "Siatas, we are companions who can entrust our backs to one another. We have faced countless dangers together. You may argue with Snowman or even beat him, but do not try to harm him."

As expected of the book's protagonist. He's filled with positivity... But what's the difference between beating and harming him? Klein couldn't help muttering inwardly.

Siatas grunted and sat down, leaving the atmosphere heavy, silent, and awkward.

Giant Groselle surveyed the area with his single vertical eye before chuckling.

"Then I'll tell you about my past.

"Before entering this book, I lived in the Giant King's Court. I was one of the guardians of the Waning Forest, a place only our king could enter. Rumor had it that 'His' parents were buried within, and that they were also the progenitors of us giants."

The Giant King's Court consists of many parts, and the Waning Forest is one of them? The most ancient ancestors of the giants are buried there? Klein listened attentively, wishing only to ask more.

To him, this information was even more valuable than the history of the Fourth Epoch. The City of Silver's hope likely lay in the Giant King's Court.

However, before he could speak, Edwina beat him to it.

"Groselle, what did the book look like when you received it?"

Groselle raised a hand and rubbed his cheek.

"There was nothing in it. It was like an empty book waiting to be written."

I even thought Groselle might be an entirely fictional character created by the book... Klein deliberated for a few seconds and didn't ask directly about the details of the Giant King's Court. He turned to Mobet Zoroast and asked, "Do you know of Blasphemer Amon?"

"Blasphemer refers to the entire Amon family. They are the archenemies of us Zoroasts. It's said that they have an extremely powerful and terrifying ancestor whom even Ouroboros and Medici take extremely seriously. They even fear 'Him,' but no one knows 'His' actual name," Mobet explained in detail.

Ouroboros, Medici? Yes, in those days, the True Creator and Rose Redemption supported the Solomon Empire... Klein's heart stirred, and he immediately asked, "Then have you heard the name Sasrir?"

Mobet was taken aback and slowly shook his head.

"Never."

The Dark Angel Sasrir's name and title vanished after the Cataclysm? Were they buried? Through this, Klein confirmed one fact.

At that moment, Snowman, still facing the stone wall, said in a deep voice, "Sasrir is the Dark Angel, the leader of the Kings of Angels, the one closest to the Lord."

I was waiting for your answer... Klein looked at the ascetic and asked gravely, "Apart from 'Him,' Ouroboros, Medici, and Amon, what other Kings of Angels are there? You needn't say all of 'Their' names."

Klein was afraid of provoking an unnecessary reaction, like the one involving the "repentant one" in Afternoon Town.

Edwina, Anderson, and Danitz had long since become bewildered. The conversation between Gehrman Sparrow and these ancient figures concerned matters they had never heard of. They found it unbelievable that this insane adventurer knew so many secrets!

After several seconds of silence, Snowman said, "There is also the Angel of Imagination, Adam..."

The moment he uttered the name, the entire cave shook. The familiar, mad, and tyrannical aura rapidly descended!

The King of the North, Ulyssan, had arrived!

Chapter 699: Boss Fight

Frunziar Edward, the Loen soldier standing guard at the cave entrance, had just moved when he saw an enormous figure descend from the sky and land on a huge boulder encased in thick layers of ice. Its membranous wings didn't fold. Instead, they remained spread, nearly blotting out all the surrounding light.

The illusory armor-like scales resembling frozen ice and the violent, eerie blue dragon eyes were instantly reflected in Frunziar's eyes. He instinctively sensed danger, raised his iron-black sword, and leaped sideways, rolling far from where he had stood.

At almost the same instant, the King of the North, Ulyssan, opened its mouth and silently spewed a distorted, icy blue flame toward the cave, freezing everything in its path!

A moment later, the icy blue flames formed a tidal wave of illusory light that surged into the dark cave, encasing everything it passed in ice.

The words "Angel of Imagination, Adam" still flashed through Klein's mind. He couldn't help recalling Emperor Roselle's description of the Twilight Hermit Order. Its goal was to resurrect the original Creator, and among its members was either a High-Sequence Beyonder of the Spectator pathway or even the Uniqueness itself. The organization summoned its members through a true dream connecting the eastern and western ends of the continent. Furthermore, it possessed the characteristic of detecting when it was mentioned. Despite all this, Klein still instinctively reacted to the imminent danger.

He lunged sideways toward an uneven section of the cave, attempting to use the rocks ahead to block the attack.

However, the surging icy blue light was like a tidal wave that inundated every corner of the cave. It sealed everything in ice, leaving no safe place within.

As his surroundings transformed into an icy prison, an enormous grayish-blue figure appeared before Klein.

Groselle had silently stepped forward. He knelt on his left knee, leaned forward, and planted his broadsword in front of him.

Light resembling the break of dawn blossomed, forming illusory walls to Groselle's left and right that protected everyone behind him.

The icy blue "tidal wave" surged over and split around the upright broadsword before colliding with the dawn-like light on either side.

Everything before Klein and the others went dark; then a faint light returned.

They could see that the bonfire had been extinguished. The cave was exceptionally dark, with only the dim light outside attempting to penetrate the layers of ice.

At that moment, every inch of space before Groselle was frozen. The giant looked like an insect trapped in amber!

Immediately afterward, the sword embedded in the ground emitted a dawn-like radiance.

The rays converged and enveloped Groselle before transforming into a Hurricane of Light that swept outward.

Silently, an enormous hole was burned through the ice, extending all the way to the cave entrance. Groselle's grayish-blue figure had vanished from his original position.

Elven Songster Siatas, who hadn't had time to tie back her hair, held her bow and arrows. Surrounded by swirling gales, she charged out of the cave without delay. Dressed in his asymmetrical black coat, the Solomon Empire's Viscount Mobet Zoroast muttered, "Don't be in such a hurry," and "It's finally here," as he ran after Siatas.

The ascetic Snowman stood as well. He tapped his chest four times as though tracing the shape of a cross.

"May the Lord bless me!"

With that hoarse, dry declaration, he stepped barefoot onto the bitterly cold ice and ran out of the cave.

Klein didn't hesitate either. He didn't draw his revolver and kept both hands empty. Together with Anderson, who tightly gripped Death Brachydont, he ran into the tunnel through the ice.

Dressed in a shirt with elaborate patterns, Edwina Edwards looked at Danitz, who was trembling under the high-level creature's aura. In a gentle but emotionless tone, she said, "Stay here."

With that, the depths of her blue eyes darkened. Gales howled around her and propelled her out of the cave.

Stay here... Danitz was stunned. He subconsciously surveyed his surroundings and saw the frost-covered walls and the completely extinguished bonfire.

The cave was silent, and he was the only one left.

Danitz gradually stopped trembling, and his mouth fell open. However, he said nothing as he watched his captain disappear beyond the cave entrance.

Outside the cave, Frunziar Edward, who had just rolled away from the first attack, saw Ulyssan spread its wings and prepare to leap into the sky, closing the distance between itself and his companions. Once he had steadied himself and ensured his own safety, he thrust his left palm diagonally forward.

Immediately afterward, he declared in ancient Hermes, "Flying is prohibited here!"

Suddenly, the frost dragon's wings, which blotted out the sky, seemed to be burdened by invisible weights a hundred times heavier than its body. They began flapping with great difficulty.

The King of the North immediately let out a furious roar. The thunderous sound bored into Frunziar's ears and made him stagger.

With a whoosh, the wings finally managed to flap, stirring the surrounding snow and frost into the air.

Although flying had become difficult for Ulyssan, it ultimately succeeded.

At that moment, Disciplinary Paladin Frunziar's expression turned solemn. Once again, he spoke a sentence in ancient Hermes: "Violators will be punished!"

The moment he said this, his figure leaped into the air at a speed greater than Ulyssan's, as though augmented by an unknown power.

Clang!

Frunziar stretched out in midair and swung the iron-black sword in his hand down at the King of the North's neck, his posture filled with absolute certainty that the blow would land.

A distinct crack appeared in the crystalline armor and spread slightly. However, Ulyssan didn't bleed.

The frost dragon didn't even seem to feel pain. Its eerie blue eyes locked onto Frunziar with a cruel and tyrannical expression.

It raised a front claw while Frunziar remained suspended in midair with no means of dodging.

At that critical moment, a hurricane swept over and blew the black-armored Disciplinary Paladin aside, causing Ulyssan's claw to strike empty air. Despite missing, the blow produced an explosive boom.

Elven Songster Siatas had acted without hesitation the moment she rushed out of the cave, immediately saving Frunziar.

Right afterward, her hair flared in defiance of the laws of nature. Each strand was distinct and wreathed in bolts of lightning.

She aimed her arrow at the King of the North, a huge but slow-moving target in the sky, and firmly drew the bowstring.

The sky darkened as though clouds had gathered, lightning leaping among them.

Unable to fly smoothly due to the Disciplinary Paladin's influence, Ulyssan abruptly folded its wings and swooped toward Siatas like a high-speed train.

At that moment, a grayish-blue figure whose presence inspired a sense of security appeared directly in the King of the North's path!

Groselle once again knelt on one knee and planted the broadsword, which was impossible for a human to wield, before him.

Dawn-like light emerged, forming an invisible, impenetrable wall.

Boom!

The collision between the frost dragon and Groselle resembled a terrifying explosion. It shattered the surrounding layers of ice and blasted them outward.

Unable to hold his ground, Groselle was sent flying like a ball. He tumbled past Siatas and slammed loudly into the mountainside. A vast quantity of snow and icy stalactites crashed down from above, nearly causing an avalanche.

Ulyssan, however, wasn't driven back. It remained where it was.

Its swooping attack interrupted, its hind legs sank into the ground while its body leaned forward. It shook its neck, then aimed its open mouth at Siatas.

Mobet Zoroast, the Solomon Empire noble, had already arrived beside Siatas. Seeing this, he hurriedly extended his right hand and rapidly twisted his wrist.

Despite its mouth being completely open, Ulyssan forgot what it was doing. It stood dazed and failed to continue its attack. Suddenly, Mobet turned his head and spat to the side.

"Ptooey!"

The spittle was entirely ordinary and possessed no special qualities.

Seizing the opportunity, the ascetic Snowman, who had just emerged from the cave, raised his arms as though embracing God's grace.

Then, he said to the Elven Songster in ancient Hermes, "God says it is effective!"

With a crackle, the lightning around Siatas brightened dramatically, surged outward, and entwined itself around her arrow.

She released the bowstring, and the arrow shot forth.

Boom!

Dark clouds gathered in the sky as a thick bolt of lightning struck down and augmented the arrow.

The arrow turned completely silver, as though fired by the god of lightning. At an utterly unavoidable speed, it struck Ulyssan's forehead.

Layers of ice vanished, and the illusory armor cracked. The arrow pierced the King of the North's head, causing it to let out a deafening cry.

Light blue blood gushed out and swiftly froze as the frost dragon's ugly face shook violently, silver bolts of electricity dancing across it.

At that moment, Klein and Anderson emerged from the cave. Disciplinary Paladin Frunziar had tumbled to the ground and risen again. Groselle extended his hands from the snowdrift and rubbed his head, apparently not seriously injured.

With so many teammates, I don't need to serve as the primary attacker. I can attempt to control Ulyssan's Spirit Body Threads... From what I've observed, it doesn't seem to possess a demigod's defensive abilities. Of course, it's still much stronger than a Sequence 5... A five-meter range is rather dangerous... Klein looked at the frost dragon and swiftly formed a plan.

Chapter 700: Excellent Teamwork

The King of the North Ulyssan's agonized cries swiftly turned into a roar. Immediately afterward, a blizzard engulfed the area outside the cave, reducing visibility to less than five meters.

Howling gales swept up dense "goose feathers" that filled every inch of space. Meanwhile, an icy blue halo rapidly spread outward along the ground, leaving frozen ice everywhere it passed.

With both his sight and hearing hindered by the blizzard, Klein instantly pictured the corresponding scene in his mind. He hurriedly bent his knees and kicked off with his heels, leaping high enough for the icy blue halo to sweep beneath him.

As for Mobet Zoroast, who wasn't skilled in combat, the icy blue halo was already too close by the time he noticed it. He had no time to jump.

At that moment, he was hoisted into the air by his shoulders while a violent hurricane lifted his feet from below. The combined forces instantly sent him flying upward and spared him from being frozen.

Mobet turned his head and, unsurprised, saw the expressionless Siatas. Because of the "law" prohibiting flight, the Elven Songster could only use strong winds to glide forward.

Anderson, Edwina, Snowman, and Frunziar each reacted appropriately, jumping in time and evading the attack. Only Groselle, who had just pulled his broadsword from the snow, was unable to dodge in time and was struck in the legs by the icy blue halo.

Layers of ice instantly surged upward, and Groselle stood frozen in place like a corpse.

Whoosh!

The exaggerated blizzard obscured every Beyonder's vision, causing them to lose sight of the frost dragon. They could only passively defend against its attacks.

At that moment, Snowman, who hadn't yet landed, once again spread his arms and solemnly declared in ancient Hermes, "God says it is ineffective!"

The violent blizzard immediately weakened. Both the raging hurricanes and dense snowflakes diminished by half.

Siatas vaguely made out an enormous, ugly, lizard-like face. The broken arrow remained lodged in its forehead.

Ulyssan had seized the opportunity to close the distance between them!

Siatas didn't panic. The strong winds abruptly reversed direction, carrying her and Mobet backward. Meanwhile, her pale, cold lips parted as she sang in ancient, abstruse Elvish, "The rocks are destined to be shattered by the waves;

"The trees are destined to be felled by the winds;

"The mountains are destined to crumble beneath the lightning..."

Since each Elvish word was rich with meaning, the resulting sentences were exceptionally concise. Therefore, the lyrics didn't take Siatas much time. Furthermore, from the moment she uttered the first word and began the beautiful, forceful melody, the winds within the blizzard began to change!

The howling wind turned chaotic and scattered in every direction. Ulyssan's enormous figure once again appeared before Klein and the others.

By the time Siatas sang the third line, Disciplinary Paladin Frunziar had already thrust out his right palm and declared profoundly in ancient Hermes, "Imprison!"

Instantly, the frost dragon that was about to charge Siatas and Mobet froze in place. Layers of transparent walls surrounded it.

As Frunziar uttered the soft command, Edwina, who had just regained her footing, made her light blue eyes turn black. A viscous liquid flowed within them, seemingly the manifestation of all the evil buried in the depths of the heart.

She gently clenched her right hand, and Ulyssan rose with a roar, instantly tearing apart the effects of Imprison.

The frost dragon's eerie blue eyes were filled with confusion and pain. It seemed still immersed in emotions of sudden madness and brutality.

Although it was perpetually in such a state, having those emotions fully provoked was still uncomfortable.

Without a doubt, it was a dragon that found its emotions difficult to control!

Seizing the opportunity provided by Ulyssan's brief pause, Anderson Hood condensed a blazing white spear in his hand. He bent backward and hurled it.

Without waiting to see the result, the Strongest Hunter caused flames to appear beneath his feet, melting the ice into water.

Whoosh!

The spear accurately struck the frost dragon's half-open mouth. It swiftly melted through the thick armor of ice, and its remaining force penetrated the upper part of the dragon's forehead.

Ulyssan let out another agonized cry. Its hind legs stamped backward, sending its body lunging forward. Staying close to the ground, it charged Anderson at a terrifying speed.

The only thing in its eyes was the worm that had dealt it such significant damage!

Schwing!

The snow split apart, and a deep, wide chasm opened in the ground. It extended all the way to Anderson's position and continued beyond it.

Bang! Driven by its terrifying momentum, the frost dragon slammed into a boulder covered in thick ice, shattering its outer shell and cracking the stone within!

Had Anderson collided with it, the Strongest Hunter would undoubtedly have been reduced to meat paste. Even being grazed would likely have killed him.

However, at a point within the chasm where Anderson had originally stood, there was a dark hole leading straight down, just wide enough for a person to fit inside.

Pa!

A hand gripped the edge of the hole. Exerting force, the Strongest Hunter, his hair disheveled, leaped out.

After throwing the blazing spear, he hadn't dodged in a panic. Instead, he had used his Beyonder powers to melt the snow beneath his feet and burn through the soil below, silently creating a shallow pit. Then, he had crouched and dropped into it, perfectly evading the King of the North's charge.

At that moment, a pure beam of light descended and struck Groselle, rapidly melting the ice that encased him.

Snowman had used an offensive attack to remove the restraints placed upon the Giant Guardian!

With an explosive burst of dawn-like light, Groselle raised his broadsword and charged the frost dragon in great strides before furiously cleaving downward.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

Groselle was only a meter shorter than Ulyssan in its non-humanoid form, and his strength was equally absurd. He repeatedly clashed with Ulyssan's front claws. Though he occasionally wavered and retreated, he quickly recovered, stepped forward, and engaged his opponent again.

With the Giant Guardian occupying the frost dragon's attention, the others responded with relative composure.

Snowman kept his arms spread as a sun-like halo appeared around him. It swiftly expanded, warming all his companions and filling them with immense courage. Furthermore, he carefully kept it from affecting the King of the North.

Not far away, Siatas's hair flared as she drew her bowstring and fired arrows seemingly formed from blades of wind or bolts of lightning. Because her target was so enormous, every arrow struck. Moreover, they all hit the same spot--the frost dragon's "shoulder."

Mobet Zoroast coordinated with Groselle, periodically stealing the thoughts that the King of the North was about to act upon and making it pause briefly. At other times, he attempted to steal its powers, but his several attempts all failed.

Holding his pitch-black sword, Anderson carefully circled to the frost dragon's flank and attempted to strike a particular spot. Disciplinary Paladin Frunziar brandished his iron-black sword, using Prohibition and Imprison to support Groselle's brawl with the frost dragon. Without these restraints, even though the giant was a Guardian, he would long since have been sent flying and subjected to a fatal stomp or blast of icy breath.

Klein looked at Edwina and raised a hand to point at himself.

"Invisibility!"

He didn't know whether she could imitate this power. If she couldn't, he would have to consider another method.

Edwina didn't ask why. Her limpid, light blue eyes immediately reflected Gehrman Sparrow's body as it lost its color and turned transparent.

Then, Klein's figure faded and vanished.

After confirming his condition, Klein swiftly ran toward the intense clash between giant and dragon. With a roll, he arrived beside the King of the North's left foot.

Then, while concentrating on dodging Ulyssan's feet as it fought, he extended his spirituality and began controlling the illusory black threads revealed by the Spirit Body Threads vision he had activated long ago.

Immediately afterward, he rolled and leaped about, searching for room beneath and around the frost dragon.

Whoosh! Whoosh! Whoosh!

Silver lightning arrows and pure azure wind blades struck a confined area, shattering the ice armor and tearing through the resilient hide. Soon, the frost dragon's right shoulder became a swath of light blue, and the movements of the corresponding claw slowed almost imperceptibly.

Disciplinary Paladin Frunziar keenly noticed this. As he dodged the icy blue breath, he pointed in that direction and declared in ancient Hermes, "Death!"

With a bursting sound, the blue blood flowing from Ulyssan's right shoulder instantly dried, and the gash widened until its translucent bones became visible.

At that moment, Edwina took out a palm-sized brass mirror and caught part of Ulyssan's body in its reflection.

She reached out with her right hand and grabbed the mirror's surface, pinching the reflection within. Then, she pulled forcefully outward!

Ulyssan's wound instantly worsened and continued spreading, as though its front claw were about to be torn off.

It cried out in agony and suddenly reared up on its hind legs.

Light blue radiance, so pale it was nearly white, surged from the dragon. Within a hundred-meter radius, the temperature plummeted, and a terrifying force followed.

All the Beyonders were suddenly sealed in ice. Their bodies became extremely cold and stiff, and they couldn't help trembling.

Siatas, Mobet, Edwina, Klein, Groselle, Anderson, and Frunziar all displayed similar symptoms. The only person who could barely retain his mobility was the ascetic Snowman, thanks to the training he had endured from being frozen countless times.

He kept his arms spread, half-closed his eyes, and solemnly declared, "God loves everyone!"

Sunlight tore through the wind and snow, and its warmth began melting away the frozen sensation.

Bang!

Ulyssan struck Groselle with a claw and sent him flying. A deep depression was left in the Giant Guardian's chest, severely injuring him.

Temporarily ignoring the others, it fixed its attention on Snowman and charged at him!

No one is protecting Snowman... It's about to leave the five-meter radius... Klein watched the scene, his body somewhat numb. He felt the urge to run into the cave and pray to himself, then respond with the Sea God Scepter from above the gray fog.

At that moment, before the frost dragon could arrive, a scarlet fireball appeared beside the ascetic and exploded without reservation, blasting him away.

It had come from the Strongest Hunter, Anderson.

At the same time, Edwina conjured a fireball in her hand and hurled it at Ulyssan's back, but it missed.

She was creating a path of flames through which Klein could use Flame Jump!

She had learned from Danitz that Gehrman Sparrow possessed this ability!
